# assay — decisions

One line per decision, with the reason. Reasoning in depth lives in
`docs/DESIGN-GUIDE.md`; this file is the record. Append, never rewrite; when a
decision is reversed, add a new line that supersedes the old one by id.

Session of 2026-08-06, base revision `d87f028b`.

## Decided

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-001 | Name is **`assay`**; distribution stays estate-local wheels | PyPI `assay` is a third-party empty reservation (v0.0, zero files, homepage misprinted as python-skyfield). The estate publishes nothing to PyPI and already ships `topos` against a *real* 5-release collision. If a public index name is ever needed, publish under a different distribution name and keep `assay` as the import name. |
| A-002 | Build as a **new standalone project**; nyxloom untouched | Removes the sequencing conflict that deferred P90 behind nyxloom's core redesign. nyxloom's own migration is P90c, later. |
| A-003 | **v1 includes mutation** | Owner's call over the recommendation to defer it. |
| A-004 | **Mutation is sequenced last** in the carved series | Mitigates A-003's risk structurally: the adapter protocol is settled by coverage and canary first, so the most language-idiosyncratic component must fit the protocol rather than reshape it. |
| A-005 | **Zero runtime dependencies** — stdlib only | Makes the standalone claim near-unfalsifiable and the scratch-venv O1 test trivially offline. assay consumes coverage.py's *output*; it never imports coverage.py or pytest. |
| A-006 | Coverage parsing lives in a **parser registry keyed by format**, not in the adapter | Format and language are independent axes: lcov is emitted by five languages, TypeScript emits three formats. Binding them copies the lcov parser into every adapter — the four-copies divergence one layer down. |
| A-007 | Lane **declares** the coverage format; assay **sniffs** the artifact and **refuses on mismatch** | §4.2a prefers DERIVE then READ. The format is a fact of the lane's own argv, so it is read; sniffing is the cross-check that catches a lane whose argv changed and was not updated. |
| A-008 | `FileCoverage.excluded` is `frozenset | None`; `None` means the **format cannot express exclusions** | Otherwise a Go lane reports "0 changed lines excluded — verified" when the format simply cannot say. NO-MEASUREMENT discipline one level down. |
| A-009 | `LanguageAdapter` protocol defined in v1; **Python + Go adapters**, Go proven by committed fixtures | Keeps `ast` and `.py` globs out of the core (P90 O3's negative), so a third adapter is additive. Go is the honest second because its profile is block-based, not line-based. |
| A-010 | `inject_import_break` / `inject_uncovered_line` are **pure** — `(text) → (text, description)` | nyxloom's versions write the file themselves; pure adapters are testable with no filesystem. A straight improvement on the extracted code. |
| A-011 | `generate_mutants` may return `UNSUPPORTED` → **`INCONCLUSIVE_NO_MUTANTS`, never green** | TESTING-METHODOLOGY already names this outcome for zero selected mutants; an adapter that cannot mutate inherits it rather than inventing one. |
| A-012 | **Delete** nyxloom's `_derive_test_command` (`src/<mod>.py → tests/test_<mod>.py`) | §4.2a anti-pattern #2 — the consumer inventing on absence. It is a naming convention, not a language fact. The lane declares the argv. The only place v1 deliberately does not take the union. |
| A-013 | Adapters **may shell out**, but must declare it in `external_tools`; a missing tool is NO_MEASUREMENT for that check | Go's `has_executable_code` genuinely needs a parser. Declaring makes prerequisites checkable up front instead of failing mid-run. |
| A-014 | Lane file is **`assay.toml`** at project root | ciu reads nothing today — `ciu test` is explicitly a sketch that "does not exist today". Naming for an absent consumer repeats the "implies capability it does not have" failure. Neutrality is preserved in the schema (`[…where]`), not the filename. |
| A-015 | Lane file structure **is D7's three-question split**: top level = WHAT, `[…judge]` = HOW, `[…where]` = WHERE | Makes tool independence visible in the file's shape. A project adopting only one tool writes only its table. |
| A-016 | `source_roots` is **required, no default**; a nonexistent root is `BAD_LANE_CONFIG` at load | All four copies ship §4.2a anti-pattern #1, and a typo'd root matches nothing → 0/0 PASS forever, a laundering gate none of them guards. |
| A-017 | **Declared rigor is enforced**: R1 requires coverage config, R2 requires mutation config, R3 requires canary config, or the lane fails to load | A lane claiming a rigor level it cannot exercise is the lane-table-implies-capability failure in data form. |
| A-018 | `allow_excluded` is a **required** per-lane field | Resolves a genuine policy contradiction in the union: topos's failure text tells users to *add* `# pragma: no cover`; nyxloom and dstdns fail the gate for it. Each project must state its position at adoption. |
| A-019 | Lane `env` is **declared-only**, with an explicit `env_passthrough` allowlist | "Augment the ambient environment" is incoherent at S3, where the container already does not inherit it — the same lane file would mean different things at different scopes. The base is a fact of WHERE. |
| A-020 | `budget` exceeded is its **own outcome** (`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`, exit 4), not NO_MEASUREMENT | Different diagnosis, different remedy, different retry policy: NO_MEASUREMENT is deterministic and must never be auto-retried; a budget overrun is load-dependent and may be. |
| A-021 | **Six exit codes**: 0 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2 ERROR / 3 NO_MEASUREMENT / 4 BUDGET_EXCEEDED / 5 INCONCLUSIVE | Each already exists as a concept in the estate; none invented. 2–5 are all not-a-pass and all block a merge. |
| A-022 | A **required `reason_code`** enum names the cause on every non-PASS outcome | Gives cause-level specificity without proliferating exit codes. Answers "DIRTY_TREE vs BASE_IS_HEAD vs EMPTY_COVERAGE" without renaming the outcome class. |
| A-023 | Rollup precedence `ERROR > NO_MEASUREMENT > BUDGET_EXCEEDED > FAIL > INCONCLUSIVE`; PASS only if every declared claim passed | ERROR and NO_MEASUREMENT invalidate everything computed beneath them; a truncated run's FAIL may be partial. |
| A-024 | Artifact carries **one `claims[]` entry per declared rigor level**, not a flat verdict | "Record the evidence actually obtained rather than reducing quality to one coverage percentage." Also gives Tier 2/3 evidence somewhere to live. |
| A-025 | **No percentage is emitted when no measurement happened** — the coverage block is omitted, not zeroed | Emitting `pct: 100.0` beside NO_MEASUREMENT rebuilds the original ambiguity inside the artifact for any consumer that reads `pct` and ignores `outcome`. |
| A-026 | A **legitimate 0/0 pass emits the numbers plus a `considered` count** | srdm's fix: an empty denominator must explain itself in prose rather than being textually identical to a broken measurement. |
| A-027 | Verdict artifact is **emitted on every outcome, including ERROR** | Precedent: the remote-mutation-audit worker emits `summary.json` "even when a baseline, mutant, or teardown fails". Without it, "assay errored" and "assay never ran" are indistinguishable. |
| A-028 | Artifact is written **only when an explicit path is given** (`--verdict-json PATH`, or `-` for stdout) | No invented paths, no uninvited writes into a repo. The exit code always works standalone, so a consumer without the flag still gates correctly. |
| A-029 | Consumers read a **JSON Schema shipped as data**; nothing links against assay | D7's "judged-result contract, so `ciu test` can surface a verdict without linking against it". nyxloom's `GateResult` is a strict subset — six keys. |
| A-030 | **assay and ciu stay separate tools**; `ciu test` (if ever built) wraps assay via the verdict artifact | The boundary is topological, not preferential: assay runs **inside** the test container next to the coverage artifact; ciu runs **outside** issuing `docker run`. Merging ships an orchestrator into every test image or splits back in half at runtime. Ergonomics are identical either way. |
| A-031 | assay ships its **own CLI** (`assay run/verify/mutate/lanes`) | Forced by P90 O1 (a CLI invocation is the oracle), by all four existing copies, and by self-hosting, which cannot route through ciu without circular adoption. |
| A-032 | Evidence is carried in **three tiers**: COMPUTED / ADJUDICATED / ATTESTED | Keeps §0 intact while letting the whole evidence catalogue be expressed. Tier 2's judgement is the tool's; Tier 3 is never verified by assay. |
| A-033 | **Tier 3 (attested) slot, commit-binding and staleness check ship in v1** | The slot must exist from day one or retrofitting means a schema bump across every adopted consumer. Staleness is the cheap half and the half that gives an attestation teeth: assay cannot judge a review, but it can prove one is stale. |
| A-034 | **Tier 2 (adjudicated) slot ships in v1 with zero integrations** | Same schema-shape reasoning; costs almost nothing to reserve. A missing or crashed scanner must be ERROR or NO_MEASUREMENT, never PASS. |
| A-035 | Two guards **beyond the four-way union** ship in v1: `EMPTY_COVERAGE` and load-time `source_roots` validation | A zero-file coverage artifact currently renders 0% → FAIL, a *wrong diagnosis* ("add tests" meaning "your `--cov` path is broken"). A typo'd source root passes forever. Both were found by applying the §4.2a test to the existing copies. |
| A-036 | Runner flags **may be appended by the caller** and are recorded as `argv_declared` / `argv_appended` / `argv_effective`; `argv_modified: true` is set, and the lane must carry `allow_argv_append` | Owner's call, with transparency as the condition. assay still **never derives** flags — deriving them is impact-based selection, which is the caller's domain. |
| A-037 | Adoption order **topos → ciu → dstdns**; netcup-api-filter, cmru, mdt later | topos is the only migration with a baseline: old and new gate run on the same commits and must agree, so union fidelity becomes mechanical rather than a review question. |
| A-038 | **Adoption declares and verifies; it does not remediate** | Changed-line coverage is a ratchet, not an audit — netcup can adopt `fail_under = 100` at 30.17% global coverage. Conflating adoption with test-writing turns each consumer into an open-ended project instead of a bounded swap. |
| A-039 | Canary results at adoption are **diagnostic output**, and feed separate per-project remediation packages | A LAUNDERS finding measures a project's floor; fixing it is a different job with a different owner. |
| A-040 | **One gated lane, `tester-unified`**; the standalone claim is proven by a scratch-venv test *inside* it | "Greens from the interactive cockpit are explicitly not a ship signal." A bare-pytest lane manufactures exactly that pathway. Bare `pytest` stays a documented developer convenience, explicitly not evidence. |
| A-041 | The **independent oracle is pytest over expected-verdict fixtures**, not assay | Breaks the self-hosting circularity: a universal-PASS bug fails every fixture assertion at once. `assay verify` is a second, explicitly non-independent layer. |
| A-042 | Fixtures are **hello-world Python and Go projects** with committed pre-generated coverage artifacts; git-state fixtures materialised in `tmp_path` | Parsing profiles and injecting into Go source are pure text operations, so the suite needs no Go toolchain — mandatory, since this devcontainer has none. A regeneration script is documented for a host that has Go. |
| A-043 | **Go stays out of the devcontainer.** Promote srdm's `gate/Dockerfile` `unit` target to `vbpub/tester-unified-go` | The cockpit having no Go toolchain means it "cannot even pretend" to produce a ship signal — a property adding Go would destroy. The `unit` target is generic (pinned `golang:1.25`, run-uid identity, prewarmed std cache, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, caches outside the bind mount); the `e2e` target (systemd-in-Docker) is srdm-specific and layers on top. `tester-unified` untouched, as its own reasoning demands. |
| A-044 | A **`tools/go` wrapper** against `tester-unified-go` gives local ergonomics at the image's pinned version | Recovers the entire agent-productivity argument for a devcontainer toolchain, without the cockpit hazard, and makes the version explicit at the call site rather than ambient. |
| A-045 | A **matrix lane is two lanes**, not a `matrix` field | netcup's `[gates.unit]` loops one argv over two images. They have different WHERE and can render different verdicts; a loop that hides which image failed is the opacity being removed. |
| A-046 | **A lane file declares what exists, not a target architecture** | dstdns's five-lane table is aspirational; only two lanes are real. Writing the other three would make the file claim capability the project does not have. |
| A-047 | Docs split follows ciu: `nyxloom-trove/decisions.md` (record) + `docs/DESIGN-GUIDE.md` (reasoning) | The split already exists in ciu (`docs/DESIGN-NOTES.md` + trove decisions) and is the estate pattern. |

Low real-world severity (a caller typo, not an assay-correctness issue); not fixed now, since A-050 says stop and ask rather than invent a code alone. |
| A-O15 | **RESOLVED by P26 (A-165 then A-211). Verified 2026-08-11: `src/assay/attestation.py` contains no `_changed_paths`, no `splitlines`, and no `diff --name-only` call at all — freshness is now one bounded `diff --quiet --exit-code -- <path>` per reviewed identity plus a raw `ls-tree -z` blob/tree identity, so there is no display-name transport left to mis-split.** Which package owns `attestation._changed_paths`' path transport? It reads `git diff --name-only` and splits the result with `str.splitlines()`, undoing neither of git's two spellings — so it has exactly the pair of defects A-134 just closed everywhere else, and A-134's encoding half does not reach it. Candidates: fold into P20 (attestation hardening, the natural home, but its handoff does not currently name this), or a small standalone repair. | Found and REPRODUCED during P15's controller review against a real repository, not inferred: a file named `weird\nname.py` comes back as the literal quoted spelling `'"weird\\nname.py"'` (git C-quotes it, nothing unquotes it), and a file named `sep<U+2028>name.py` comes back as TWO phantom entries, `'sep'` and `'name.py'`, with the real identity gone — `core.quotePath=false` (correctly) leaves its non-ASCII bytes raw, and `splitlines()` treats U+2028 as a line break. Deliberately NOT fixed in P15: `attestation.py` is in that package's `scope.forbid`, and improvising past a forbid is the failure mode the BLOCKED rule exists to prevent. Pre-existing since P10, unchanged by P15, and unreachable without an adversarially-named file — hence open rather than blocking. `git diff --name-only -z` plus `git._decode_or_reject` is the obvious shape of the fix. |
| A-O16 | **RESOLVED by P21 (A-183). Verified 2026-08-11: `src/assay/verdict.py`'s `EXCLUSION_CAPABILITIES = ("reported", "unavailable")` cites this row by id — the artifact now distinguishes a format that cannot express exclusions from one that truthfully reported none.** Should the verdict artifact distinguish "this coverage format cannot report exclusions" from "it reported none"? `FileCoverage.excluded is None` (unknown) and `frozenset()` (known-empty) stay distinct upstream exactly as A-135 requires, but `evaluate_coverage` intersects the changed lines with `frozenset()` in both cases, so `Coverage.excluded_lines` has no spelling for unknown. Candidates: a third state on the mapping, a sibling `exclusion_data_available` flag, or accept the loss and say so in the design guide. | Raised by P16's own work item 3 ("format inability... must not be rewritten to empty") during the controller review. Judged NOT a correctness defect and therefore not repaired inside P16: `has_disallowed_excluded` is false in both cases too, so status and payload agree and R1 re-derivation is unaffected — the loss is diagnostic only (a reader cannot tell a clean lane from a blind format). Closing it needs a new artifact field and reaches the format registry and every adapter, all outside P16's `scope.touch`. Decide before P25 (real lcov/Istanbul parsing), which is the first package with a format whose exclusion support genuinely differs from coverage.py's. |
| A-O19 | **RESOLVED 2026-08-11 by A-217 (option 2 — build a real source-side statement-position oracle). Retained here for its evidence trail; it is no longer open.** What does a Go R1 *line* claim assert, given that `go test -coverprofile` cannot supply per-line statement identity? A cover block carries a positional extent plus `numStmts` — a count, never the statements' own positions — so `calc.go:21.27,26.2 2 1` says only "two statements somewhere in lines 21–26". Candidates: (1) accept block-extent attribution and document the narrowing, re-specifying P28's tuple at block granularity; (2) obtain real statement positions from a source-side Go AST helper, which is new capability belonging to P29; (3) narrow the Go R1 claim to block granularity in the artifact, which needs P21's v4 owner. | **Blocks P27 dispatch.** Raised by P27's own work item 6 and A-172, which required the carver to probe and freeze the mapping and named a three-witness contradiction a product decision. C-sol-1 ran the probe against real bytes in the pinned `go1.25.12` toolchain (`carve-assets/P27/BLOCKED-grammar.md`, `probe-results.json`) and found: the half-open *position* range is proven by blocks sharing a boundary position, but A-172's named discriminator — an end column of 1 — is producible only in source `gofmt` rewrites and discriminates nothing, because the column-1 token ending one block begins the next. Either candidate rule marks 17 lines executable in `calc.go` where the source has 7 statements (four function signatures, four closing braces, two statement continuations); `go` itself reported 71.4% = 5/7, siding with the hand manifest. On the real two-commit diff the inclusive rule, the half-open rule, and the manifest all report **66.7% changed-line coverage over different line sets**, so a percentage oracle cannot separate them — A-208's vacuous-agreement failure mode in the Go setting. (Corrected 2026-08-11: those sets are *not* disjoint, as this row first said. Block extent is a strict over-approximation — `{22,25} ⊂ {21,…,26}` — for the executable and executed sets, though not for the missing set, which executed-wins-on-overlap can launder.) Precedent cannot settle it: assay's `go_cover.py` and srdm's `covergate/profile.go` share the inclusive convention, and P28 exists to qualify assay *against* covergate, so adopting it makes that comparison circular — exactly what A-172's reason paragraph warns of. Options 2 and 3 reach outside P27's `scope.touch`. |

## Decided — session 38, 2026-08-11 (SQL-first resequence and the v5 contract)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-219 | **The wave is resequenced: schema v5 (`P33`) → SQL/DDL adapter and real-PostgreSQL integration (`P34`) → the ship milestone → then the Go work resumes with P27's re-carve, P28, and P29–P32.** Operator decision, relayed 2026-08-11. Three structural consequences fixed here: (a) B001 is **absorbed** rather than deferred — A-215's checkpoint questions are answered by P33's design and proved by P34's qualification, so no un-numbered checkpoint remains pending; (b) **P27–P32 keep their ids** even though they now run later, because ids are identity and the chain is order — A-153/A-167 already warn that pre-renumber ids are uninterpretable, and these are cited across merged packets, briefs, decisions and reports, so a third renumber would corrupt that trail; (c) the two new packages are split at the schema/adapter seam, matching P21's precedent that an artifact contract is its own package. **The risk A-215's ordering was chosen to avoid is knowingly accepted:** a second language's mutation contract is now frozen before the first language's mutation tier (P29) exists, and before any real Go R1 qualification (P28) exists. | A-217 removed the premise of the old order. It ruled A-O19 as option 2, so P27 is no longer a dispatchable package awaiting a ruling — it needs a re-carve around a real statement-position helper, which is the same architectural work SQL's parser/helper needs. Designing the helper boundary and the schema once, for both languages, is strictly cheaper than designing each twice and retrofitting. The B001 assessment independently found the hard blocker that makes v5 unavoidable regardless of order: v4's `mutation_operator` is a closed four-value Python-only enum, so SQL R2 cannot be spelled at all without a version bump, and A-138/A-170 make that a consumer migration owned by a real package rather than a tweak. On the accepted risk: the exposure is narrower than A-215 feared, because P33's design leaves Go's operator vocabulary deliberately empty for P29 to fill as an additive `oneOf` branch — so the second-language contract constrains Go's *namespace*, not its *operators*. The operator has accepted a v6 if that proves wrong. |
| A-220 | **Schema v5's shape is fixed by `nyxloom-trove/SCHEMA-V5-DESIGN.md`.** Five changes: (1) `language`, `source_roots` and `base` hoist out of `judgment.r1` into a new required `judgment.resolved`; (2) `mutation_operator` becomes language-qualified and closed per language (`python:*` keeping the existing four, `sql:*` gaining seven, **Go deliberately empty until P29**); (3) `mutation` gains a fifth `equivalent` bucket, paired both-present-or-both-absent with a declared `judgment.r2.equivalence_artifact` and excluded from the score denominator; (4) `judgment.r2` gains a required `kill_attribution` enum (`declared`/`unattributed`) plus optional `kill_signal_artifact`, and `mutant_outcome` an optional `kill_signal`; (5) a new optional top-level `helpers` array records what an adapter actually invoked, with a closed three-value `role`. Deliberately **excluded**: any SQL R3 space, any per-mutant time bound, and any change to `mutant_outcome`'s site identity. | (1) is a **v4 bug fix, not an SQL accommodation**: `judgment` permits `{r2}` with no `r1`, and A-192 already makes `R0,R2` legal, so a Python or Go `R0,R2` lane today records no language, no source roots, and no comparison commit — while R2 scopes mutation to changed lines against exactly that commit. SQL is only the first language for which `R0,R2` is the *sole* honest declaration. (2) a flat extension would let a Python lane declare `drop-check`; per-language closure makes that a load-time error, and leaving Go empty means a Go R2 declaration fails loudly instead of running and reporting nothing. (3) answers the write-side lossiness the B001 assessment found — migration-chain and idempotent-DDL mutants are no-ops that v4 would report as "survived", i.e. as evidence about the tests when it is evidence about the mutation; comparing two command-written artifacts keeps A-215's no-DSN boundary exactly intact. (4) does **not** close the kill-attribution gap and does not claim to: Assay observes exit status and declared artifacts, never the database's causality, so the honest move is to make the weakness visible in every artifact rather than absent — dstdns's witnessed `RestrictViolationError`-via-unrelated-trigger case is the proof that "killed" is weaker evidence than "survived" for schema. (5) the P27 probe's whole pinning exercise existed because symbolic toolchain references make a verdict unreproducible; `external_tools` declares what a lane needs and nothing recorded what actually ran. On the exclusions: SQL R3 is refused by construction because the `uncovered-line` canary needs R1 coverage that DDL has no tool to produce, and reserved-but-unusable schema space invites a plausible-looking implementation. The per-mutant bound was **checked rather than assumed** — `mutation.budget_exceeded` is already a per-mutant bucket, so the attribution concern raised in the B001 assessment already has an answer, leaving only a declared ceiling for which there is an argument and no measurement. Adding a field on an argument instead of evidence is precisely what the P27 probe taught against; the v6 trigger is named in the design document. |

## Decided — session 39, 2026-08-11 (P33 carve: Go vocabulary and locked v4 evidence)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-221 | **v5's `go:*` vocabulary is exactly three operators: `go:compare-swap` (`<`↔`<=`, `>`↔`>=`, `==`↔`!=`), `go:boolop-swap` (`&&`↔`\|\|`), and `go:bool-const-flip` (`true`↔`false`).** Operator decision, adopted with evidence rather than deference. **A `falsy-swap` analogue is deliberately excluded, not overlooked:** Python's exploits duck-typed truthiness (`0`/`""`/`[]`/`None` all falsy in boolean context), while every Go condition is a strict `bool`, so there is no 1:1 mapping and a `nil`-swap or zero-value operator would be new, unmeasured mutation-testing design. Also deliberately excluded: arithmetic-operator swap, increment/decrement, and statement removal. These operators are **declarable but unproducible** in P33 — no adapter generates them, so a Go R2 lane loads and renders payload-free `INCONCLUSIVE/MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` (A-183). P29 may extend the vocabulary with real evidence; the per-language `oneOf` shape makes that additive and needs no v6. | Verified against the real catalogue rather than assumed: `adapters/python.py`'s `_COMPARE_SWAP` is exactly `Lt↔LtE, Gt↔GtE, Eq↔NotEq, Is↔IsNot`, so the three Go operators are faithful analogues — Go's `==`/`!=` already perform identity comparison on pointers and interfaces, which is why nothing from Python's catalogue is missing, only inapplicable. The exclusions are not conservatism for its own sake: A-112 is the governing rule and `_COMPARE_SWAP`'s own comment states it — "this package reuses ONLY the catalogue, never extends it" — with an individually unsupported construct contributing zero sites rather than aborting. Importing a fuller mutation-testing operator set would be exactly the invention v5 was told to avoid. **This also corrects a defect in the carver's own v5 draft**, which left `go:*` empty and thereby made a Go R2 declaration fail at *load*; the design guide and A-183 both say Go renders `UNSUPPORTED` at runtime until P29 lands its helper, so the empty enum silently contradicted shipped doctrine. One implementation trap recorded for P29: `true` and `false` are predeclared *identifiers* in Go, not keywords, so they can be shadowed — a site generator must resolve them as the predeclared constants, never by matching token text. |
| A-222 | **v4 locked carve assets are historical evidence and are NOT rewritten to v5.** The 17 paths in `carve-assets/P33/migration-manifest.json`'s `locked_carver_owned` bucket stay exactly as they are. Where a locked acceptance suite asserts v4 artifact *shape* and would therefore break once the product emits v5, the suite's oracle moves into P33's own v5 acceptance material and the v4 suite is retired, with the carve report naming what coverage moved where. The implementer may not edit any path in that bucket; discovering that one seems to need editing is `escalate_if`. | A-138/A-170 make a schema version a consumer migration with no in-place upgrade, and A-176/A-206/A-214 lock these assets precisely so implementation pressure cannot weaken a frozen expectation. Rewriting P21's or P26's frozen v4 expectation into v5 would falsify the record of what those merged packages actually proved — the artifact would claim a package was accepted against a contract that did not exist when it was accepted. A-197 permits the carver to correct a locked asset for a *proven defect in that asset*; a later version bump is not such a defect, so A-197's exception does not apply and the assets are not touched at all. Naming the 17 paths mechanically, in a committed manifest, is what keeps this from being rediscovered as a mystery when 90 other files migrate around them. |

## Decided — session 40, 2026-08-11 (P33 re-carve after NOT READY)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-223 | **The v5 repair set, answering every externally visible decision the pre-dispatch review found unmade.** (a) **`base` is conditional, `language`/`source_roots` are not:** `judgment_resolved` requires only `language` and `source_roots`; `base` is required inside it only when `judgment` carries `r1` or `r2`. (b) **`kill_attribution` is DERIVED, never declared:** the single declared source is `judge.mutation.kill_signal_artifact`; present ⇒ `declared`, absent ⇒ `unattributed`. (c) **Helper correspondence holds only in the observable direction:** every `helpers` entry requires a correspondingly-judged claim (`mutation-sites` ⇒ an R2 claim with a `mutation` payload; `statement-positions` ⇒ an R1 claim with `coverage`); the converse — a claim requiring a `helpers` entry — is **deferred to P34**, which is the first package with a helper-producing adapter. (d) **An all-inert run has its own terminal:** a new `INCONCLUSIVE` reason code `ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT` fires when `killed + survived == 0` and `equivalent` is non-empty, ranked after `survived` in A-117's precedence. (e) **`kill_signal` is legal only on `killed` entries**, expressed in the schema by constraining the other four buckets' items. (f) **Provenance is pinned:** `resolved.base` is the pre-snapshot consumer-repository resolution, R1's in-snapshot resolution must be asserted equal to it, and `resolved.source_roots` is the declared project-relative spelling (A-049), never the relocated absolute scratch paths (A-149). (g) **v4's "at least one tier" guarantee is restored explicitly**, not silently dropped: `judgment` requires `resolved` *and* at least one of `r1`/`r2`/`r3`. (h) `$id` becomes `urn:assay:schema:verdict:5`. | Each answers a defect the review proved rather than argued. (a) `JUDGE_FIELDS_BY_RIGOR` was read directly: `R1` and `R2` both carry `base`, `R3` carries only `language`/`source_roots`/`canary` — so hoisting all three unconditionally required a value the product has never had for an `R0,R3` lane, and three committed fixtures are already in that shape. The evidence also confirms the *other* two hoists are correct, since all three tiers declare them. (b) inventing a lane field would have handed an externally visible grammar decision to an implementer; deriving it has direct precedent in this codebase — A-036 derives `argv_modified` from `argv_appended` precisely because "deriving it removes a whole class of artifact in which the two disagree". Same class, same fix. (c) the original invariant's antecedent — "was produced with an external helper" — is not readable from artifact bytes, so any implementation was vacuous; the reversed direction *is* readable, and A-142/A-144 already establish deferring a check until a producer makes the state reachable. (d) without it, `killed 0, survived 0, equivalent 3` walks the shipped `judge_mutation` to `PASS`, which is A-026/A-035's 0/0-is-100% bug reintroduced inside the change that exists to fix a lossiness problem — and it also makes O3's exclusion clause falsifiable for the first time, since an implementation that ignores `equivalent` now renders `PASS` where the contract says `INCONCLUSIVE`. A-050 requires stopping rather than inventing a reason code alone; this is that ruling. (e) a kill signal on a mutant nothing killed is a contradiction, and the constraint is locally expressible, so per A-182 it belongs in the schema. (f) v5 collapses two `base` resolutions into one field and two `source_roots` spellings into one; leaving which-wins unstated is the A-143/A-149 family that has already been wrong twice. (g) dropping `minProperties` was an undocumented widening that made `judgment: {resolved}` valid for a run that judged nothing — the A-025/A-136 family. |
| A-224 | **P33 executes A-222's second clause: the registered gate stops running P26's v4 acceptance suite, and P33 supplies the v5 acceptance material that carries its coverage forward.** The P26 locked assets remain frozen and unedited as historical evidence; they are simply no longer *executed*. `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh` therefore enters `scope.touch`, and the carve supplies a locked v5 acceptance suite naming exactly what coverage moved from `carve-assets/P26/test_acceptance.py` and where it landed. Consequent scope corrections, all of which were defects: `src/assay/mutation.py` and `src/assay/errors.py` move from forbidden to `touch` (they own `judge_mutation` and the reason vocabulary that A-223(d) changes), `src/assay/cli.py`, `gate/python/**` and `tests/test_verdict_schema_is_packaged.py` gain explicit `touch` status. | The review proved there was no in-scope path to a green gate: `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh:231` runs P26's locked suite, which asserts `verify_document(<v4 template>) == []` and exact equality against a v4-shaped artifact, both of which fail the moment the shipped schema is v5 — while the suite is locked by A-214, its templates are inside A-222's frozen bucket, and the gate script had no scope status. A-222's own second clause already prescribed the remedy and the first carve never executed it, which is the A-072 shape: a ruling that reached `decisions.md` but not the handoff. The scope corrections are not widening for convenience: `verify.py` (already in scope) imports `judge_mutation` from `mutation.py`, so every question about how `equivalent` affects a judged status already crossed the boundary I had drawn, and A-223(d) cannot be implemented without the reason vocabulary. Forbidding a file the package must change is the same defect P27's missing status on `go_cover.py` was. |
| A-225 | **A-221's stated reasoning is corrected; its operator catalogue is unchanged.** The three `go:*` operators stand as a faithful transcription of `_COMPARE_SWAP` under A-112, and the `falsy-swap` exclusion stands. What is withdrawn is the justification that an empty `go:*` enum "would have made a Go R2 declaration fail at load, while A-183 and the design guide say Go renders `INCONCLUSIVE/MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` at runtime". That is wrong about the shipped product. The correct justification is narrower: v5's vocabulary is complete for every language whose operator catalogue is already known and transcribable, so P29 needs no version bump to reach Go. **P33 does not make a Go or SQL R2 lane reachable and must not claim to.** | Read directly rather than recalled: `cli._resolve_declared_adapters` runs `for level in lane.rigor: if level != "R0": registry.get_adapter(built_in, lane.judge.language, level)`, and `_built_in_registry` registers Python only. So a Go or SQL R2 lane is refused `ERROR/BAD_LANE_CONFIG` before anything executes, per A-139 — identically whether `go:*` is populated or empty. A-183 says as much itself: "The Go R2 producer remains unreachable through the registry." My load-versus-runtime argument therefore described a distinction that does not exist in this build. The original work item 5 compounded it by requiring behaviour that needs `cli.py` registry changes, `adapters/go.py` (which P33 forbids), and a non-existent `adapters/sql.py` that is P34's deliverable; it is replaced by a work item that adds the vocabulary and *proves the refusal behaviour unchanged*. Recording the correction rather than quietly restating it, because a future reader who found the old reasoning would conclude the registry behaves in a way it does not. |

## Decided — session 41, 2026-08-11 (P33 round-2 repairs; the inventory closes the class)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-226 | **Every locked v4 expectation that live gate code compares against gets a carver-supplied v5 sibling under `carve-assets/P33/expected/`, and the consumer is repointed at the sibling. The v4 original is never edited.** The inventory is produced by `carve-assets/P33/sweep_v4_consumers.py`, a locked asset that walks the registered gate's transitive execution path rather than grepping for patterns, and it is **three** consumers: `gate/python/qualify_topos.py` (P25's `pass`/`missing` templates), `carve-assets/P26/test_acceptance.py` (P26's four), and `tests/test_python_qualification.py:259` (P25's `pass`). The third was found by the sweep and by neither review. **A-224 is amended: P26's module is NOT retired.** The gate deselects exactly the four template-coupled tests plus `test_registered_gate_runs_locked_acceptance_from_the_wheel_and_marks_it` and keeps running the other nineteen. | Round 1 named one instance of this class and I repaired that instance plus a scanner pattern for it; round 2 found a second at the very next gate step; the sweep found a third. That is A-141's rule — *a defect class closes when its inventory closes, not when its instance does* — and the correct closure is an inventory of **consumers**, not another content pattern, because `qualify_topos.py` builds `carve-assets/P25/expected` from `/`-joined components and no grep for that literal can find it. A v5 sibling beats an in-harness v4→v5 projection because a projection is machinery to be trusted whereas a frozen expectation is evidence to be checked, and the round-2 reviewer correctly rejected an unfrozen transform inside a gate harness as a proof source. On A-224's amendment: retiring P26's module dropped 18 of 24 tests, only 4 of which touch artifact shape, and the lost 18 include A-212's process-group kill on a witnessed descendant-held pipe, aggregate bounds before the first Git call, literal-pathspec identity and annotated-tag peel refusal — security boundaries this project paid for with real incidents and which v5 does not touch. My "nothing else in P26's suite asserted artifact shape" was a true sentence that silently substituted shape coverage for coverage. `--deselect` lives in the gate script, which is already in scope, and no locked byte moves. |
| A-227 | **`judge.mutation.kill_signal_artifact` is reserved in the v5 artifact contract but NOT declarable in lane config until P34.** `config` refuses it at load with a typed error naming P34. `kill_attribution` therefore derives to `unattributed` for every real P33 lane, while both values remain expressible as documents and are exercised by locked templates. **`helpers.role` keeps all three values with a uniform rule:** every entry requires at least one computed claim above R0 carrying a payload, tightened per role — `mutation-sites` ⇒ an R2 claim with `mutation`, `statement-positions` ⇒ an R1 claim with `coverage`, `executable-code` ⇒ either, because executability gates both changed-line classification and mutation-site discovery. **The `only-if` half of the base rule is closed now:** an `r3`-only `judgment` carrying `base` is refused by the schema. | On `kill_signal_artifact`: making a field declarable while shipping no producer for the value it implies creates a legal lane whose own artifact has no representable shape — the model would refuse to construct what the config invited. Reserving the artifact shape while refusing the declaration keeps v5 stable for P34 without inventing a bounded-safe-input reader on the A-210 scale inside a contract migration. On `role`: leaving one of three values unruled is exactly what this handoff's own test constraints forbid — *"a vocabulary needs a rejected value per language, not one rejection standing for all three"* — and narrowing the enum would churn v5 again when P34 arrives. On the `only-if`: the round-2 reviewer verified the producer cannot reach that state (the loader refuses `judge.base` on an R0,R3 lane as inert config, A-062), so this is not a producer bug — but `verify.py` exists to judge *foreign* documents, A-182 assigns locally expressible conditionals to the schema, and this is one `if/then/not` branch in the identical form of the ones already there. Cheap, in-class, and it closes a hole in the one layer whose whole purpose is untrusted input. |
| A-228 | **`ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT` inherits every constraint its three sibling terminals carry, in every layer that owns one**: a schema branch requiring the `mutation` payload and pinning `rigor` to `R2`; membership in `verdict.py`'s `_MUTATION_ONLY_REASON_CODES`; the limit sentinel's bucket enumeration extended to `equivalent`; and the "FOUR identity buckets" prose corrected to five in both the schema description and `verdict.py`'s error text. | The round-2 review proved from the locked bytes that `MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED`, `NO_MUTANTS` and `MUTANT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` each get an explicit reason/payload/rigor branch and the new terminal got none — so a payload-free, non-R2 `ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT` validates. That is verbatim the forgery A-136 closed for `NO_MUTANTS`, reopened one bucket over, inside the change that exists to fix a lossiness problem. The root cause is the same one A-226 names: I added the terminal where the round-1 finding pointed and did not carry it to the layers that constrain its siblings. Recording it separately from A-226 because the *repair* is different — an inventory of layers rather than of consumers — while the *failure mode* is identical, and a future reader should be able to see that both instances share it. |

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-229 | **The sweep's own closure gaps are closed and the sweep is pinned by a planted-decoy oracle; the P26 deselect list drops to four; CA8 is taken rather than deferred.** (a) `sweep_v4_consumers.py` v2 seeds from `assay.toml`'s declared argv and the gate script's inline heredocs as well as the gate script itself, follows subprocess targets, resolves dotted modules through the real package layout, handles `from . import X`, and replaces the four-name/five-regex predicate with "reads a path under a carver-owned frozen tree, by any idiom, and compares it" — plus a second `indirect-path-from-caller` category for a consumer whose frozen path arrives on its command line. (b) `test_sweep_finds_a_planted_decoy_consumer` plants a consumer under `src/assay/adapters/` reached by a dotted subpackage import and requires the sweep to find it; `test_sweep_finds_every_known_consumer` freezes the five established consumers; `test_sweep_closure_never_leaves_the_project` pins the boundary. (c) `test_all_structural_and_aggregate_bounds_precede_every_git_call` is **removed from the deselect list** — it tests A-210's aggregate-bounds ordering and touches no artifact shape. (d) **CA8 is taken in `gate/python/qualify_topos.py`** via the existing `base_override`, as a scenario declaring `judge.base` as a tag on the base commit. | (a) Round 3 demonstrated a *real* missed consumer — `gate/distribution/release_wheel.py`, executed in the gate and comparing against a frozen release manifest — and four further structural gaps, of which the sharpest is that `src/assay/adapters/**` and `coverage_parsers/**` were unreachable by leaf-name resolution while `adapters/python.py` sits in this package's own `scope.touch`. The indirect category exists because `release_wheel.py` receives its manifest path from its caller, so **no name-based predicate could ever have found it**; a longer directory list would not have closed the class. (b) Nothing ran or pinned the sweep, so every one of these gaps would have regressed silently — the inventory tool needs the same falsifiability it exists to provide, and a decoy nobody imports would prove nothing because it genuinely is not in the execution path, which is why the decoy is reached by a real import edge. (c) A round-2 summary table and its own appendix disagreed about that test and the wrong one was adopted, dropping a security oracle by transcription slip. (d) The deferral was reasoned from "it needs a real repository, so it belongs with the producer proof", and that premise does not survive checking: `qualify_topos.py` already writes the lane's `base`, already has a `base_override` parameter, already uses it at `:836`, is `implementer_owned`, sits in `scope.touch`, and is already edited by work item 8b. A-143 is explicit that a fixture failing to make declared and resolved genuinely different proves nothing, and P33 is the package that collapses two independently-resolved base values into one field. Raised in round 1 (B11), round 2 (O1/CA8) and round 3 (R3-D3); deferring a fourth time would be choosing not to close it. |

## Decided — session 43, 2026-08-11 (P33 round-4 repairs; two independent reviews)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-230 | **Four remaining artifact-contract gaps are pinned, and one P33 claim is narrowed rather than propped up.** (a) **`helpers` emission default: OMIT.** An artifact that used no helper has no `helpers` key; it never serializes `helpers: []`. (b) **`equivalence_artifact` gets the same config disposition as `kill_signal_artifact`:** reserved in the artifact contract, refused at config load with a typed `LaneConfigError` naming P34. (c) **Both refusals are pinned to `LaneConfigError`,** not to "any exception whose message contains two substrings". (d) **P33's claim about `kill_attribution` is narrowed:** P33 specifies the derivation `declared ⟺ kill_signal_artifact present` and does **not** claim to witness it, because its only source is P33-refused until P34. `unattributed` is the only value any P33 lane produces; `declared` is artifact grammar exercised by documents. P34 witnesses the derivation with a real producer. | (a) leaving it unpinned made externally visible artifact grammar an implementer's invention, and omission is the rule the artifact already follows for `judgment`, which is absent rather than empty when nothing was judged — A-025/A-136's "do not record a fact nothing witnesses". (b) `equivalence_artifact` was declarable while `kill_signal_artifact` was refused, an inconsistency with no reason behind it: P33 ships a producer for neither. (c) a test accepting any `Exception` carrying two substrings is satisfied by a wrong exception class with an accidentally-right message, which is the A-060 "an oracle that cannot fail" class one level down. (d) the alternative was inventing a model-level construction seam so P33 could prove a derivation whose input P33 itself forbids — machinery built solely to make a claim testable, which is worse than not making the claim. Narrowing is the honest move and it costs nothing real: the rule is still specified, still schema-enforced for documents, and P34 is the package that can actually exercise it. |
| A-231 | **`SCHEMA-V5-DESIGN.md` is superseded in part by the decisions that followed it, and the handoff now says so explicitly: where the locked schema or a named decision conflicts with design-doc prose, the schema and the decision win.** The two known conflicts are corrected in place: "Go gets no operators" (superseded by A-221) and `resolved.base` as unconditionally required (superseded by A-223a). | The handoff calls that file "the specification", so an implementer reading it got the wrong answer on two settled questions — a second-opinion reviewer with no access to the decision history hit exactly that and concluded the package contradicted itself. A design document written before three repair rounds cannot be the specification without a precedence rule; adding one is cheaper and more honest than pretending prose and locked bytes will stay in sync. |

## Decided — session 44, 2026-08-11 (P33 round-5; a count is not evidence)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-232 | **A stated pass/fail count is not evidence. Every carve that claims a test run, a sweep run, or a fixture load must paste the actual command and its actual output, and must classify each pre-implementation red as LEGITIMATE (green under a correct implementation) or ILLEGITIMATE (unsatisfiable by any implementation).** Adopted for this wave and recorded as general carver practice. | Twice in this package a locked test could not pass against *any* implementation and an aggregate count concealed it: round 4 found three config tests calling a function that does not exist, and round 5 found the same three still unsatisfiable after that repair because the fixture was missing seven required lane fields — the fix was made and never re-run. "30 failed / 8 passed" reads identically whether a red is a correct oracle awaiting its implementation or a test that is simply broken, which is precisely the distinction that matters before dispatch. The classification is cheap: this round's 31 reds trace to exactly four causes, all legitimate, and the check that produced that finding was one `grep -E "^E "` over the real output. |
| A-233 | **The sweep gains a location rule and a supplier guard on both indirect branches.** A file *inside* a frozen tree that resolves paths relative to itself is a consumer of that tree by location, since no string in it can ever name the tree. An environ-sourced consumer counts only when some other member of the gate's execution path — **including the gate shell script** — actually sets that variable, and the variable name is resolved through module constants. | `carve-assets/P20/test_acceptance.py` does `ASSET_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent`: a fact about location, not contents, so text matching could never find it — and it had been appearing in the inventory only by accident, because it mentions `os.environ` elsewhere for an unrelated reason. Meanwhile the environ branch had no supplier guard at all, so it reported anything mentioning `os.environ`. Fixing both required three real failures to be driven out one at a time: the name is a module constant (`ENV_VAR = "ASSAY_SELF_HOSTING_VERDICT"`), its setter is the gate's shell script rather than any module, and the setter spells it bare (`NAME="$path"`) rather than quoted. None of the three was visible by reading; each came from running the sweep and printing what it saw. |

## Decided — session 45, 2026-08-11 (Fable full-codebase review of merged assay)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-234 | **The committed Go coverage fixtures are STALE and are a tracked pre-P27-re-carve blocker, not regenerated now.** `tests/fixtures/go/hello/hello.out` and `tests/fixtures/canary/go/greet/greet_control.out` are hand-shaped to the disproven "block extent == statement lines" premise. The real bytes are captured at `carve-assets/P27/witness/coverage-hello-fixture-REAL.out`, the discrepancy and the re-carve's four owed items are recorded in `carve-assets/P27/GO-FIXTURES-STALE.md`, and `hello.go`, `adapters/go.py` and `coverage_parsers/go_cover.py` all carry stale banners. Nobody may cite these fixtures as evidence that Go union fidelity is proven (A-009/A-042) until the re-carve lands. | I regenerated against the pinned `tester-unified-go:local` that P27's carve already froze, so a real answer was available for the first time since P08 wrote these by hand with no toolchain (A-042). The committed fixture is wrong in **both** coordinates, which is more than the review claimed: real is `hello.go:29.32,31.2` / `36.35,38.2`, committed is `29.34,30.42` / `36.37,37.44` — blocks end at `Rbrace + 1` per A-218's own arithmetic, not at the return statement's column, and the start columns are off by two. `hello.go`'s claim that closing braces are "untracked by any block" is false of real profiles. **Regenerating alone would not be an improvement:** `test_adapters_go_union_fidelity.py` encodes the current mapping, and the correct new expectation depends on the statement-position oracle A-217 ruled for. Swapping a hand-guessed profile for a real one while the parser still over-approximates would replace a wrong profile with a real profile whose block extents are then read as statement truth — exactly the conflation A-O19 exists to remove. Capturing the evidence and sequencing the fix into the package that owns the oracle is the honest split. |
| A-235 | **`LanguageAdapter.statement_spans` has no seam through which A-217's oracle could ever be invoked, and closing that is a named design item the P27 re-carve owns — with its own probe, not implementer improvisation.** `base.py` consults `statement_spans` only for lines appearing in NO coverage bucket, gated on `requires_span_attribution`; `go_cover.py`'s `range(start, end + 1)` puts every block line into executed-or-missing, so for Go there is never an unattributed line. The re-carve must rule between (i) the parser emitting block extents as a distinct over-approximation the evaluator then intersects with statement positions — which needs a representation `CoverageProfile`/`FileCoverage` does not have today — or (ii) correcting at the adapter/evaluate boundary instead. | A-217(e) accepted "the helper-protocol boundary A-163 assigned to P29 now reaches back into P27" in principle; this is the concrete shape of that cost, and it is a real interface decision rather than a detail. Discovering it mid-dispatch would repeat the failure mode six P33 review rounds were spent removing: a work item whose observable does not exist. Recording it now means the re-carve's probe is designed against a known gap instead of finding it. |
| A-236 | **Two of the three schema under-enforcement findings are closed in the schema; the third is withdrawn.** (b) `judgment.r2` gains the `kill_attribution`/`kill_signal_artifact` pairing its own description already promised — `declared` requires the artifact, `unattributed` forbids it. (c) The `ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT` branch's description called its own check "cross-object" when `killed`/`survived`/`equivalent` are siblings inside one `mutation` object and the neighbouring `MUTANT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` branch already constrains exactly those fields locally; corrected to say LOCAL. **(a) is deliberately NOT done** — see A-237. | (b) enforcing a rule the schema's own description already stated costs nothing and closes it for a foreign document; verified that `declared`-without-artifact and `unattributed`-with-artifact are now rejected while both correct shapes still validate. (c) is the same stale-description class six review rounds found one instance at a time, and it was factually wrong rather than merely unenforced. |
| A-237 | **A-182's schema-ownership claim is narrowed rather than enforced: the shipped schema owns refusal of IMPOSSIBLE payloads, not requiredness of EVIDENCE. Requiredness-of-evidence belongs to the model and the raw verifier, and that split is deliberate.** A-182's "every locally expressible rule … including reason/payload conditionals within a single object" overclaims and should be read with this narrowing. | I attempted the enforcement first and the full suite refused it. Adding PASS/FAIL-requires-its-payload branches for R1/R2/R3 broke two tests in `tests/test_verdict_conformance.py`, and their own assertion text reads *"claims-cover-declared-rigor is NOT expressible in JSON Schema 2020-12 — this stays schema-valid **on purpose**"*. That file carries a **family of four** such assertions, so the schema's silence is a documented layer boundary authored by a prior package, not accretion. Overriding it would have meant editing tests that exist to prove layer independence, in the same commit that changed the contract they check — the precise move this project distrusts. The reviewer offered exactly these two options; the evidence chose between them. Note the boundary is not a hole: `verify.py` refuses a payload-free PASS with "the strongest form of exactly the lie P16 exists to catch", so every case is caught in the real pipeline. What was genuinely wrong was the doctrine text promising more than the schema delivers, and that is what this fixes. |
| A-238 | **P34's carve must rule on two things before dispatch, not discover them.** (i) B001 item 3 — whether the flat seven-method `LanguageAdapter` stays honest for an R2-only language, given `has_executable_code`, `normalize_coverage_key`, `statement_spans`, `inject_import_break` and `inject_uncovered_line` are all meaningless for SQL — is still unanswered and is the first real design question P34 faces. (ii) A genuine tension between A-183 and V5-5: the helpers-correspondence rule binds a `mutation-sites` entry to an R2 claim **carrying a mutation payload**, but `MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED` is payload-free by design, so recording the helper's identity when a SQL parser fails on unparseable DDL makes the document schema-invalid — the producer must omit exactly the provenance you would want to debug the failure. | Both surface on P34's first real inputs — (i) at interface-design time, (ii) on the first unparseable DDL file — and both are externally visible rulings rather than implementation choices. AUTHORING's NOT READY criterion applies to a carve that leaves either open. (ii) has candidate shapes worth weighing rather than improvising: permit a payload-free `helpers` entry specifically for the discovery-failure terminal, or give discovery failure a shape that carries provenance without a mutation payload. I am recording the question and its candidates rather than ruling now, because P34's carve is where the SQL adapter's real constraints become visible and a ruling made here would be made blind. |

## Decided — session 53, 2026-08-11 (dstdns real-lane isolation: what ships now)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-254 | **New optional lane key `env_required`: the subset of `env_passthrough` whose ABSENCE refuses the lane, `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`, decided before any Git work.** A name in `env_required` that `env_passthrough` does not declare is refused at *load*. Empty by default, so every existing lane is unchanged. **This ships now and is the only code dstdns's request needs from assay today.** | Built because the hole is live and I measured it rather than inferring it: `resolve_command_plan` copies a passthrough name only `if name in source`, so a lane declaring `env_passthrough = ["CIU_IMAGE_REVISION", "CIU_INSTANCE_ID"]` with neither set emits `env_effective` **without them and PASSes clean** — driven end to end before the fix. dstdns asked for provenance that holds "**by construction**"; without this it holds by hope, and the artifact that records nothing looks identical to the artifact that records everything. That is A-025's rule (absence of a signal is never a positive fact) broken at the environment layer, and it is precisely the hollow-evidence failure mode six P33 rounds existed to remove. `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` reuses the terminal that already means "the lane as declared cannot be honoured here" (the tracked-coverage-artifact refusal); a dedicated `NO_MEASUREMENT` code would be marginally more precise and would cost a closed-enum widening that every consumer's schema copy would then reject — a price A-138/A-170 make deliberate rather than incidental. Pre-repository ordering is asserted by test, not assumed: a dirty tree must not mask a missing identity. |
| A-255 | **"Embed provenance in the verdict" NEEDS NO ASSAY SCHEMA CHANGE — it already works, and what is missing is a trustworthy PRODUCER, which is ciu's.** `env_effective` is a lane-resolved field recorded on every outcome where a lane resolved; a lane declaring `env_passthrough`/`env_required` for a provenance variable has that value in the artifact verbatim, and the artifact verifies clean. **Verified by driving a real S3 lane:** `env_effective` carried `CIU_IMAGE_REVISION=1b369e23` and `CIU_INSTANCE_ID=dstdns-pkgP96`, `verify` clean. **No new field is added, and none should be.** What is NOT possible today, and must not be faked: *verifying* provenance. assay cannot read an OCI label from inside the container (A-030 — it never shells out to docker), so until **CIU-21** injects the image's own baked label as an env var, whatever is recorded is **caller-asserted, not ciu-attested**, and the artifact must not imply otherwise. | The temptation was a `provenance` object in the verdict schema. Refused: a field whose only producer is a caller's assertion is a hollow claim with a schema blessing, which is worse than no field — and A-029 makes the schema the *only* external contract, so a field there is a promise to every consumer. Recording an environment value in `env_effective` promises exactly what is true ("this ran with this variable set to this") and nothing more. The honest ladder, in order: **today** — record it via `env_required`, caller-asserted; **after CIU-21** — the same recipe becomes ciu-attested with zero assay change, because the value then comes from the image's own label; **after CIU-20** — `ciu provenance --json` becomes assay's first Tier-2 *adjudicated* evidence entry, which is the only route that is verified rather than recorded and the only one that survives post-hoc audit. Enforcement (refusing on mismatch) is a further step needing a new reason code and therefore a v6-class widening; it is NOT proposed now. |
| A-256 | **Same-instance re-verify is PROCESS DISCIPLINE over an already-existing field, NOT an assay schema feature. The comparison belongs to the verdict comparer, not to `verify`.** Two verdicts are "the same instance" iff their `env_effective` carry the same instance identity — checkable from the artifacts today, by anything, with no assay change. assay will not add a cross-artifact check. | Three reasons, in decreasing order of how much they settle it. (1) **`verify_document(document)` is single-document by construction.** "These two artifacts came from the same instance" is a relation *between* artifacts, not a property *of* one; A-029's contract is that a consumer can independently verify one artifact against the shipped schema. A cross-artifact relation has no home in that contract and would quietly change what `assay verify` means. (2) **assay could not verify the identity anyway.** At S3/S4 the instance name is ciu's fact, arriving through the environment; assay can record it (transparency) but calling it verified would be the same hollow claim A-255 refuses. (3) **The mechanical version of this is the comparer**, already the accepted roadmap direction for cross-artifact work — a CLI verb over files, which is exactly where a same-instance assertion belongs. Until it exists, the discipline is one line for a controller: point the reviewer at the same `CIU_INSTANCE_ID` and let both artifacts record it. That is not weaker than a schema feature; the artifacts carry the evidence either way, and the only question is who compares them. |

## Decided — session 52, 2026-08-11 (the hollow-PASS/FAIL gap, closed)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-251 | **The hollow-PASS/FAIL gap is CLOSED, in the schema and in the raw verifier, and A-182's original one-sentence doctrine is RESTORED: the schema owns every locally expressible rule, requiredness of evidence included.** Four new `claim.allOf` branches, transcribed from `Claim._check_a_judged_status_carries_its_own_payload` and `_MUTATION_ONLY_REASON_CODES` verbatim: R1 ∧ status∈{PASS,FAIL} ⇒ `coverage`; R2 ∧ status=PASS ⇒ `mutation`; R3 ∧ status∈{PASS,FAIL,INCONCLUSIVE} ⇒ `canary`; `reason_code`=MUTANTS_SURVIVED ⇒ `mutation` + rigor R2. **And nothing else** — a payload-free R2 **FAIL** stays legal because it is A-116's own truthful propagation shape, as do the payload-free ERROR/NO_MEASUREMENT/BUDGET_EXCEEDED terminals, which describe machinery that never produced a result to judge. A matching `verify._check_a_judged_status_carries_its_own_payload` gives the rule a second, independently reachable witness. **A-237's narrowing and A-240's defence of it are superseded.** | The decisive fact is one I verified myself rather than transcribed: the narrowed doctrine ("the schema owns refusal of impossible payloads, not requiredness of evidence") **was already false of the shipped schema in three places** — `claim.allOf` branches 7, 8 and 9 each carry `then: {required: ["mutation"]}`, keyed on `NO_MUTANTS`, `MUTANT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and `ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT`. So the choice was never "tighter schema vs simpler doctrine"; it was "make the doctrine true again vs write a third, more contorted one to excuse the gap". `MUTANTS_SURVIVED` was the one member of the model's `_MUTATION_ONLY_REASON_CODES` the schema had never pinned, which is why the fourth branch is per-reason-code rather than an enum — it matches how this schema already expresses the rule. **Fable's flip condition was checked and does NOT hold:** `SCHEMA-V5-DESIGN.md`'s named v6 trigger (`per_mutant_seconds`) says *"Measure before adding"* and is conditional on a measurement P34's probe has not taken, and the other candidate v6 driver (adjudicated-evidence fields for the provenance integration) is post-Go by Fable's own sequencing. Folding into a v6 that may never exist would have left the gap open on a contingency. **Two test vehicles needed repair, both predicted, and both were vehicle faults rather than doctrine faults — verified individually:** `test_verdict_reason_codes.py`'s `a_claim_with` helper already documented the rule ("the claim this helper builds has to be honest about rigor and payload") and was simply missing the `MUTANTS_SURVIVED` row; `test_verdict_conformance.py::test_verify_rejects_claims_for_an_undeclared_rigor_level` used a bare R1 claim as its vehicle, and giving it a real coverage payload *strengthens* its own assertion — the extra claim is now independently schema-valid, so the only thing wrong with that document really is the cross-object rule JSON Schema cannot see. |
| A-252 | **In-place tightening of a shipped schema version is legal ONLY when a DIFFERENTIAL sweep proves zero truthful documents regress, with the sweep output pasted. This is the SECOND such tightening of v5 (A-236b was the first); a third needs an explicit ruling, not this precedent.** The sweep must be differential — valid-before versus valid-after — not "validate everything against the new schema". | Stated because the naive form of the sweep gave a wrong answer on the first run: validating the 49 committed v5 documents against the tightened schema flagged **six** as broken, and every one of those six is a carve-asset template carrying `@PLACEHOLDER@` tokens (`@ENDED@`, `@KILLED_REPLACEMENT_SHA@`) that is not schema-valid as literal bytes under *either* schema. Reporting them would have been a false alarm; not noticing they were already invalid would have made "zero break" unfalsifiable in the other direction. The differential form answers the question actually being asked: **43 valid before and after, 6 invalid before and after, 0 regressed.** The sweep also has to be shown non-vacuous, or "zero regressions" proves nothing about branches nothing reaches: the four new branches are entered by 10, 2, 5 and 2 real committed claims respectively, and 4 payload-free non-PASS R2 claims — A-116's shape — stay valid. Both layers were then proved independently load-bearing by controlled break: emptying the raw table reddens exactly the 7 raw tests and no schema test; deleting the schema branches reddens exactly the 7 schema tests and no raw test. |
| A-253 | **`MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`'s producer — the effective-PATH preflight over an adapter's declared `external_tools` — belongs to P34, unconditionally, and NOT to whichever package first happens to need an external tool. A-246's assignment to P27 is superseded.** P27's re-carve then only *declares* its statement-position helper in `external_tools`; the mechanism already exists and is already tested. This holds **whether or not** P34's own DDL parser turns out to be external: if P34 goes stdlib-only, it still builds the preflight and tests it against a declared-empty tuple, which is the honest control anyway. | This restores what A-163 actually said rather than inventing a new split: its own words are *"P21 owns the vocabulary, P26 owns effective-PATH reachability, and **P27 only adds the helper declaration**"* — the mechanism was never P27's. A-246 recorded the P22 → P26 → P27 hop as a fait accompli and, in doing so, quietly promoted P27 from declarer to builder. Three reasons P34 is the right owner now: it runs first under A-248, so assigning it there means the mechanism exists before *either* consumer needs it; the preflight is language-free (a bounded lookup over a declared tuple), so it is core work that no adapter should own by accident; and P27's re-carve is already carrying A-217's oracle, A-234's fixture regeneration and A-239's new protocol hook — adding "invent the external-tool preflight" to that is how the harder problem gets solved badly. The alternative I rejected was a conditional ruling ("P34 owns it iff its probe picks the external route"), which is precisely the leave-it-to-whoever-writes-code-first outcome this decision exists to prevent. |

## Decided — session 51, 2026-08-11 (ship: cmru adoption + zipapp, implemented)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-249 | **B002/B003 are IMPLEMENTED, five of the six planned steps landed.** New `gate/distribution/build_release.py` (stdlib-only, standalone, in `release_wheel.py`'s register) makes the private exact-OID `--no-local` sparse clone, installs the locked five-wheel closure with `pip --require-hashes` from the committed wheelhouse, builds with `--no-index --no-build-isolation`, then emits a reproducible zipapp built **from that wheel**, both `.sha256` sidecars, and — only for a tagged build — A-200's release manifest. `cmru.toml` gains `[project.assay]` (`prefix = "assay-v"`, `artifacts = ["wheel"]`) with an explicit `steps.build` so cmru's built-in `python -m build` never runs, `steps.run-tests` pointing at the **registered gate** rather than a bare pytest (A-040), and `steps.push` invoking the built-in `wheel-publish` explicitly with `--extra-asset` globs. assay is added to `project_order`/`default_projects` and all four `step_project_order` lists. **Step 6 (the first real release) is NOT done and is not mine to take** — see A-250. | Everything here was measured rather than reasoned, and three of the findings changed the design. (1) **Reproducibility needed two fixes, not one.** The zipapp scoping had only found the mtime issue; a real build showed the **wheel** itself differs between builds (`93a562cb…` vs `5f35bb65…`) until `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` is set from HEAD's own commit timestamp — `pyproject.toml`'s build-system comment already named that mechanism and nothing enforced it. With the wheel fixed the zipapp *still* differed, and diffing the archives named the culprits: `direct_url.json` (PEP 610, records the wheel's absolute `file://` path) and `RECORD`'s `../../bin/assay` line (the console-script shim embeds the building interpreter's shebang). Both are metadata about one installation, not about the distribution. Stripping them gives byte-identical archives from two different output directories. (2) **`zipapp -m` would have silently broken every non-zero exit.** Its generated entry point is `assay.cli.main()` with no `sys.exit`, so the process always exits 0 — a FAIL verdict would read as success at the one boundary a consumer reads. Verified by reading `zipapp`'s own template, and `__main__.py` is now written by hand with a test driving a real failing lane through the built artifact. (3) **`release_wheel.py` accepts an SCM development identity** — A-200's text says development builds are not release manifests, but the helper validates the *spelling* and `.devN+g<sha>` is legal PEP 440. The builder now asks git (`describe --exact-match --match assay-v*`) instead of pattern-matching a string, which is the first mechanical enforcement of that policy. A near-miss worth recording: `cmru status` reports assay's first release would be `assay-v0.1.0`, which is *also* `pyproject.toml`'s `fallback_version` — blacklisting that string outright, which was my first instinct, would have refused assay's own first release. The tag is the discriminator, not the spelling. |
| A-250 | **cmru's `wheel-publish` gained an additive `--extra-asset` flag (repeatable, glob-capable, default empty), and that is the only change made to a shared tool.** `publish_versioned` has always accepted `extra_assets`; the CLI handler simply never exposed it, so a `wheel` project could not attach a companion artifact without reimplementing the release call. Zero matches is a hard error, never a silent skip. **The first real release is NOT taken.** `cmru release --project assay --dry-run` reaches cmru's own release-transaction guard and exits 1: *"Local main is 103 commit(s) ahead of origin/main"*. Confirmed to be cmru's global guard and not an assay misconfiguration — `--project ciu --dry-run` fails identically. Publishing therefore needs a push of `main` plus explicit authorisation, and both are the operator's call. | The gap was CLI plumbing, not design, which is what made touching a file seven products share acceptable; it is defaulted off, and a differential test asserts that a publish with no `--extra-asset` produces exactly the two assets it always did. cmru's own suite: 453 passed before, 458 passed after (4 new tests, 1 renamed for globs), and the pass-through was proved load-bearing by controlled break — dropping it reddens exactly `test_extra_assets_are_attached_to_the_same_release`. Not publishing is not timidity: a GitHub Release is irreversible and outward-facing, and the dry run's refusal is the release model working as designed rather than an obstacle to route around. |

## Decided — session 50, 2026-08-11 (ship moves ahead of SQL)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-248 | **RESEQUENCED AGAIN: the ship milestone (cmru adoption + zipapp) runs BEFORE P34. Execution order is now `P20–P26 (done) → P33 (done) → [ship: cmru + zipapp] → P34 → P27 (resumed) → P28 → P29 → P30 → P31 → P32`.** This reverses P34's "NEXT, unblocked" status: P34's own rulings stay landed (A-242/A-243) and its carve inherits rather than makes them, but **it must not be dispatched until ship is done**, and `handoffs/README.md`'s table row now says so. A-247's stop-short is lifted: B002/B003 are cleared to implement, cmru first and zipapp as a fast-follow. Also settles the M6-position flag raised in `3-roadmap.md` — and **renumbers the roadmap's milestones so ids run in execution order**: M4 = ship (F014), M5 = SQL (F013), M6 = Go (F008), M7 unchanged. | Operator decision, and it takes the side this roadmap had already written down as the honest alternative to its own ordering: a zipapp is the near-zero-cost answer for a consumer with no installed Python manager, which is A-O04's srdm blocker, and every package merged before shipping is value no consumer can reach. What made it landable rather than another checkpoint is that A-247's two blockers turned out to be *rulings*, not unknowns — adopt cmru's orchestration, decline its build; the release manifest is authoritative over a `.sha256` sidecar. **The milestone renumber is a deliberate one-time exception to this project's own id-stability doctrine** (A-153/A-167), taken while those ids were a single commit old and cited nowhere outside `3-roadmap.md` and `2-product-definition.md`, both updated atomically here. They are identity from now on — which is exactly why P34 keeps its number while running second, and why the queue was NOT renumbered a third time. |

## Decided — session 49, 2026-08-11 (cmru + zipapp: scoped, not landed)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-247 | **cmru adoption (B002) and the parallel zipapp artifact (B003) are SCOPED AND HANDED BACK, not landed — sequence cmru first, zipapp as a fast-follow behind it.** Two rulings are owed before any edit: (1) assay adopts cmru's **orchestration** and declines its **build**, via an explicit `[project.assay.steps.build]` override, because `handlers.py::cmd_wheel_build` is bare `python -m build --wheel` with no `--require-hashes`, no `--no-build-isolation`, no committed wheelhouse and no private clone — taking the batteries-included handler would silently downgrade A-198/A-199; (2) when both a cmru `.sha256` sidecar (S1.3) and A-200's `release-manifest.json` sit on one Release, **the manifest is authoritative**, because only it can feed pip's `--require-hashes`. The zipapp needs **no new cmru artifact-type slot**: it ships as two extra assets on the wheel profile's Release via an explicit push step. | Not landed because adoption is not confined to a new project block: `cmru.toml`'s `[orchestration]` `project_order`, `default_projects` and the four `step_project_order` lists are shared by **seven other products**, and `cmru release` fast-forwards `origin/main` after running each changed project's gate in a shared release worktree — so a misdeclared assay step can fail a release that has nothing to do with assay. That is the B001 design-checkpoint shape exactly. The zipapp half is mechanically proven rather than argued: built, driven, and measured. A src-tree zipapp reports `assay 0+unknown` and **emits verdicts recording that as `assay_version`, which its own `verify` accepts** — so it must be built FROM the released wheel, where the dist-info travels inside the archive and `importlib.metadata` resolves `1.2.5`. `importlib.resources.files()` reads the packaged schema from inside the zip (`urn:assay:schema:verdict:4`, 27949 bytes, origin `/tmp/assay2.pyz/assay/__init__.py`), three real v4 artifacts verify exit 0, a v5 artifact is correctly refused, and `grep -rn __file__ src/assay/` finds only a docstring — assay is zip-safe by construction. Two naive builds differ (`2a0feb3f…` vs `6ab6ee47…`); one `find … -exec touch -t 198001010000` makes them byte-identical (`fdfbe9ef…`), so cmru's S9.4 determinism contract costs one line, but shipping without it would put a non-reproducible asset on a Release page whose premise is reproducibility. |
