| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-246 | **The Fable round-3 "Addendum 2" hygiene findings are landed: three open questions marked RESOLVED (A-O14, A-O15, A-O16), two discharged as stale-open (A-O01, A-O02), one updated-but-still-open (A-O03), one blocking-text correction (A-O09), and four stale mechanism/ownership clauses corrected in place (A-129, A-163, A-069, A-102). Plus two mechanical fixes: `errors.py`'s `SNAPSHOT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` comment no longer says "RESERVED for P22", and the duplicate "session 24" header in this file is renumbered `31b`.** Nothing was closed on Fable's word: every claim was re-verified against the shipped code and the verification is written into the row it justifies. | The rows were not merely untidy — three of them actively mislead. A-129 tells a future reader to reuse `verdict.load_schema()` in `verify.py`, which the shipped module deliberately does not do and cannot do under A-005; A-163 assigns effective-PATH reachability to a package that shipped without it, so the deferral has no owner; A-102's block-extent premise is exactly what A-217 disproved. A-O09's dead "before v1 freezes" deadline is the same class one step milder. The stale-but-harmless ones (A-O01, A-O02, A-O14, A-O15, A-O16) cost attention on every read of the Open section, which is the section a resuming session is most likely to trust. Every resolved row is kept in place with a banner rather than deleted, following A-O19's own precedent: the evidence trail is worth more than a shorter table. |

## Decided — session 47, 2026-08-11 (A-241 closed, and half-corrected)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-245 | **A-241 is fixed by PARTITIONING `_INDEPENDENT_R2_TERMINALS`, not by requiring verbatim propagation across it — and A-241's own headline example was legitimate producer output, not a bug.** New `verify._POST_BASELINE_R2_TERMINALS` = `{MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED, BASE_IS_HEAD, UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT}`: for those three, a payload-free R2 claim beside a non-PASS R0 sibling is a failure unless the sibling carries the IDENTICAL pair. The other three — `GIT_FAILED`, `DIRTY_TREE`, `HEAD_CHANGED` — stay unconstrained by the baseline, because each has a real producer path that renders it beside a baseline that did **not** pass. A-241 named `ERROR/GIT_FAILED` against an `ERROR/EXEC_FAILED` baseline as its example; that artifact is **legitimate** and tightening it would have made `assay run` emit documents `assay verify` refuses — the exact defect the independent-terminal branch was added to close. Committed `a7c16d0c`. | The partition is read off the producer, not chosen: `runner.py` puts R2 target resolution and mutation discovery inside `if r2_declared and result.outcome is Outcome.PASS`, and the not-PASS arm renders `build_mutation_claim(result, None)`, which propagates the baseline pair verbatim (A-116) — so those three reasons cannot co-occur with a failed baseline. The other three can: `_replace_highest_higher_rigor_claim_with_git_failed` leaves every lower completed claim as it was (A-193/A-194), and A-195/A-178's post-command refusal keeps the REAL R0 claim while marking every other declared level. **Both facts were established by driving `run_lane`, not by reading it** — `R0=FAIL/COMMAND_FAILED` beside `R2=ERROR/GIT_FAILED` and beside `R2=NO_MEASUREMENT/DIRTY_TREE` were each produced and round-tripped through `verify_document` returning `[]`, and both are now permanent tests. The equal-pair escape is deliberate: `_refuse_lane_with_plan` renders one pair on every declared level, which is verbatim propagation by construction, so accepting it keeps the check fail-safe against any refusal path whose reason lands in this set. Load-bearingness was proved by controlled break in both directions (empty set → exactly the three negatives red; all six → exactly the three over-tightening guards plus both producer witnesses red), per A-232. |

## Decided — session 46b, 2026-08-11 (roadmap acceptance)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-244 | **A-O06 (`fail-before/pass-after` as a computed Tier 1 method) is the accepted next capability priority, to be carved after the Go wave closes at P32. Planning acceptance only — NOT a dispatch, and it gets no package number until P32 merges.** It does not enter the P20–P34 queue, does not gate the ship milestone, and nothing in the current queue may cite it as a dependency. | Chosen over the other two v2 candidates on reuse and evidence value: A-O06 inverts machinery that already exists and is already qualified (the R3 canary's build-a-variant-commit-and-assert-the-expected-red path), so it adds a genuinely new *claim* — "this test would have caught the bug" — at close to zero new substrate. A-O05 (whole-project/sampled mutation) needs a budget decision first and A-O07 (serial/parallel parity) is cheaper but proves less. Sequencing it after P32 rather than before keeps the frozen-wave chain serial, which is the property that has been finding defects. Recorded now so that a future session picking up after the ship milestone does not re-run this comparison. |

## Decided — session 46, 2026-08-11 (Fable addendum: accepted shapes, and A-237 corrected)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-239 | **A-235's accepted shape, superseding its "two candidates".** Candidate (ii) — correction at the adapter/evaluate boundary with no shared-model change — is **rejected as information-theoretically insufficient**, not merely messier. Adopted instead, and this is what P27's re-carve starts from: (1) `go_cover.py` emits **block extents as an explicit representation** (an optional `blocks` field or sibling type) rather than pre-merging them into line sets — P27's re-carve designs it, under A-084's rule that the package proving the need owns the extension; (2) statement positions arrive through a **NEW protocol hook**, never by overloading `statement_spans`; (3) the intersection — given block extents plus statement positions, compute statement-granular line sets — lives in the **core as a pure, language-free function**, the division of labour P07 already established for spans. Built **Go-specific, not as shared infrastructure.** | (ii) fails on a case P27's own probe already proved real: a statement on a shared block boundary (the column-1 case A-218 establishes) is permanently collapsed to "executed" by `go_cover.py`'s overlap-merge **during parsing**, and no post-merge correction can recover it because the merge has already discarded the per-block column data the recovery would need. On the new hook: `statement_spans`' contract is "called ONLY for unattributed lines", frozen by A-097/A-101, which gates in the wrong direction — it rescues gaps, whereas Go needs to *demote fabrications* — and its type is line-only with no columns. On Go-specificity: there is no third consumer to amortize against. TypeScript's likely format (Istanbul) is already statement-precise and SQL has no coverage tool at all, so building this as shared infrastructure would be speculative generality. Recorded so a later reader does not re-derive the elimination. |
| A-240 | **SUPERSEDED 2026-08-11 by A-251, which CLOSED the gap this row argued to leave open. Both of this row's justifications are now on record as wrong: the original (four conformance tests) was false, and the replacement (only PASS/FAIL can violate A-116, so closing makes a real oracle unreachable) measured an OVERBROAD branch set — under the correctly-scoped four branches zero committed documents regress and the misreport oracle survives untouched. Retained in full for the trail; A-237's narrowing does NOT stand.** A-237's narrowing stands, but its stated evidence was wrong and is corrected here. The schema owns refusal of impossible payloads, not requiredness of evidence — for a measured reason, not the one originally given.** A-237 claimed `tests/test_verdict_conformance.py` carries "a family of four assertions documenting that boundary". **That claim is false.** Verified by reading all four: they defend argv arithmetic (`:485`), claims-cover-declared-rigor in both directions (`:526`, `:539`) and evidence-covers-declarations (`:560`) — three genuinely cross-object rules, none of them payload requiredness. Two of the four do not involve a payload-free claim at all, and the one that does uses it as an incidental vehicle. **The real reason is different and stronger:** `test_verify_rejects_an_r2_claim_that_misreports_its_own_failed_prerequisite` proves A-116's verbatim-propagation rule, and driving the shipped verifier across every payload-free-legal status shows the oracle fires for **none** of them — `ERROR/GIT_FAILED`, `ERROR/UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`, `ERROR/MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`, `NO_MEASUREMENT/EMPTY_COVERAGE` and `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/LANE_TIMEOUT` all pass re-derivation as independent terminals. So the only statuses that can violate A-116 on a payload-free claim are exactly PASS and FAIL — precisely what the schema branches would forbid. Closing the gap makes that oracle **structurally unreachable** and forces deleting a real test. | Fable was right about the evidence and I was wrong; the correction matters because a future reader would otherwise cite four tests that do not say what A-237 claimed. I attempted the enforcement a second time on Fable's prompting — A-236b having established that v5 can be tightened in place — got it working and schema-valid, changed the two test vehicles, and then found the cost by running the verifier rather than reasoning about it. **A new gap surfaced while checking, and is recorded rather than fixed here (A-241).** The honest summary: the boundary is right, my first justification was not, and the second attempt is what produced a justification that survives. |
| A-241 | **FIXED 2026-08-11 in `a7c16d0c` by A-245; and its own headline example was WRONG.** Retained for the evidence trail. Verifying tests: `tests/test_verify_layer_independence.py::test_a_post_baseline_only_terminal_beside_a_failing_baseline_is_rejected` (three reasons), its `..._beside_a_passing_baseline_stays_accepted` differential, `..._genuinely_baseline_independent_terminal_survives_a_failing_baseline`, `..._a_failing_lane_that_leaves_the_tree_dirty_round_trips_through_verify`, and `tests/test_runner_p23_cleanup_and_budget.py::test_a_cleanup_failure_after_a_FAILING_baseline_is_still_verify_clean`. **A-116's verbatim-propagation enforcement was PARTIAL.** A payload-free R2 claim carrying `ERROR/GIT_FAILED` against a baseline of `ERROR/EXEC_FAILED` is a verbatim-propagation violation the shipped verifier does **not** catch: it returns zero failures. Same for `ERROR/UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` and `ERROR/MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`. | Found while measuring A-240, by driving the real verifier rather than reading it. A-116 says the payload-free claim must reuse the baseline's `(outcome, reason_code)` **verbatim**; the re-derivation check treats every ERROR/NO_MEASUREMENT/BUDGET_EXCEEDED status as an independent terminal and stops, so a wrong *reason code* under a right *outcome* passes. The existing test only ever covered the PASS/FAIL slice, which is why this was invisible. Not repaired in this commit because it is a verifier behaviour change with its own oracle requirements, and bundling it into a documentation-correction commit is the un-verified-repair pattern six P33 rounds were spent removing. Recorded with its evidence so it is not re-discovered. |
| A-242 | **A-238(i) resolved: KEEP the flat seven-method `LanguageAdapter`; the five SQL-dead stubs RAISE rather than return a plausible value.** A-238 overstated the deadness — SQL genuinely needs `excluded_dir_names`, `source_globs` and `is_test_path` (plus `name`, `external_tools`, `requires_span_attribution`, `generate_mutation_sites`). Exactly five are meaningless: `has_executable_code`, `normalize_coverage_key`, `statement_spans`, `inject_import_break`, `inject_uncovered_line`, and `RegistryEntry.rigor` already makes all five provably unreachable through the CLI before any adapter method is called. **P34's carve must record one sentence distinguishing this from `GoAdapter.statement_spans` returning `None`**, which is *correct by contract* under Go's declared `requires_span_attribution = False` — a no-op, not a placeholder. **Named reopen trigger:** revisit capability-factored sub-protocols if a second R2-only language appears, or if an R1-capable language wants a genuinely different capability shape. | Raising follows the existing `_BASELINE_NEVER_READ` precedent in `verify.py`, and the reasoning transfers exactly: there is no correct return value for `has_executable_code` on SQL, so returning Go's fail-closed `True` would silently manufacture a false-FAIL path if a future wiring mistake ever made it reachable, whereas raising converts that mistake into a crash at its own site. The distinction from Go's `None` needs saying because the two patterns look identical in a diff and mean opposite things — one is the contract, the other is the absence of one. The reopen trigger makes this a decision with a condition rather than a silent default, which is what A-238 left it as. |
| A-243 | **A-238(ii) resolved: a payload-free `helpers` entry is permitted for `MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`, and for that terminal only.** Three scope guards: (1) the widening admits **only** `MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED` — `MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` (nothing was invoked) and `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL` (the helper could not have been invoked) must NOT admit a `helpers` entry, so this is not read as "failure terminals may carry helpers generally"; (2) the rule is phrased generically — *a `helpers` entry requires either a claim carrying the payload that role produces, or a claim whose terminal names that helper's own failure* — so P27's re-carve can reuse it for the `statement-positions` role without rearguing the shape; (3) both layers get the widening (model and raw verifier) and the negative gets a test in P34's own style: a `mutation-sites` helper beside `MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` must still be refused. **Needs no schema change** — the helpers-correspondence rule is cross-object and lives only in `verdict.py`/`verify.py`. **Deliberately still open:** a helper that ran successfully on a run that then failed for an unrelated reason (a deadline, say). That sub-case belongs to P34's carve looking at real SQL runs, not a blind ruling now. | The precedent is already in the codebase: `judgment.r2` is recorded for the `MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` terminal for exactly this reason — "the policy was resolved and applied before discovery returned UNSUPPORTED, so dropping it would hide what a consumer needs to interpret the refusal" (A-183). The helper case is that sentence with "policy" replaced by "helper". Without the widening, a SQL parser that fails on unparseable DDL forces the producer to omit precisely the provenance someone debugging the failure would want — and it would surface on P34's first unparseable file. Guard (1) matters because the three failure terminals are not equivalent: only one of them means the helper actually ran. |

## Decided — session 2, 2026-08-06 (from P01's pre-flight)

The P01 pre-flight returned NOT READY with three blockers and seven ambiguities,
all of them defects in the specification rather than in the plan. Every entry
below is a controller ruling closing one of them. This is the propagate step in
`WORKFLOW.md` working as intended: the cost of finding these was one orientation
turn, against an implementation plus a review plus a rework.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-048 | The five `judge` fields are **conditionally** required (R1 declared), not unconditionally. An R0-only lane has no `[judge]` table and therefore no `source_roots`/`allow_excluded`. | P01's O1 listed them in a flat required list while Work item 6 required assay's own R0 lane to omit them — the package's own deliverable was a lane its own loader had to reject. A-018's "required per-lane" meant *per-lane rather than a global CLI flag*, never "at the lane's top level". |
| A-049 | `source_roots` resolve against **the directory containing `assay.toml`** (the project root), never the repo root. | The lane file must not need to know where it sits in a monorepo, and a project vendored one level deeper must not silently start measuring nothing. Reconciling git's two spellings (`--relative` is cwd-relative, `status --porcelain` is always repo-top-relative) is the core normaliser's job. Freezes the answer for P02/P04/P08, which is why it could not stay implicit. |
| A-050 | `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`'s `reason_code` is `LANE_TIMEOUT`. The reason_code enumeration is **closed**; an implementer needing an unlisted code stops and asks. | A-022 requires `reason_code` on every non-PASS outcome, but the DESIGN-GUIDE table omitted exit 4 entirely — the only occurrence in the estate was inside P07's O1. P01 would have had to either invent an enum member or contradict its own O4. |
| A-051 | `PASS` **omits** `reason_code` rather than carrying null. | A pass has no cause to name, and `null` invites a consumer to treat absence and emptiness as different states. |
| A-052 | `budget` is **parsed** at load, not merely checked for presence. | A malformed duration discovered at run time in P07 is a failure the config layer could have caught, which is the whole argument for a strict loader. |
| A-053 | Closed vocabularies for `scope` (S0–S4), `rigor` (R0–R3), `enforcement` (`gate`/`advisory`). | Neither `enforcement` nor the ladders had a stated vocabulary anywhere in assay's own docs; a loader cannot reject what was never enumerated. The ladders come from TESTING-METHODOLOGY §Axis 1/2 and are now cited in DESIGN-GUIDE §12. |
| A-054 | `assay lanes` renders NO verdict artifact. It raises a typed error carrying the reason code; the CLI maps it to an exit code. Artifact emission belongs entirely to P07. | A-027 ("emitted on every outcome") governs a lane RUN. `assay lanes` does not run a lane, and A-028 makes emission conditional on an explicit path that `assay lanes` has no flag for. |
| A-055 | P01 defines the claim **envelope** only (`source: computed\|adjudicated\|attested`, `status`, `verified_by_assay`); kind-specific payloads are P09's. | P09's `scope.touch` already includes `verdict.py` and `config.py`, so the two packages were about to design the same shape twice. The envelope is what all three tiers share and what the schema needs on day one; the payloads are not. |
| A-056 | `jsonschema` is added to the **test** extra. A-005 constrains RUNTIME dependencies only. | O4 needs a validator, `jsonschema 4.26.0` is already in `tester-unified:local`, and hand-rolling one would mean the test proves the hand-rolled checker rather than the schema. |
| A-057 | `pyproject.toml` sets `fallback_version` in `[tool.setuptools_scm]`, and `.gitignore` covers build residue. | Verified empirically in the gate image: with `root = ".."` and no `assay-v*` tag in the repo, installing from a copied tree fails with `LookupError: setuptools-scm was unable to detect version`, and installing in place writes `build/` and `src/assay.egg-info/` into the bind-mounted worktree — which would interact with P02's DIRTY_TREE tests and P11's self-hosting. |
| A-058 | Handoff `tier` is `implement-2` throughout. Which MODEL is dispatched is `WORKFLOW.md`'s table, not the tier field. | `sonnet5-high` is a **stale** tier name — the B16 rename moved the ladder to capability bands (`implement-1`, `implement-2`, `implement-1-free`, `review-3`), and `routes.host.toml` has no Opus *implementation* band at all (`claude-opus-high` sits under `review-3`, which is review and carve authority). Naming a band that does not exist, or borrowing a review band for implementation, would both be worse than stating the band honestly and letting the dispatcher record the model. |
| A-059 | P01 is **split** into P01a (config loader + CLI) and P01b (verdict model + JSON Schema). | Estimated at ~410–520 changed executable lines, at or over the series' ~400 carving limit, and the two halves share no code. More importantly they have different claims to attack: "does the config contract refuse to invent?" and "does the schema REJECT a malformed verdict?" — and the second is where the package's hollowness risk concentrates. |
| A-060 | Static dependency-purity checks are made by **AST walk** against `sys.stdlib_module_names`, never by grep. | P01's O5 grep was verified VACUOUS: `grep -rn` prefixes every line with a path containing "assay", and the `-v` alternation contains `assay`, so every line was filtered regardless of content. It passed clean on a file importing `requests`, `flask` and a function-level `boto3`. Two further defects even once the path issue is fixed: the `re` alternative matches any line containing that substring, and `^import` misses indented imports. An oracle that cannot fail is worse than no oracle. |

## Decided — session 3, 2026-08-06 (ratified from P01a's implementation and brief)

Batched deliberately. `decisions.md` is read inside the orientation snapshot, so
every edit here invalidates it; rulings therefore accumulate in the brief chain
and land together at a rebuild point. This is the first such point — the
snapshot does not exist yet, so applying them now is free.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-061 | Judge requirements are **per declared rigor level, not a cumulative ladder**. R2 and R3 additionally require `language` + `source_roots`. | `rigor` is a LIST of independent methods, and §6 gives one claim per declared level — so an R3-without-R2 lane must not be forced to declare mutation config it never runs. Requiring a fact nothing reads is the same hazard as inventing one. Ratifies P01a's reading; `JUDGE_FIELDS_BY_RIGOR` is the implementation. |
| A-062 | **Judge config for an UNDECLARED rigor level is refused.** | Overrides P01a's first reading, which allowed it on the grounds that the rigor list is the claim so surplus config claims nothing. True of the data, false of the reader: `fail_under = 100` on an R0 lane reads exactly like a coverage floor and is not one — nothing consumes it, so if it is wrong nothing fails (§4.2a). This project's subject *is* a declaration implying absent capability; dstdns's five-lane table over two real lanes is the specimen and this is its field-level twin. Also aligns the loader with §12, which already said an R0-only lane "has no `[judge]` table at all". |
| A-063 | An **empty** `[judge]` table is the only one an R0 lane may carry. | The exact boundary A-062 draws: an empty table declares nothing, so there is nothing surplus to refuse. Found while closing A-062's branches, having first been dismissed as a harmless edge. |
| A-064 | The loader additionally refuses: empty `rigor`, a duplicated rigor level, empty `argv`, empty `source_roots`, an absolute `source_root`, a file with no lanes, and unknown keys in a lane or `judge` table. `where`, `mutation` and `canary` sub-table CONTENTS stay opaque. | All correct and all previously uncited, so a later package could have diverged. The opacity carve-out is §7 holding: `where` is data assay parses and never interprets, and `mutation`/`canary` internals belong to P10/P08. |
| A-065 | `schema_version` is required in `assay.toml` and must equal `LANE_SCHEMA_VERSION` (1). It is **distinct** from the verdict artifact's own `schema_version`. | Two independently versioned contracts. Conflating them would couple a lane-file format change to every emitted artifact. |
| A-066 | **House style, binding on every later package**: `errors.py` owns `Outcome`, `ReasonCode`, `EXIT_CODES`, `REASON_CODES` — import them, never redefine. One test module per oracle, its docstring naming the oracle *and* the negative it defends. | P01a set these as the first package; nine follow, and consistency is cheaper to mandate than to retrofit. `errors.py` imports nothing, so anything may depend on it. |
| A-067 | **A test that proves a property must itself be checked by breaking the property.** | P01a's own dependency-purity test was partially vacuous — it installed with a `PYTHONPATH` exposing host site-packages to pip's resolver, so declaring `requests>=2` still installed clean. That is A-060 one level up, found only by mutating the thing the test existed to catch. Generalised here because A-060 was written about one grep and this is the class. Also: compare round-trips against an INDEPENDENT parser (`tomllib`), never against assay's own. |
| A-068 | **P03 owes the `judge.coverage.format` cross-check.** The loader accepts any non-empty string. | The vocabulary is "a key the parser registry knows" and the registry is P03's. Hardcoding a format list in the loader would recreate the four-copies divergence one layer down. Recorded as a debt rather than left as a hope. |
| A-069 | A-057's `fallback_version` is **declared but unexercised**, and built wheels version as `0.0.0`. | `setuptools_scm` is present in no interpreter in `tester-unified:local` (only `setuptools`, and only under `sys.base_prefix`), so the plugin never loads and the `LookupError` A-057 records is not reproduced by the suite. P11's scratch-venv proof depends on this; it is a known gap, not a surprise to discover there. **QUALIFIED 2026-08-11 by A-246: this now describes ONLY the standalone-fixture path.** A-198/A-199 gave gate and release builds a real hash-bound `setuptools-scm` closure, so a clean tag builds `1.2.3`, a mutated tracked tree builds a distinct `.dev…+g….d…` identity, the untagged monorepo builds a non-placeholder SCM development identity, and the no-VCS witness exercises `fallback_version` explicitly — the "declared but unexercised" and "wheels version as 0.0.0" claims are both false there. They remain true where `setuptools_scm` is genuinely absent, which is the gate image itself: `tests/test_standalone.py:142` skips exactly on `find_spec("setuptools_scm") is not None` and its own comment scopes the `0.0.0` fallback to that case. |
| A-070 | Offline install in the gate image is **two steps in two environments**: `pip wheel --no-build-isolation --no-deps` with `PYTHONPATH=<setuptools dir>`, then `pip install --no-index <wheel>` with a CLEAN environment. | Putting that `PYTHONPATH` on the *install* leaks host site-packages into pip's resolver, which is precisely how A-067's vacuity happened. Recorded because P11 must repeat this exactly. |

| A-071 | **P04, P05, P08 and P10 each own their claim payload**: `src/assay/verdict.py` and `src/assay/schemas/**` are in their `scope.touch`, added as ADDITIVE branches following the pattern P01b states in the schema's own `$comment`. Widening the claim object to `additionalProperties: true` is forbidden — it deletes P01b's guard. | Found by P01b's readiness pass: only P01b and P09 could touch those files, while four later packages need fields there (P02 explicitly *forbids* `verdict.py`). Supersedes A-055's "payloads are P09's" — P09 owns attestation and adjudication, not coverage. A package that adds a rigor method should own its claim payload; forbidding that forces either speculative up-front design or a permissive schema, and both are worse. |
| A-072 | **A ruling delivered only in an agent message is not applied.** Every ruling lands in `decisions.md` and, where it changes scope or oracles, in the handoff files, before the next dispatch. | A-071 was ruled in P01b's go-message and reached exactly one agent; the four handoffs it governs were never edited, and the defect would have resurfaced at P04 as if it had never been diagnosed. Batching ratifications is correct; *deferring* them to a message is not the same thing, and the difference was invisible until checked. |

## Decided — session 4, 2026-08-07 (P01c contract repair)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-073 | An ordinary declared command that runs and exits non-zero is `FAIL/COMMAND_FAILED`; `EXEC_FAILED` remains inability to start the process. | R0 is itself a judged rigor claim: the command ran but did not pass. `TESTS_FAILED` would falsely make assay a test runner even though argv may name any tool. Supersedes A-050's closed reason set by adding the missing cause; A-021's six outcomes and exit codes do not change. |
| A-074 | Verdict schema v2 separates computed `claims[]` from sibling `declared_evidence[]` / `evidence[]`, whose exact identity is `(source, key)`. | Provenance (computed/adjudicated/attested) and rigor (R0-R3) are orthogonal. This supersedes A-024's claim that Tier 2/3 live in rigor-keyed claims and the remaining shared-envelope part of A-055. A-032's three tiers and A-024's exact coverage rule survive on their correct axes. |
| A-075 | An attested commit may equal the commit under test or be its ancestor. Ancestor evidence is current only when every declared reviewed path is byte-identical; changed reviewed paths render `NO_MEASUREMENT/STALE_ATTESTATION`, and absence renders `NO_MEASUREMENT/MISSING_ATTESTATION`. | Exact equality made staleness unreachable: there can be no changed interval after an attestation that is required to name HEAD. Assay records the evidence obtained and can reject stale evidence without pretending to judge the review. |
| A-076 | A bare `argv[0]` requires `PATH` in lane `env` or `env_passthrough`; an argv containing `/` does not. | `subprocess` with `env={}` may still resolve a bare executable through an implementation default. Without load-time rejection the lane depends on a fact it never declared, contradicting A-019. Assay's own lane explicitly passes PATH through. |
| A-077 | Assay's nyxloom gate reads `CGROUP_PARENT_DEV_BACKGROUND` and verifies the named host cgroup has a configured resource knob before launching Docker; unset, missing and unlimited/transient-looking slices refuse. | A hardcoded slice shadows the estate's authoritative environment, while an unknown slice name fails open into an unlimited transient unit. The verifier follows srdm's host-cgroup probe and has no slice-name fallback. |
| A-078 | Tier 2's sibling evidence shape is reserved in schema v2, but no adjudicator registry or unknown-adjudicator oracle ships until the first real integration. | A zero-integration registry would be decorative and could only validate its own empty set. The stable `(source, key)` envelope makes the first integration additive without claiming capability that does not exist. Supersedes the mechanism implied by A-034 while preserving its schema-compatibility goal. |

## Decided — session 5, 2026-08-07 (adversarial re-carve)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-079 | The P02–P11 handoffs at input `d87f028b` are withdrawn and superseded by P02–P14 at repaired input `9bd7d206`. | The prior series contained confirmed unfalsifiable/hollow oracles, scope contradictions, missing dependencies, stale references, and bundled claims. Dispatching it would knowingly waste implementation work on the pre-v2 contract. |
| A-080 | Independent expected-verdict conformance begins with the first producer in P04 and is an obligation of every later package that adds a producer branch; P14 audits completeness rather than introducing the discipline. | Deferring A-041 would leave most of the series protected only by assay's own model. Before P04, domain components do not emit verdicts and instead use independent literal input/output fixtures. |
| A-081 | Canary construction and execution remain one package, and success requires a passing control plus a valid known-bad case rejected for the configured cause. | These are two observations of one attackable claim. Splitting them produces two hollow halves; accepting arbitrary failure permits syntax/config breakage to masquerade as gate integrity. |
| A-082 | Mutation construction and execution are separate claims. The `jobs` bound is proved without wall-clock timing at the executor boundary: the actual executor factory must receive `max_workers=jobs` and all work must be submitted through it. | A valid mutant can exist without being run honestly, while a runner cannot repair an invalid experiment. Serial/parallel equality alone does not prove the cap; timing is machine-dependent. The stdlib executor owns the runtime concurrency guarantee once assay demonstrably supplies the declared bound. |
| A-083 | Standalone packaging and self-hosted conformance are separate packages. | A clean wheel can work while self-gating is circular, and a source checkout can self-test while the wheel is unusable. Each has an independent negative and distinct allowed files. |
| A-084 | The adapter protocol may gain statement-span, canary, and mutation capability only in the package that first proves each need; adding Go after spans must not edit the base protocol or evaluator. | Speculating all capabilities up front makes structural tests decorative. A fake adapter proves the initial language-free boundary; Python proves fidelity; Go is the first mechanical additivity test. Canary injection and mutation are capability-specific operations, not evaluation policy. |
| A-085 | P10 loads only attested evidence into the schema-v2 sibling arrays; adjudicated evidence remains reserved with no registry or loader. | Preserves A-034's additive consumer shape and A-078's refusal to ship an unattackable zero-integration mechanism. |
| A-086 | P08 adds `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL` to the NO_MEASUREMENT reason set when it first ships an adapter with a declared parser prerequisite. | DESIGN-GUIDE §11 already requires a missing adapter tool to be NO_MEASUREMENT, but the closed A-050/A-073 vocabulary has no truthful cause for it. `EMPTY_COVERAGE` and `EXEC_FAILED` would both lie. The additive reason and its independent full artifact belong to the package that first makes the state reachable. |
| A-087 | **Supersedes A-086 before dispatch:** P08 ships no external Go prerequisite. Its narrow `has_executable_code` lexer/parser recognizes function bodies and returns strict `true` on malformed or unknown input; it is proven from committed text with no Go toolchain. | A packaged `go run` helper would be uncompiled and therefore unproved in tester-unified, merely moving the gap. The semantic question is much narrower than compiling Go and can be parsed conservatively: false is returned only for confidently function-body-free files. No missing-tool state becomes reachable, so adding its reason would be decorative. |
| A-088 | The two landed packages are renamed **`assay-P01a` → `assay-P00`** and **`assay-P01b` → `assay-P01`** (handoffs, LOGs, BRIEFs, and every `depends_on`). Confirmed real, natural "unattributable" trigger exists (a changed interior line whose enclosing span is itself untracked, dstdns's own documented case) — only "overlap" needed this clarification. |

Not landing it now would mean P12's own implementer re-discovers the same wrong-citation defect from scratch. |
| A-114 | **`generate_mutants`'s return contract is pinned**: `tuple[Mutant, ...] \| Literal["UNSUPPORTED"]`, a WHOLE-ADAPTER-CALL-level union exactly as DESIGN-GUIDE §11 sketches (`generate_mutants(text, lines) -> mutants \| UNSUPPORTED`) — never a per-construct union. `GoAdapter.generate_mutants` returns `UNSUPPORTED` unconditionally (Work item 1, A-042 — no Go toolchain exists to prove syntax validity). `PythonAdapter.generate_mutants` returns `UNSUPPORTED` only when `text` fails `ast.parse` (mirrors `statement_spans`'s own existing `SyntaxError`/`ValueError` → `None` precedent, `adapters/python.py`'s `_statement_spans`, A-101) — never for an individual unsupported construct within an otherwise-parseable file, which simply contributes zero mutants for that site rather than aborting the whole call. A new frozen `kw_only` dataclass `Mutant` (A-092) lives in `mutation.py` (`verdict.py` is forbidden): at minimum `lineno: int`, `operator: str` (validated in `__post_init__` against the closed four-value catalogue — plain `str` with closed-set validation, not an enum, matching `CanaryResult.mechanism`'s own precedent for a package-local closed vocabulary), `description: str`, `mutated_text: str` (the FULL text with exactly one construct changed — text, not a file write, per the adapter surface's "pure where it can be" framing), plus a derived `identity` property (mirroring `EvidenceDeclaration.identity`/`Evidence.identity`, P10) for O1's "stable identity" requirement, rather than a separately stored field. Work item 2's "explicit ineligible/ambiguous reasons" is satisfied at the TEST layer, not the public return contract: `generate_mutants` never returns an entry for a site it excludes, and O3's "independent expected manifest" is a hand-written fixture enumerating the exact expected `Mutant` identities for a literal source file (A-041/A-080 pattern) — removing an eligibility filter is caught because it adds an identity absent from that fixture, not because ineligibility itself must be an observable runtime value. An implementer wanting direct unit coverage of eligibility logic should test a private per-site classifier function directly (P10's own precedent: `_check_ancestor_or_equal` imported and tested directly), not expose a public type for it. | The handoff's own Work item 2 ("stable mutant identities and explicit ineligible/ambiguous reasons") and O3 ("independent expected manifest" catching a removed eligibility filter) both imply finer granularity than DESIGN-GUIDE §11's binary sketch alone pins, and `verdict.py` being forbidden means whatever new type this needs has no existing home — left unresolved, two implementers would plausibly diverge on whether `UNSUPPORTED` applies per-construct or per-call, and on whether ineligibility needs its own public shape, the same class of gap A-101 (`StatementSpan`) and A-108 (`CanaryResult`) already closed twice each. `ast.parse` failure was independently confirmed as `python.py`'s existing precedent for "cannot analyze this text" (not a new invention). |
| A-115 | **`boolop-swap` targets ONE operator TOKEN at a time, never `ast.BoolOp.op` wholesale.** Verified empirically: `a and b and c` parses as a single `BoolOp(op=And(), values=[a, b, c])` — one AST node for a chain of any length, but the chain corresponds to `len(values) - 1` separate textual `and`/`or` occurrences. A chain of N operands therefore has N-1 independently-targetable sites, each its own `Mutant` with its own identity, flipping exactly one token and leaving every other token in the chain untouched. Nyxloom's own `.op` reassignment (safe only because it reprints the WHOLE node via `ast.unparse`, per A-112) cannot be ported as-is once the operation is a byte-exact splice (A-114) — reassigning `.op` on the shared node would conceptually flip every occurrence in the chain at once. Flagged for the implementer, not itself ruled on (ordinary, non-chained `Compare` nodes have exactly one operator so this rarely arises in practice): `ast.cmpop` nodes (`Lt`, `Eq`, ...) carry no `lineno`/`col_offset` of their own — verified empirically (`dir(ast.Lt())` has no position attributes) — so `compare-swap`'s byte span must be derived from the gap between the left operand's `end_col_offset` and the first comparator's `col_offset`, then the exact operator substring located within that gap. | Direct consequence of A-114's byte-splice requirement colliding with how CPython's own `ast.BoolOp` models a chain (one node, shared `.op`, for any chain length ≥ 2) — verified directly rather than assumed, since nyxloom's reference implementation never had to solve this (its whole-file `ast.unparse` reprint makes the ambiguity invisible). Left unruled, "one construct" is genuinely undefined for any 3+-operand boolean chain, which real Python source uses often enough (`if a and b and c:`) that a fixture exercising it is likely, not a corner case. |

## Decided — session 16, 2026-08-07 (P12 readiness pass)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-116 | **The R2 claim payload is two new frozen `kw_only` dataclasses in `verdict.py`**: `MutantOutcome` (`path: str`, `lineno: int`, `operator: str`, `description: str` — the lightweight, artifact-safe projection of `Mutant.identity`'s first three fields plus the external `path` context `Mutant` itself has no field for; deliberately WITHOUT `mutated_text`, since embedding full file content per survivor would make a verdict artifact carrying many survivors unusably large) and `Mutation` (`total: int`, `killed: int`, `survived: tuple[MutantOutcome, ...]`, `crashed: tuple[MutantOutcome, ...]`, `budget_exceeded: tuple[MutantOutcome, ...]`, with `__post_init__` enforcing `total == killed + len(survived) + len(crashed) + len(budget_exceeded)` and non-negativity — FOUR per-mutant buckets, not three, per O3's own explicit "killed, survived, crashed, and budget-stopped" and O4's "Killed, survived, error, and budget-exhausted... sets" enumerations). `Claim.mutation: Mutation \| None = None`, gated to `rigor == "R2"` in `__post_init__` exactly like `coverage`/`canary`. Schema gets a third `allOf` branch (`$defs/mutation`, `if rigor != R2 then not required mutation`) mirroring R1/R3's own branches (`schemas/verdict.schema.json` lines ~238-239) — `Claim.__post_init__` validation alone does not suffice, per this project's standing "two independently-verified layers" discipline (A-071, reconfirmed at every R1/R3 payload addition since). `Claim.mutation`'s PRESENCE is baseline-conditional, not derivable purely from `(outcome, reason_code)`: `None` means mutation testing never started (baseline itself never resolved to `PASS`); present (with `total >= 0` and possibly a non-empty `crashed`/`budget_exceeded` bucket) means it did — the SAME "absent means unknowable, empty means known-and-empty" discipline A-025 already applies elsewhere, one level up (at claim-payload presence rather than within-payload fields). This means two claims can both render `(ERROR, EXEC_FAILED)` — one with `mutation=None` (baseline crashed), one with `mutation` present and a non-empty `crashed` bucket (some individual mutants crashed) — and that is CORRECT, not an ambiguity needing a new `ReasonCode`: the reason code names the outcome CLASS, the payload (when present) carries the specific mechanism, exactly how `Evidence`/`ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` (P10) already covers several distinct causes under one code. | The readiness pass's own proposal (modeled on `Coverage`'s aggregate-plus-detail split, A-096) was directionally right but needed the bucket count corrected against the oracles' own explicit four-way language, and needed the presence-ambiguity question resolved rather than left as an open `escalate_if` candidate — resolving it via the already-established A-025 pattern avoids inventing a new `ReasonCode` in a file (`errors.py`) forbidden to this package. Cross-checked against nyxloom's own `MutationResult` (`total`/`killed`/`survivors: list[tuple[path, lineno, description]]`): insufficient as a direct copy — it has no `crashed`/`budget_exceeded` buckets at all (confirmed reading `mutation_gate.py` directly: `_run_is_killed` treats ANY non-zero exit as "killed" and sets no subprocess timeout, collapsing exactly the distinctions O3/O4 require — not to be ported, see A-122). |
| A-117 | **`judge_mutation`'s outcome/reason-code mapping, using only already-existing `ReasonCode`s** (no new code needed, `errors.py` stays forbidden): baseline non-`PASS` reuses `execute_command`'s own `(outcome, reason_code)` verbatim, `Claim.mutation` omitted; `mutation.total == 0` → `INCONCLUSIVE`/`NO_MUTANTS`; else if `mutation.crashed` is non-empty → `ERROR`/`EXEC_FAILED`; else if `mutation.budget_exceeded` is non-empty → `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`/`LANE_TIMEOUT`; else if `mutation.survived` is non-empty → `FAIL`/`MUTANTS_SURVIVED`; else `PASS`. | This precedence order (crashed > budget_exceeded > survived) is not arbitrary — it matches the existing, already-ratified `ROLLUP_PRECEDENCE` (`ERROR > NO_MEASUREMENT > BUDGET_EXCEEDED > FAIL > INCONCLUSIVE`) applied at the per-claim level exactly as it already applies across claims, so a reviewer who knows one already knows the other. Left unruled, an implementer would need to invent this ordering (or the bucket set) from scratch, risking divergence from the cross-claim rollup's own established severity ordering. |
| A-118 | **A-O05 (open questions table) is UNCHANGED — it names a genuinely different, larger, still-deferred v2+ feature** (whole-project/sampled mutation, needing its own budget/sampling config nothing currently declares) **and does not block P12.** P12's own "budget-exhausted"/"crash, or timeout" oracle language (O1's baseline half, O3's per-mutant half) is fully satisfied by reusing `execute_command`'s EXISTING `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`/`LANE_TIMEOUT` machinery, at both the baseline call and every per-mutant call (A-119) — no new aggregate budget/cap config field, no `unattempted` bucket in the payload, since P12's mutant set is already changed-lines-scoped (bounded by the diff P11 already narrowed to, never sampled). Also corrected: the handoff's "Context to read first" item 1 names a "mutation execution/budget section" of DESIGN-GUIDE that does not exist as a titled heading — the actual content spans §6 (`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`), §11 (the adapter surface plus the `judge.mutation` TOML fragment), and §12 (R2 requiring `judge.mutation`). | A-082 (session 5) already pulled the `--jobs` CAP into v1 scope for P12 — only A-O05's OTHER two items ("whole-project/sampled mutation", "budget-exhausted reporting" in that whole-project context) remain genuinely open, and neither is P12's job: P12 mutates CHANGED lines only, an inherently small, already-bounded set, with no sampling concept anywhere in its own oracles. Confirmed by reading DESIGN-GUIDE's own worked `judge.mutation` TOML example directly: no budget/cap/count/sample field appears anywhere in it. |
| A-119 | **Both the baseline run and every per-mutant run call `runner.execute_command` UNMODIFIED** (confirmed it already supports the needed injection points: `cwd: Path` required, `process_runner: ProcessRunner = default_process_runner` and `clock: Clock = _utc_now` both injectable) — never a second, hand-rolled subprocess-invocation path. Only `cwd` varies between the baseline call (`cwd=project_root`) and each mutant call (`cwd=<its own scratch copy>`, A-120); `cwd` is not part of `argv`, so this cannot affect O5's "declared argv byte-for-byte" requirement. The new execution/orchestration logic (executor-factory injection, per-mutant copy-and-run, result aggregation) lives in `mutation.py` beside `Mutant` — mirroring `canary.py`'s own precedent of holding one claim-tier's entire orchestration in a single dedicated module — while `runner.py` gains only the R2 wiring into `assemble_verdict` (a fourth optional parameter alongside `evidence`/`declared_evidence`, A-110/A-111), matching how it already wires those through rather than owning their internals. | Reusing the real execution boundary rather than reimplementing it closes the exact risk the readiness pass flagged: two independently-written "run a test command" code paths (one via `execute_command`, one bespoke for mutants) drifting apart on env handling, the append-permission gate, or timeout semantics. `execute_command`'s signature was read directly, not assumed, before writing this ruling. |
| A-120 | **Per-mutant isolation is copy-per-mutant**: `shutil.copytree` the project tree into a fresh scratch directory per mutant (mirroring AUTHORING §3b.B's already-established "fresh `tmp_path` per test" house pattern, and `tests/conftest.py`'s own existing `standalone` fixture's `shutil.copytree` precedent), write `mutant.mutated_text` over the target file INSIDE the copy, run `execute_command(..., cwd=<copy>)`, discard the copy afterward — never in-place-with-a-lock (real, unneeded serialization risk), never a `git worktree`-per-mutant (real, unneeded git-admin-locking complexity, confirmed present in nyxloom's own reference: `_WORKTREE_ADMIN_LOCK` plus a documented "~2.5% intermittent leftover-worktree defect" from an earlier version lacking that lock). O3's "byte-exact restoration" is satisfied BY CONSTRUCTION — the real/shared source tree is never written to at all — and its own oracle should be read as "the shared source's bytes are provably unchanged after every terminal case" rather than "prove a write-then-restore round-trip is exact." | Neither of nyxloom's own two strategies (`_run_is_killed_in_place`'s process-wide lock, which fakes concurrency at the executor-submission layer only; `_run_is_killed_isolated`'s per-mutant `git worktree`, which needs its own admin locking and has a documented historical defect) is necessary once this project's own existing fixture convention (fresh, disposable, independently-owned scratch state per unit of work) is applied one level down from "per test" to "per mutant run." Copy-based isolation also sidesteps a real, unaddressed question the oracles never name: if the live `project_root` were mutated in place, a dirty working tree (uncommitted user edits) would make "restore" ambiguous or destructive — moot once the real tree is never touched. |
| A-121 | **`config.py` is removed from P12's `scope.touch`** (same class of over-scoping A-106/A-111 already found twice). `jobs: int` (and no operator-filter parameter — see below) is a DIRECT parameter to P12's new mutation-execution entry point, not sourced from `assay.toml`/`JudgeConfig.mutation` — matching the established "caller supplies it directly" pattern. `judge.mutation`'s TOML shape (`jobs`, `operators`) stays unwired/aspirational, exactly like `judge.canary`'s and P10's evidence declarations already are — full `assay.toml`/CLI wiring across rigor levels remains entirely P14's job (STATE.md's own standing note). **No operator-filter parameter is added in P12 either**: none of O1-O5 exercises selecting a subset of the four mutation operators, and adding one now — even unwired — would be exactly the "capability with no scoped consumer" pattern A-090/A-093/A-094 already forbid at three earlier package seams. `judge.mutation.operators` in DESIGN-GUIDE's own worked TOML example remains aspirational until a future package (most plausibly P14, or a v2 package) both reads it AND has something that consumes a filtered operator set. | `JudgeConfig.mutation: Mapping[str, Any] \| None` is confirmed already reserved and fully opaque (`config.py`'s own `_as_opaque_table` call verifies only that it is a table) — no decision ever specifies P12 needs to parse its contents, and none of O1-O5 requires `assay.toml` round-tripping to be exercised. Leaving `operators` unconsumed is not a new defect this package introduces; it is the same, already-accepted deferred-wiring shape every other `judge.*` opaque sub-table already has today. |
| A-122 | **The executor's `max_workers` bound must come from a caller-supplied `jobs: int`, never a machine-derived heuristic** (nyxloom's own `mutation_gate.evaluate`'s `max(1, (os.cpu_count() or 2) - 2)` is confirmed present and MUST NOT be ported) — this is A-082's own "the actual executor factory must receive `max_workers=jobs`" ruling, restated because the cited reference implementation itself violates it. Additionally, confirmed directly: nyxloom's `_run_is_killed`/`_run_is_killed_in_place`/`_run_is_killed_isolated` never set a subprocess `timeout` and treat ANY non-zero exit as "killed" (`True`), collapsing a genuine kill, a crash, and a hang into one bucket — this MUST NOT be ported either; P12's own per-mutant run must apply the lane's own timeout via `execute_command`'s existing mechanism and produce the four distinct terminal classes A-116/A-117 name (killed / survived / crashed / budget-exceeded), never collapsing any pair of them. | Both facts were verified by reading `mutation_gate.py` directly, not assumed from its docstring summary — the reusable part of the reference implementation (A-113) is strictly the fan-out/ordering discipline, and this decision closes the two remaining traps in it that A-113 did not itself call out: the executor cap is machine-derived rather than declared, and per-mutant execution has no timeout and no crash/kill distinction at all. Landing this explicitly now avoids an implementer skimming the reference file and copying either line by habit, the same failure mode A-112 already prevented once for the substitution mechanism. |
