## Decided — session 18, 2026-08-07 (P14 readiness pass, the final package)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-128 | **O1's audit is narrowed to what a complete `Verdict` artifact can actually represent.** `ERROR`/`GIT_FAILED` and CLAIM-level `ERROR`/`FORMAT_MISMATCH`/`ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` are excluded from the fixture-matrix audit — confirmed, by reading `runner.py` and `cli.py` directly, that these are structurally unreachable as complete artifacts by DELIBERATE, PRE-EXISTING design: `evaluate_r1`'s own docstring states a genuinely broken coverage artifact or a git failure "is a structural failure, not a judged outcome, and propagates uncaught," and `cli.py main()`'s only exception handler (`except AssayError as exc: print(...); return exc.exit_code`) exits with the raised error's own exit code WITHOUT ever calling `assemble_verdict`/`write_verdict` — no verdict JSON is ever emitted for these three claim-shaped cases, so no "complete artifact" can exist to fixture. This is accepted, documented behavior, not a defect P14 introduces or must repair (matching the handoff's own "an upstream package is incomplete and must be repaired there" framing — repairing it would mean changing `evaluate_r1`, which stays forbidden per A-132). **`ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` is DIFFERENT and must be closed**: the identical `ReasonCode` IS reachable as a complete `Verdict` via the EVIDENCE array (P10's attested-evidence staleness, A-110 — already proven end-to-end, `tests/test_runner_assemble_verdict_evidence.py` already builds a full `Verdict` with an `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` evidence entry), and no fixture for that shape currently exists among the 33 already committed. Add one, mirroring the existing `evidence_*.json` fixtures. | Verified directly rather than assumed: ran `errors.py`'s `REASON_CODES` to confirm the exact closed 19-pair set, listed all 33 existing fixtures and cross-referenced every pair, then read `evaluate_r1`'s docstring and `cli.py main()`'s exception handling directly to confirm WHY the three gaps exist rather than guessing they were simply forgotten. Silently fixturing an artifact shape no producer code path can actually emit would itself be a defect (AUTHORING §3b.C's "no hollow tests" extends to hollow fixtures); silently ignoring the audit's own explicit claim ("every legal producer outcome/reason pair shipped by P04–P12") without saying so would repeat A-072's own class of mistake at the requirements level instead of the ruling level. |
| A-129 | **`assay verify`'s shape is an ARTIFACT VALIDATOR, matching the handoff's own O2/O3 text exactly — not the lane-canary runner `nyxloom-trove/STATE.md`'s "Longer-lived notes" describes** (`assay verify --lane X` producing an R3 canary result about a named lane). That note is stale, pre-implementation planning text and is superseded by this ruling. Concrete contract: `assay verify <path>` (or `-` for stdin, matching `--verdict-json`'s own convention) reads a verdict-JSON artifact, validates it against packaged schema v2 (reusing `verdict.load_schema()`, imported not reimplemented), then checks what JSON Schema 2020-12 cannot express: outcome-agrees-with-rollup (reusing the existing, PUBLIC `rollup()` function against the artifact's own `claims[]`/`evidence[]` statuses — `verdict.py` is forbidden to EDIT, not forbidden to IMPORT from), `argv_effective == argv_declared + argv_appended`, claims-cover-declared-rigor exactly, evidence-covers-declared-evidence exactly. Exit 0 only when BOTH schema-valid AND internally consistent; any rejection (malformed JSON, schema violation, unknown key, wrong rollup, coverage mismatch) is non-zero, with the specific failure(s) reported on stderr. | Two authoritative-looking sources described genuinely different tools with different argv shapes and different underlying mechanisms (a lane-canary runner vs. a JSON artifact validator) — left unresolved, an implementer would have to guess which one the four oracles actually need, and guessing wrong wastes the whole implementation cycle on the LAST package in the series. The handoff's own O2 text ("accepts every valid independent artifact... rejects malformed/unknown-key/wrong-rollup artifacts") is unambiguous, specific, and the most recently authored of the two sources (the same adversarial re-carve session that fixed everything else in this project) — it wins. **MECHANISM CLAUSE CORRECTED 2026-08-11 by A-246. The contract half — artifact validator, not lane-canary runner — STANDS. The clause "validates it against packaged schema v2 (reusing `verdict.load_schema()`, imported not reimplemented)" is superseded by the implementation: `src/assay/verify.py`'s own docstring states `load_schema()` is deliberately NOT called, because conformance is dataclass RECONSTRUCTION (A-005 ships zero runtime deps, so no Draft 2020-12 evaluator exists at runtime for a raw schema document to be fed to). Recorded by id so nobody "restores" `load_schema()` use in `verify.py` citing this row in good faith.** |
| A-130 | **The self-hosting mechanism (O3) is pinned.** `nyxloom-trove/nyxloom.toml`'s gate command is restructured to, inside the SAME `docker run`, build and install a real wheel FIRST (reusing A-070/A-123's two-environment recipe exactly: `pip wheel --no-build-isolation --no-deps` with `PYTHONPATH` pointed at an importable `setuptools`, then `pip install --no-index` into a scratch venv with a clean environment), prepend that venv's `bin/` to `PATH`, then invoke `assay run tester-unified --verdict-json <path>` using THAT wheel-installed `assay` — never `/opt/tester-venv`'s ambient copy — against `{worktree}/assay`'s own checkout. `assay.toml`'s `[lanes.tester-unified]` env table DROPS `PYTHONPATH = "src"` (keeping it would re-shadow the installed wheel on `sys.path` and defeat the entire point) — `argv[0]="python"` then resolves via the already-declared `env_passthrough=["PATH"]` to the scratch venv's own interpreter, so the pytest run genuinely imports the WHEEL-installed `assay`, no PYTHONPATH trick needed. A SEPARATE, independently-invoked step — `tests/test_self_hosting.py`, run via `/opt/tester-venv`'s OWN pytest (the ambient gate interpreter, never the scratch venv) — reads the just-emitted verdict.json and validates it independently (schema validity plus non-schema checks, e.g. "commit matches known HEAD," "lane == tester-unified"). This independent test, NOT `assay verify`, is O3's real oracle; `assay verify` (A-129) is exercised too, as a secondary consumer per O2, but must never be the ONLY validator (O3's own negative names exactly this failure). **The existing `cgroup_parent=...`/`--cgroup-parent="$cgroup_parent"` wiring around the `docker run` invocation must be preserved EXACTLY** — O4's "retains the verified cgroup helper" requirement — easy to drop by accident while restructuring the surrounding bash. | Proposed concretely by the readiness pass and confirmed technically sound: `assay.toml`'s current lane already declares `env_passthrough = ["PATH"]` (A-076-compliant), `nyxloom.toml`'s current gate command already computes and passes `cgroup_parent` correctly (read directly, not assumed), and P13's `standalone` fixture already proves the underlying two-environment recipe works inside exactly this image. Keeps everything inside `tester-unified` (no `escalate_if` trigger: "the nyxloom gate would need to run outside tester-unified" does not fire) and keeps `assay verify` explicitly non-primary (no `escalate_if` trigger: "self-hosting requires assay to be its sole oracle" does not fire either, since `test_self_hosting.py` is the real oracle). |
| A-131 | **The controlled producer mutation (O3's "a controlled producer mutation," "universal-PASS producer mutation") is pinned.** `shutil.copytree` the source tree into a scratch directory AT TEST TIME (mirroring P12's own established isolation precedent, A-120 — never touching the real, committed `runner.py`), apply ONE surgical text edit to the COPY's `runner.py` (e.g. replacing `assemble_verdict`'s `outcome = rollup(statuses)` line with an unconditional `Outcome.PASS`), build and install a wheel from that MUTATED copy (the SAME two-environment recipe as A-130, a fresh, independent build — never the shared/real gate build), run `assay run` against it to produce a genuinely "always PASS" verdict.json, and demonstrate: `assay verify` WRONGLY accepts it (schema-valid, internally consistent, semantically wrong — O3's own negative, demonstrated for real rather than merely asserted), while `test_self_hosting.py`'s independent check correctly REJECTS it (because it compares against something outside the mutated producer's own control). Record the number of independent assertions the mutation trips (A-067), the same real-mutation-with-a-real-count discipline every prior package has used. | Needs zero `scope.touch` change (`runner.py` never in scope, A-132) because the mutation happens against a filesystem COPY under `tmp_path`, never the committed file — the identical sidestep P12 already established one level up for its own isolation requirement (A-120). Demonstrating BOTH halves (the naive `assay verify`-only path failing, the independent test succeeding) in one test is what actually proves the project's central claim ("assay can gate itself without becoming the only witness to its own correctness") rather than merely asserting it. |
| A-132 | **`src/assay/runner.py` is added to P14's `scope.forbid`, explicitly** — confirmed, not left silently ambiguous the way the handoff originally left it (present in neither `scope.touch` nor `scope.forbid`). None of A-128 through A-131's mechanisms need to touch the real, committed `runner.py`: the self-hosting/mutation mechanisms operate on filesystem copies (A-130/A-131); `assay verify` is new code in its own file (`verify.py`) that IMPORTS `runner.py`'s already-public `rollup()` function, never edits it; and A-128 rules the three genuinely-unreachable fixture-matrix pairs as accepted, pre-existing design behavior rather than a defect requiring an `evaluate_r1` change. | The same class of "over/under-scoping left implicit" defect this project has repeatedly found and fixed (A-090, A-093, A-094, A-106, A-111, A-121) — for the FINAL package, leaving a load-bearing file's scope status implicit is worse than usual, since there is no later package whose own readiness pass would catch the gap if this one missed it. |
| A-133 | **`nyxloom-trove/nyxloom.toml`'s `asserts = ["tests-pass"]` is CONFIRMED ACCURATE and stays unchanged** — broadening it to `changed-line-coverage`/`canary-verified`/`mutation` (the real, established vocabulary other estate projects use, confirmed by reading `ciu`/`topos`'s own `nyxloom.toml`s and `nyxloom/src/nyxloom/adapters.py`'s `_RIGOROUS_ASSERTS`) would be EXACTLY the "declaring an unimplemented capability" failure O4's own negative names: even after A-130's wheel-based self-hosting restructure, assay's own gate never mechanically applies R1+ rigor to its own diff (`assay.toml`'s lane stays R0-only, by A-046's own already-established "a lane file declares WHAT EXISTS, not a target architecture" principle — confirmed unchanged, not itself something P14 revisits). The stale "P11 upgrades this declaration when the capability is real, and not before" comment — present, verbatim, in BOTH `nyxloom.toml` and `assay.toml` — is corrected in both: P11 (valid mutant construction) never touched either file and had no scope to; P14 is the package that finally touches both, and P14 does NOT upgrade rigor beyond R0 either, so the comment must state the REAL, permanent reason (self-hosting is deliberately R0-only) rather than point forward to a package that already came and went without doing this. Additionally, `handoff_globs = ["nyxloom-trove/handoffs/*.md"]` is narrowed to `["nyxloom-trove/handoffs/assay-*.md"]` — independently confirmed via a real lint run that the CURRENT glob matches `nyxloom-trove/handoffs/README.md` (a prose file, no frontmatter) and trips a real `L1 error: missing leading '---'`; the narrower glob excludes it without needing to touch the README itself, which is not in P14's own `scope.touch`. | Both stale comments and the glob defect were verified directly (`cat`, not summarized) before ruling, matching this project's standing discipline of checking rather than trusting even its own prior documentation. P00/P01's own DEEPER handoff-body linter errors (missing worktree/branch/BLOCKED mentions, broken cross-repo globs) remain genuinely out of P14's `scope.touch` (its own `"README.md"` entry matches only `assay/README.md`, confirmed via `fnmatch` semantics, not the trove's `handoffs/README.md` or any handoff body) — these are recorded as accepted, permanent, named debt in the final `STATE.md` update rather than expanded into P14's scope or fixed as an unreviewed controller-only edit to historical records. |

## Decided — session 19, 2026-08-07 (P15 controller review and merge)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-134 | **Git's output is a byte stream and a unified diff is `\n`-delimited: assay decides both boundaries itself, and a Python convenience default is never allowed to decide either.** Three concrete bindings, all merged into P15: (a) `assay.diff._unquote_git_path` removes git's trailing space-disambiguation tab BEFORE testing whether the remaining spelling is C-quoted. Git appends that tab to the printed LINE whenever the line contains a space (`strchr(line, ' ') ? "\t" : ""`, its own `diff.c`), so on a path that a double quote, a backslash, or a control byte already forced into quoted form the tab lands AFTER the closing quote — testing for the quote first left the whole quoted spelling unrecognised and made the raw text `"b/a \"b c.py"`, escapes and un-stripped `b/` prefix included, the file's recorded identity. (b) `assay.diff.parse_added_lines` splits its input on `"\n"` alone and never with `str.splitlines()`, which additionally breaks on a bare `\r`, a form feed, a vertical tab, `\x1c`-`\x1e`, `\x85`, U+2028 and U+2029 — all ordinary source content — tearing one diff body line into two; against a counts-driven state machine that closes the hunk early and silently DROPS its remaining additions, the under-measurement direction. The single trailing empty field a final newline leaves is dropped; no real unified-diff line is ever empty. (c) Every git invocation goes through `git._run_bytes` and one `git._decode_or_reject` policy: UTF-8 decoded explicitly, no universal-newline translation, and a typed `ERROR`/`GIT_FAILED` for output assay genuinely cannot represent. `subprocess`' `text=True` is banned at this boundary — it decodes with whatever codec the ambient `LC_CTYPE` names, raises a bare `UnicodeDecodeError` no caller catches, and rewrites a lone `\r` inside a source line into a phantom second line. P15's decode-or-reject policy is thereby applied to every pathname-bearing command, not only `git status`. | All three found by the controller in P15's own new code, all three reproduced against a real `git` binary and a real repository BEFORE any code changed, and all three broken-and-reverted afterwards with recorded failure counts under A-067 (3 / 2 / 3). They share one root cause worth stating once: P15's rewrite correctly refused to let a diff body line's CONTENT decide hunk structure, then let `str.splitlines`, `subprocess`' locale default, and an ordering accident decide three other boundaries. (b) is a genuine REGRESSION rather than inherited debt — the old content-sniffing parser mis-shifted the same input but never lost a line — which is why it is ruled rather than merely fixed. O1 already named "spaces, tabs, Unicode, and an embedded newline" and "the exact new-side line": the defects were inside what the oracle claimed, and survived because every fixture varied one dimension at a time. Standing consequence for the rest of the series: when an oracle enumerates input shapes, at least one fixture must COMBINE them. |
| A-135 | **P15's `FileCoverage` construction-time invariants make an internally-inconsistent coverage object impossible to instantiate, so from P16 onward a "contradictory artifact" fixture is built as a VALID `FileCoverage` (or hand-written payload) carrying WRONG summary numbers, status, or policy — never as a self-contradictory coverage object.** This binds P16's work item 6 and its context item 3 specifically: the three accepted contradictory artifacts from the post-series review's finding 2 (a `PASS` reporting 0 percent coverage, a `PASS` with a surviving mutant, a `PASS` with a transformed canary) must be reproduced that way. `excluded=None` remains meaningfully distinct from an empty known set and is not a workaround for this. | Raised by P15's own successor brief; a ruling delivered only in an agent message is not applied (A-072), so it lands here and in P16's handoff body before P16 is dispatched. The precedent already exists inside P15 itself: `test_canary_go_pipeline.py`'s pre-existing "fails for the wrong reason" fixture constructed a `FileCoverage` with the same lines both `executed` and `excluded`, and had to be rebuilt exactly this way — `executed` narrowed to the control's own already-covered line, `excluded` left as the appended lines — with its asserted outcome unchanged, since `canary.py` keys off `changed_lines & excluded` rather than `executed`. |

## Decided — session 20, 2026-08-07 (P16 controller review and merge)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-136 | **A judged status carries the payload it judged, and the refusal lives in the MODEL, not in the verifier.** `Claim` now refuses to construct: an R1 claim whose status is `PASS`/`FAIL` with no `coverage`; an R2 claim that is `PASS`, or carries `MUTANTS_SURVIVED`/`NO_MUTANTS`, with no `mutation`; and an R3 claim that is `PASS`/`FAIL`/`INCONCLUSIVE` with no `canary`. This is the exact converse of the three pre-existing `NO_MEASUREMENT` rules (A-025 at claim level): those forbid a payload where nothing was measured, these forbid a judged status where nothing was. Each is stated only where a producer proves it: `evaluate_coverage` returns `PASS`/`FAIL` and always with a `Coverage`; `judge_mutation` reaches `PASS` only on its `mutation is not None` branch and neither mutation-only reason code exists in `execute_command`'s baseline vocabulary; `judge_canary` returns exactly those three outcomes and `build_canary_claim` attaches the result it judged to every one. `ERROR`/`BUDGET_EXCEEDED` at R2/R3 stay payload-free — they describe machinery that never produced a result, not a judgement of one. | P16 shipped re-derivation of R1/R2/R3 status and four passing reproductions of sol finding 2's contradictory artifacts, and still accepted all three of the same artifacts with the payload DELETED instead of contradicted — `r1_pass` minus `coverage`, `r2_pass` minus `mutation`, `r3_pass` minus `canary`, each cheaper to forge than the ones it catches, each leaving the top-level rollup in perfect agreement. Every re-derivation function returns early when there is nothing to judge, which is correct; the error was expecting a validator to catch a state the model should never have been able to build. A-135 established that discipline for coverage one package earlier and it generalises: where a producer proves a state unreachable, make it unconstructible rather than merely unaccepted, and the verifier inherits the guarantee for free through reconstruction. |
| A-137 | **A re-derivation may skip only the input it genuinely cannot judge, never the whole check.** `verify._check_r2_rederivation` returned early whenever the artifact carried no R0 claim — including when a real `mutation` payload was present, which `judge_mutation`'s own first-statement early return proves needs no baseline at all. Since `rigor = ["R2"]` is a legal lane declaration (`assay.config` requires only a non-empty, duplicate-free subset of R0-R3), sol finding 2's second artifact evaded the entire check by not declaring R0. The R0-derived stand-in is now built only on the `mutation is None` branch; the never-read branch is handed a sentinel object rather than a plausible-looking fabricated baseline, so if that proof ever stops holding the failure is a loud `AttributeError` instead of a silently wrong verdict. | The package's own docstring stated the proof ("`baseline` is never touched when `mutation` is not `None`") and then guarded on `baseline`'s source anyway — a guard written for the wrong precondition. Its test asserted the hole as the contract on the one input shape where skipping IS right. Standing consequence: when a check's precondition is "I have enough information," derive it from the datum actually needed, not from a sibling that usually accompanies it. |
| A-138 | **A verdict artifact's schema version is a consumer migration, never a producer upgrade — and a foreign version is reported AS a version problem and nothing else.** `verify_document` compares `schema_version` before every other check and, on a mismatch, returns that one diagnostic and stops. It never coerces, defaults, or in-place upgrades a stale artifact: an artifact records what one run of one assay judged, so the only honest way to obtain a v3 verdict is to produce one. Recorded in DESIGN-GUIDE §6 alongside the schema-as-data contract. | P16 work item 7 asked for exactly this and it was not built: no test fed a v2 artifact to `assay verify` at all. A real v2 artifact reported `schema: 'excluded_lines'` — a bare `KeyError` naming a v3 field its producer had never heard of — plus two cross-field complaints that are consequences of the version rather than independent defects. Every later check reads a shape the verifier was never written against, so continuing past the version buries the one actionable sentence under the noise it generates. This also fixes the accumulate-every-failure rule's one honest exception, stated here rather than left as an undocumented special case. |

## Decided — session 21, 2026-08-08 (P17 controller review and merge)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-139 | **Once `HEAD` is resolved, EVERY terminal path emits a complete artifact — including a refusal to run at all, and including one caused by a rigor level this build cannot reach.** `cli._resolve_declared_adapters` now consults the registry for every declared level above R0 (not the literal `"R1"`), before anything executes; `runner.refuse_lane` (the generalised, exported `_refuse_before_running`) renders the result as a real `Verdict` carrying one `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` claim per DECLARED rigor level. A typed `AssayError` reaching `main()`'s handler after HEAD is known is now a defect, not a style. | Work item 3 says it in one sentence: "Initial HEAD failure may remain a pre-artifact error because no honest commit identity exists; every later terminal path must emit a complete artifact." Two paths violated it and both were reproduced against the installed console script, not inferred: `judge.language = "go"` returned exit 2 with nothing to read, and an R2 lane RAN ITS COMMAND TO COMPLETION (`cov.json` present afterwards) before dying in `assemble_verdict` with no record that anything had happened. The second is the worse half — side effects committed, and a consumer's CI holding only an exit code. `escalate_if`'s "a terminal path with a known HEAD cannot be represented by schema v3" does not apply: the shape was already being built 100 lines away for the dirty-tree case. `assemble_verdict`'s own missing-claim guard STAYS as the library-level backstop for callers that are not this CLI; it is simply no longer what a real `assay run` reaches first. The implementer identified this itself, declined to decide it alone, and filed it as its own least-confident call in P18's and P19's carried-in notes — the correct move, and the reason this is a ruling rather than a finding. |
| A-140 | **A refusal mutates nothing. Validate, then refuse, then mutate — never mutate, then refuse.** `run_lane` performs the coverage-artifact SAFETY check (pure), then the whole-tree cleanliness check, and only then removes a stale artifact. **Consequence, and it is a real requirement on consumers, not a detail: the declared coverage artifact must be git-ignored or absent**, otherwise it is untracked worktree state and the run is refused. | The original order removed the artifact BEFORE the cleanliness guard, which had two consequences neither the diff nor a green gate could show. A run refused as `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`DIRTY_TREE` — an outcome whose whole meaning is "I did not judge anything" — had already deleted a file; and the guard was evaluating a tree this function had itself just modified, so it could never report the state it actually found. Generalised, it was destructive: a lane naming ANY untracked regular file as its `artifact` had that file deleted, reproduced with an uncommitted source file that came back gone. Deleting to make a tree look clean is precisely the silent-invention anti-pattern AGENTS.md §4.2a names; refusing loudly with the file intact is the trade that policy asks for. The removal that remains is the only `unlink` in the module that provably cannot destroy anything a consumer still wanted, because every other shape at that path refuses one step earlier. |
| A-141 | **A capability is not closed until the artifact matrix that audits it moves too — and an audit that forbids the new shape is worse than one that merely omits it.** A-128's "three structurally unreachable pairs" is CLOSED: `ERROR`/`GIT_FAILED`, and claim-level `ERROR`/`FORMAT_MISMATCH` and `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`, each now have a hand-written fixture. `EXCLUDED_ENTIRELY` is empty (kept as a named constant so any future re-narrowing is an argued edit, never a silent one) and the conformance audit is LEVEL-AWARE. | Work item 6 made all three reachable and the tests were never updated, so `test_verdict_conformance.py` shipped three statements that were no longer true of the product: it excluded two pairs as "structurally unreachable", it instructed its reader not to fix `evaluate_r1` to reach them (which is exactly what this package was chartered to do), and it asserted `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` "must never appear on a claim" — an assertion a CORRECT fixture would have failed. It stayed green only because no fixture was added; the suite was pinned to the pre-P17 world and would have actively resisted the repair. The level-awareness is not cosmetic: `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` now has two genuinely different producers (a coverage artifact the lane never wrote, P17; a broken attestation file, P10), and a flattened pair set would let either fixture silently cover for the other's deletion, retiring one package's negative without anyone noticing. |
| A-142 | **Adapter external-tool preflighting (work item 2's fourth clause) is deferred once more, and this is where that is recorded.** No code preflights `LanguageAdapter.external_tools`; a lane whose adapter needs an absent tool has no `NO_MEASUREMENT` reason code to render. The check AND its reason code belong to the first package that registers an adapter genuinely declaring an external tool — **which is P22, named concretely in A-144 below rather than left as "some later package".** | The deferral itself is correct and follows A-086/A-087's Go precedent exactly: the closed vocabulary (`errors.py`, outside P17's `scope.touch`) has no member for "missing tool", and both adapters that exist declare `external_tools = ()`, so the state is unreachable through any real producer path this build ships — building the check would be decorative code no test could discriminate. What was wrong was only WHERE it was written down: a `registry.py` docstring and the implementation commit's body, plus a note in a downstream handoff. A-072 already ruled that a decision living only in an agent-visible place is not applied. It is applied here. |
| A-144 | **P22 owns `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`, and its `scope.touch` was amended to include `src/assay/errors.py` and `src/assay/cli.py` so that it can.** A-086 named the member and ruled it "belongs to the package that first makes the state reachable"; A-087 deferred it (P08's Go adapter shipped `external_tools = ()`) and A-142 deferred it again for the same reason. P22 is the first package where an adapter genuinely declares a tool it cannot run without. P23 declares `assay-go-helper` in `GoAdapter.external_tools` and relies on P22's preflight; a corresponding note was added to both handoffs. | Found while propagating P17's outcome, by checking whether the packages that inherit the deferral could actually execute it. They could not: **P22's work item 7 already demands that "unavailable helper prerequisites render honest non-PASS outcomes" and P23's work item 7 already demands breaking "external-tool preflight" — while neither package's `scope.touch` contained `errors.py` or any preflight call site.** That is the "forbid the file the correct implementation needs" carve defect, which produces hollow improvisation or a spurious BLOCKED rather than the work. `ERROR`/`EXEC_FAILED` is not an escape hatch: A-086 already rejected it as a lie (it means the lane's own command never started, a different fact), and DESIGN-GUIDE §11/A-013 say a missing declared prerequisite is `NO_MEASUREMENT`. The widening is minimal and deliberate — `runner.py` stays out of P22's scope because `runner.refuse_lane` is already public and already total over `lane.rigor` (A-139), so the refusal needs calling, not writing. |
| A-143 | **`judgment.r1.base` records the RESOLVED commit, and at least one oracle must declare a symbolic ref to prove it.** The installed-wheel R1 test declares a branch name and expects the 40-char commit it resolves to. | The behaviour was correct from the start; the EVIDENCE was not. Every test in the suite declared `judge.base` as a full SHA, and `resolve_base` on a full SHA returns that same SHA — so replacing the resolved value with the raw declared string passed the entire suite. A contract whose correct and incorrect implementations are indistinguishable to every test is untested, however many tests mention it. The same trap applies to any later "we recorded the resolved X" claim: the fixture must make resolved and declared genuinely different, or it proves nothing. |

## Decided — session 22, 2026-08-08 (P18 controller review and merge)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-145 | **A repo-relative path and a project-relative path are two different spellings, and every boundary that crosses between them says which one it speaks.** `MutationTarget.path` is REPO-top-relative (`git diff`'s own spelling, the same one `Coverage.missing_lines` keys use, so one artifact never mixes two conventions); each mutant's scratch tree is a copy of the PROJECT root. `mutation.project_prefix` is the single conversion, `run_mutation` now REQUIRES `repo_top` alongside `project_root`, and a target outside the project root is refused before submission rather than discovered inside a worker thread. | P18 wired diff-derived paths into a copy-of-project-root for the first time, and the two conventions had never had to agree before — every fixture in the suite, and every P12 test, had the project AS the repository top, so the mismatch was invisible. Reproduced against the real CLI on the layout assay itself has inside `vbpub`: a project in a subdirectory got `FileNotFoundError` out of a worker thread, out of `run_lane`, out of `assay run` — AFTER the lane's command had run, with no artifact emitted. That is A-139's own shape, reintroduced one package later, and no oracle in P18 could see it because none of them had a project root that differed from the repo top. `repo_top` is REQUIRED, not defaulted to `project_root`: a default that silently assumes they are equal is exactly the assumption that produced the bug. |
| A-146 | **`assay run --help`'s own "this build evaluates ..." sentence is a capability declaration, and it is pinned to the registry that decides it.** A test now derives the levels named in the real `--help` output from `cli._built_in_registry`'s own entries plus `R0`, so the prose cannot drift from the capability in either direction. | P18 widened the registry to R2 and left the sentence saying "R0 and Python R1". Under-declaring is not harmless just because it is the safe direction: this project exists to close the gap between a declared capability and a real one, and the post-series review's finding 1 is that exact gap in the other direction. Pinned to the registry rather than to a literal string, because a literal would only move the drift one file over. |
| A-147 | **An oracle clause that cannot be honoured is written down as unreachable, with the argument; it is never quietly dropped — and P18's work item 7 was quietly dropped.** O4's installed-wheel R2 fixture now exists: five of its six named terminal shapes (killed, survived, no-mutants, baseline-adverse, budget-exceeded) as complete hand-written documents through the real wheel, each with the shared tree hashed before and after. The sixth, a CRASHED mutant, is recorded IN THE SUITE as not producible through a real lane, with the reason. P23's `scope.touch` also gains `cli.py` — the second instance of A-144's shape. | Work item 7 and O4 were not implemented, not declared skipped, and not mentioned in any commit body; the nearest thing shipped was an in-process, field-by-field, killed-only assertion. This is the third consecutive package where "a complete artifact" was satisfied by a handful of field assertions (A-141, A-143, now this), so the instruction is being restated in every downstream carried-in note rather than assumed. The crash bucket genuinely cannot be reached: `argv` is byte-identical for baseline and mutants (A-118), the scratch tree is a faithful copy, and only one file's text differs — so any argv that launches for the baseline launches for every mutant, and any argv that does not fails the baseline first and short-circuits. Writing that down is worth more than a sixth test would have been; leaving the gap silent would have read as coverage. |
| A-148 | **`judgment.rN` is tied to the claim it describes, and P19's `scope.touch` was widened to `verdict.py`/`verify.py` so that someone can do it.** Presence must agree with the payload-bearing claim in both directions (as `judgment.r1` already does), and every operator/mechanism a payload NAMES must be one the recorded policy declared. | P16 deliberately added no correspondence for `judgment.r2`, on the correct reasoning that R2's status was already re-derivable from the mutation buckets alone — and told P18, in its own carried-in note, to ask whether that still held once a real producer populated it. It does not: `jobs`/`operators` are now recorded facts no rule ties to anything, which is the "a field a consumer must take on trust" gap the whole series exists to close. P18 could not close it (`verdict.py` forbidden, `verify.py` not in `scope.touch`) and correctly did not improvise — but it also did not record the answer anywhere a controller would act on, leaving a deferral with no executor: **no package in P19–P25 could touch either file.** Found the same way A-144 was, by reading the downstream scopes rather than the diff. P19 populates `judgment.r3` and meets the identical question, so it owns both. |

## Decided — session 23, 2026-08-08 (P19 controller review and merge)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-149 | **A run relocated into a copy must be judged by relocated paths, and every absolute path in the lane is enumerated before the relocation is called complete.** `canary.run_isolated_canary` respells `judge.source_root_paths` against the scratch project root (`_relocate_source_roots`) before handing the lane to the pipeline that runs inside the copy. `source_root_paths` is the only field that needs it — `judge.coverage.artifact` and `judge.canary.target` are project-relative and resolved against whichever project root they are handed, and `judge.base` is a revision — and that enumeration is part of the ruling, not an aside. | P19 copied the consumer's repository into scratch state and then judged the copy using the CONSUMER's own absolute source roots. Nothing raised: `evaluate_coverage`'s source-root boundary simply rejected every changed file, `considered` fell to 0, `pct` became a vacuous 100.0, and R1 PASSed having measured nothing — the exact laundering-gate shape A-016/A-035 already name for a typo'd source root, reached by relocation instead of by typo. The consequences were not cosmetic: **`uncovered-line`, one of exactly two mechanisms, could never PASS** (its transformed half PASSed vacuously, so every real run reported `CANARY_SURVIVED`), and every import-break control PASSed for a reason unrelated to the one its own oracle named. The branch was gate-green at 1826 tests and 100% statement+branch coverage. Reproduced by running the one lane shape no fixture used — `uncovered-line` with R1 declared alongside R3 — and fixed before merge. A-145 is the same family one package earlier (repo-relative vs project-relative); this is the second time a copy-based orchestration has been wrong about paths in a way no fixture could see, so the rule is now stated as "enumerate", not "check the obvious one". |
| A-150 | **A mechanism is only PROVED where its expected reason is producible, and the fixture must declare the rigor level that produces it.** `uncovered-line` expects `UNCOVERED_LINES`, which R1 produces and nothing else does; an `R0`+`R3` lane can therefore only ever report that mechanism as having SURVIVED. At least one fixture per declared mechanism must declare the level its expected reason comes from — for `uncovered-line`, `rigor = ["R0","R1","R3"]`. | O3 says "import-break and uncovered-line each PASS only for their specific expected reason". P19 shipped six R3 fixtures and an installed-wheel artifact set in which `uncovered-line` appeared exactly twice, both times as a SURVIVOR, both times on an R0+R3 lane where a PASS was structurally impossible — so half of O3 had no witness, and the suite recorded the impossibility as the expected shape. This is why A-149 was invisible: the only configuration that could expose it was the only one nobody wrote. Generalised, it is A-143's shape one level up — there, correct and incorrect were indistinguishable because every fixture declared an already-resolved base; here, a whole mechanism's PASS branch was unreachable because every fixture declared a rigor set that could not reach it. |
| A-151 | **"Unreachable through the real product" is a claim, and it carries the same burden of proof as any other — a demonstrated reproduction, not an argument from the code's shape.** P19's `test_standalone.py` recorded that a wrong-observed-cause needs a deliberately mislabeled adapter. It does not: `import-break` injected into a module the lane's own tests never import leaves R0 passing and is caught by R1, so the observed reason is genuinely `UNCOVERED_LINES` against an expected `COMMAND_FAILED`. Both a unit-level and an installed-wheel artifact now exist for it. A genuine NO-OP transform remains the one shape a real adapter cannot produce, and that one is argued from a property of the injectors that is checked by their own tests. | A-147 ruled that an unhonourable oracle clause is written down with its argument rather than dropped, and P19 followed it — but the argument was wrong, and a wrong impossibility is worse than a silent gap, because it propagates as fact: P24's handoff had already been told to trust it before dispatch. The tell is available without any reproduction at all — the mechanism/expected-reason pairing fixes what is EXPECTED, never what a run OBSERVES, so "the pairing is fixed" cannot bound the observed side. Standing consequence: when a package records a shape as unreachable, the review reproduces the argument, not just the shapes that were built. |
| A-152 | **`judgment.r3.target` is recorded and structurally unverifiable under schema v3; this is accepted, named, and not to be rediscovered as a defect.** A-148's correspondence check ties `judgment.r3.mechanism` to `Claim.canary.mechanism` in both the model and `verify.py`, because the payload carries a mechanism. It carries no target, so nothing in the artifact can witness `target` being wrong. `judgment.r2` has no equivalent gap (`operators` is checked against every operator the payload names) and `judgment.r1` has none either. | Closing it needs a `canary.target` field in the verdict schema — a v4 migration touching `src/assay/schemas`, which is forbidden to every package in P19–P25, and A-138 already rules that a schema version is a consumer migration rather than a producer upgrade. Recorded as an accepted gap rather than improvised around: the alternatives available inside v3 (inferring the target from `judgment.r1.source_roots`, or from the canary description string) would each be a rule that looks like verification and is not, which is the failure mode this whole series exists to remove. Tracked as A-O18 in `STATE.md`. |

## Decided — session 24, 2026-08-08 (P15–P19 adversarial post-series review)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-153 | **Renumber the unimplemented queue into a contiguous P20–P29 series.** P20 repository/artifact integrity, P21 verdict v4, P22 exact isolation, P23 versioned wheel, P24 real Python qualification, P25 attestation, P26/P27/P28 real Go R1/R2/R3, P29 Vitest. Completed P19 and earlier history do not move. | The first proposal used P19a/P19b/P19c to avoid identity churn, but live `nyxloom lint` rejects suffix ids (`^[a-z][a-z0-9]*-P[0-9]{2,4}...$`). These handoffs are unimplemented, so this is the last cheap moment to give the executable queue valid ordered identities. The mapping is recorded here so older A-144/A-147/A-151 references to P22/P23/P24 remain interpretable as pre-renumber names. |
| A-154 | **Every lane declares R0.** R2 and R3 are repeated executions of the declared command and cannot honestly exist without its baseline identity; `uncovered-line` R3 additionally declares R1 because only R1 can produce `UNCOVERED_LINES`. | The loader currently permits `rigor = ["R2"]`, while the real runner unconditionally constructs an R0 claim and crashes with a bare `ValueError`. Treating R0 as an invisible prerequisite would record fewer claims than Assay actually ran. Requiring it makes the lane declaration, execution ledger, and one-claim-per-declared-level rule agree. This supersedes A-061 only on R0: R1/R2/R3 remain otherwise independently selectable. |
| A-155 | **Resolve one immutable effective command plan and reuse it for baseline, every mutant, and both canary halves.** The plan includes declared argv/env, permitted appended argv, permitted passthrough values, budget, and the project-relative working-directory identity; relocation changes only the repository root that identity is resolved beneath. | P18/P19 call `execute_command(lane, cwd=copy)` again, which re-resolves only the lane declaration and silently drops baseline `argv_append` and passthrough values. The artifact therefore describes a command different from the one R2/R3 actually judged. An effective plan is a fact already measured at the input boundary; recomputing a smaller substitute is a shadowing default. |
| A-156 | **R2/R3 isolation snapshots the whole repository topology, preserves only contained symlinks, and uses a controlled Git namespace.** A nested project remains at the same repo-relative path; absolute/escaping symlinks are refused; copied baseline artifacts are never accepted as fresh output; transform commits use plumbing over exact bytes, with neutral identity and no hooks, filters, or ambient Git configuration. | Copying only `project_root` makes a baseline that reads `../shared` pass in the real monorepo and every mutant fail in scratch, awarding a false R2 PASS. `copytree`'s default dereferences symlinks and can copy external/unbounded content. R3 currently copies a baseline coverage artifact and can read it after control/transform commands that produced nothing. Ambient `GIT_DIR` was also reproduced redirecting Assay from repository A to repository B. |
| A-157 | **Schema v4 is the next pre-adoption artifact contract.** It carries every mutant identity including killed mutants, closes mutation-operator vocabularies in model/schema/verifier, binds the canary target, distinguishes exclusion capability from known-empty exclusions, records a declared mutant-count ceiling, validates timestamp order, and gains truthful reasons for output-write and mutation-limit failures. No v3 artifact is upgraded in place. | Schema v3 cannot verify which killed sites satisfied the declared operator policy, cannot witness `judgment.r3.target`, and erases A-008's `None` versus empty exclusion distinction. Concurrency bounds workers, not total executions. A-O14/A-O16/A-O18 and these new gaps all require one consumer migration; batching them once before any external adoption is cheaper and safer than serial schema bumps. |
| A-158 | **A normally-started mutant command that exits nonzero is killed; `crashed` is reserved for failure to start or execute the command boundary.** Assay does not infer test failure versus compiler rejection from unstructured tool output. | The effective baseline passed and isolation varies one mutant only, so a normal nonzero is deterministic evidence that the mutant was rejected. P27's old (pre-renumber P23) demand that Go compilation failure be separated from ordinary test rejection is not derivable from an arbitrary declared argv and would force output-text heuristics or a second invented command. This preserves A-117's already-shipped observable distinction rather than manufacturing a language-specific guess in the core. |
| A-159 | **The P26–P28 language-abstraction proof uses the real `shared-ramdisk-depot-manager` module in a disposable copy, in addition to tiny Assay-owned fixtures; it does not migrate or edit srdm.** Assay's result is compared with srdm's independent `tools/covergate` result on the same changed input. | A hand-written Go fixture proves parser mechanics, not that the abstraction survives a real multi-package Go repository, module prefix, existing gate policy, and nested-project topology. srdm is the estate's native Go implementation and independent oracle. Keeping the exercise disposable separates adapter validation from consumer adoption and avoids expanding Assay's authority into another project. |
| A-160 | **`lane.budget` bounds the whole Assay lane invocation, not each subprocess independently.** Every baseline, mutant, control, transform, snapshot, and evaluation consumes one shared budget; the next unit is not started when none remains. `max_mutants` is the separate deterministic cardinality bound. | Applying the full lane timeout to each of N mutants turns a declared five-minute budget into N×five minutes plus copying and parsing, so `jobs` bounds concurrency but neither wall work nor cost. The declared budget is singular and belongs to the lane; interpreting it per subprocess is a masked multiplicative default. Tests use injected clocks/processes and never timing thresholds. |
| A-161 | **Repeated-execution state is reconstructed from the resolved commit's tracked Git objects, not copied from the consumer working tree.** The complete repository topology and project prefix are preserved; ignored/untracked files are not implicit inputs. A lane needing runtime data obtains it from declared environment/argv or commits it — Assay never guesses an ignore-copy policy. | Whole-working-tree `copytree` copies stale ignored coverage and arbitrary caches, dereferences symlinks by default, and makes repeatability depend on facts absent from the commit Assay records. A committed-object snapshot gives baseline, mutants, and canaries identical bytes, retains monorepo siblings, and makes the recorded commit a real content identity. It also removes the need to decide which ignored files are secretly required, a fact only the consumer could know. |
| A-162 | **Before Go qualification, the installed wheel must pass one external Python-project qualification against a disposable current Topos tree.** This is P24 and is validation-only; a real adoption remains a separately carved package in Topos's own trove. | Assay's self-hosting proves distribution isolation but can share the producer's assumptions, while hello-world Python fixtures prove mechanics only. Topos is the original first-adoption target (A-037), already runs in `tester-unified`, and carries an independent changed-line coverage implementation. A disposable comparison tests realistic packaging, monorepo paths and a large suite without granting Assay authority to modify a busy consumer project. |

## Decided — session 25, 2026-08-08 (solution-bearing recarve follow-up)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-163 | **The one pre-adoption v4 migration reserves every already-known truthful successor terminal:** `ERROR/OUTPUT_WRITE_FAILED`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/MUTANT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/SNAPSHOT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, and `NO_MEASUREMENT/MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`. `max_mutants` is required in `1..10,000`; discovery reads at most max+1 and the limit artifact records `candidate_count=max+1`, zero attempted mutants, and four empty identity buckets. | P22 cannot truthfully report a bounded snapshot refusal and P26 cannot preflight its first real external tool if P21 leaves their reasons out of the schema it exclusively owns. Adding decorative unreachable enum members is normally forbidden; here both consumers are already carved and ordered immediately after the migration. The explicit mutation sentinel lets an independent verifier distinguish refusal-before-submission from a truncated sample. This extends A-157's named v4 reason set and supersedes the old package-number wording in A-142/A-144: P21 owns the vocabulary, P26 owns effective-PATH reachability, and P27 only adds the helper declaration. **OWNERSHIP HOP RECORDED 2026-08-11 by A-246. This row assigned effective-PATH reachability to P26; P26 shipped as attestation/deadline hardening and did not take it. Verified: `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL` has NO producer anywhere in `src/` (only the `ReasonCode` member and the `REASON_CODES` table entry), and `errors.py`'s own comment now reads "RESERVED for **P27**". So ownership went P22 → P26 → P27 with the last hop recorded nowhere — the A-072/A-147 shape. Benign today (the state is unreachable; both adapters declare no external tools), and P27's re-carve is the right owner now that A-217/A-239 make the statement-position helper assay's first real external tool. **P27's re-carve must claim it explicitly rather than inherit it silently.** |
| A-164 | **Topos differential qualification compares only semantics Topos's independent evaluator can express.** Use `allow_excluded=true` for three-way parity; test `allow_excluded=false` against the hand manifest, not against Topos, whose current evaluator has no exclusion-capability channel. Integrity terminals absent from Topos are likewise Assay-versus-manifest checks. | Requiring two independent tools to agree on a fact one structurally erases does not strengthen the oracle; it pressures the implementation to erase the richer fact too. The useful independence is exact agreement on shared covered/missing/comment/0/0 semantics plus an independently authored manifest for the asymmetric policy. |
| A-165 | **Attestation freshness is answered per reviewed identity with bounded `git diff --quiet --exit-code ... -- <path>`, not by transporting and splitting a set of changed display names.** Exit 0 is current, exit 1 is stale, other exits are typed failures; Git pathspec semantics make a reviewed directory cover descendants. | The old newline/U+2028 failure exists only because Assay asked Git for a spelling it did not need and then parsed display records. A NUL-safe list would repair transport, but the actual question is boolean per already-declared path. Removing the unnecessary representation eliminates quoting/decoding/membership ambiguity and makes the comparison bound explicit. |
| A-166 | **P20–P29 are implementation-shaped contracts, not invitations to design.** Their normative packets fix public/wire shapes, state flow, topology, bounds, decisions, prepared proof, traceability, and remaining degrees of freedom. Before/after baseline is `ebbe208c4d4ff275da2ca6bd276bea103fca2563`; the canonical rule is AUTHORING revision `2026-08-08-r4`. | P15–P19's implementer and reviewer were both strong, but code/tests/review shared the same package-local interpretation. More intelligence does not repair a missing interface or an input distribution nobody named. Transferring the carver's load-bearing solution lets cheaper implementers execute rather than rediscover it, while an adversarial specification review and reviewer-authored combined-axis attacks address common-mode failure. |

## Decided — session 26, 2026-08-08 (cost-aware P20+ recarve)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-167 | **Supersede A-153/A-166's ten-package queue with thirteen serial packages P20–P32 at AUTHORING `2026-08-08-r5`, anchored to `2f2167f5928e5deacd93f1e9565238aef8acfe32`.** Old P20/P21 remain P20/P21; old P22 splits into P22 snapshot substrate + P23 execution integration; old P23/P24/P25 become P24/P25/P26; old P26 splits into P27 Go gate/adapter + P28 real-srdm R1; old P27 splits into P29 helper protocol + P30 real R2; old P28/P29 become P31/P32. Git rename history preserves the old text. | These are not 33 microtasks. Three splits isolate expensive design/security seams from repeatable integration so a failed helper, snapshot, or real-consumer proof does not invalidate a large mixed package. The serial dependency chain keeps orientation and merge overhead bounded and lets each later contract be JIT-frozen only after upstream uncertainty shrinks. |
| A-168 | **P23 introduces bounded common `MutationSite` discovery; P29 only makes Go implement it. Discovery never returns a tuple of full mutated source files.** The common adapter call receives selected operators and the remaining `max_mutants+1` capacity. A submitted job materializes one full replacement only at execution time, with at most `jobs` such files live. V4 outcome identity is `(path,start_byte,end_byte,replacement_sha256,operator)`; line/description are diagnostic. | The prior Go packet bounded one input file at 64 MiB and candidates at 10,001 while retaining `mutated_text` per candidate—hundreds of GiB under its own legal inputs. More importantly, P21's v4 cap would already have been a false bound for Python if P23 collected the old adapter tuple before applying max+1. Moving the common site seam into P23 makes the cap true before external Python qualification; P29 then adds only syntax-aware Go discovery. |
| A-169 | **Role routing is explicit and independent review remains fresh.** Sol xhigh performs mandatory JIT carve/probe for P20/P21/P22/P29 and any drifted contract; Opus xhigh implements P21/P22/P29; Sonnet xhigh implements the other ten; a fresh Opus xhigh context independently reviews every package. Luna may control Git/session/brief mechanics only and stops for Sol/Opus on semantic promotion or contract changes. | Model intelligence should be spent where irreducible reasoning remains. A cheap controller is safe only when it routes facts mechanically. Giving schema, snapshot-security, or AST/protocol design to the same medium implementation path recreates the common-mode risk the P15–P19 review exposed. |
| A-170 | **Only one verdict schema is active in a released Assay build.** P21 replaces the shipped schema with v4; producers emit v4 only and `assay verify` rejects v1–v3 with one version-only diagnostic before visiting foreign fields. Historical decisions/tests may name older versions, but there is no runtime negotiation, default upgrade, compatibility writer, or multi-version adoption target. | Multiple historical schema numbers describe contract evolution during greenfield development, not a promised compatibility matrix. One strict current schema keeps adoption and independent verification unambiguous; retaining automatic migrations would preserve mistakes before any external consumer needs them. |
| A-171 | **A failed syntax-aware mutation-discovery boundary is `ERROR/MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`; valid discovery with zero selected sites is `INCONCLUSIVE/NO_MUTANTS`.** P21 reserves the v4 reason before P29 makes the helper reachable. Missing helper remains the separate prerequisite terminal `NO_MEASUREMENT/MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`. | Treating malformed helper output, invalid source, protocol failure, or helper crash as an empty candidate list converts inability to measure into apparent evidence that nothing was mutable. Conversely, treating a genuinely valid empty result as an execution error discards an honest inconclusive state. |
