A real end-column-1 fixture distinguishes the two. Choosing an existing implementation instead of the instrumented source semantics would make independent qualification circular. |

## Decided — session 27, 2026-08-09 (P20 JIT carve and proof freeze)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-173 | **Every Git child uses one absolute executable resolved from the caller-declared `PATH`, a closed replacement environment, and an explicit resolved `--git-dir` + `--work-tree` pair.** The environment includes `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`; diff always receives `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv`; global config disables hooks, fsmonitor, and commit signing. Repository discovery finds the nearest real `.git` marker and uses only a sanitized `rev-parse --absolute-git-dir` bootstrap, preserving linked worktrees. | P20's first packet removed ambient `GIT_DIR` but still left two false identities. A local `core.worktree` redirects `rev-parse --show-toplevel`/status despite `-C` and even a command-line `-c core.worktree=...`; only explicit global `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` fixed the witnessed probe. A replace ref made a real base-to-HEAD diff empty while every commit spelling remained valid. Local `commit.gpgSign` could launch an arbitrary configured signer through canary's real commit path. P22 already promised replace-free committed-object reads, so omitting these controls would have deferred known contradictions into implementation. |
| A-174 | **Safe artifact I/O has a frozen descriptor-owning state machine and byte parser.** `reserve_output(...)->OutputReservation` is side-effect-free; `arm()` alone may unlink the unchanged prior regular file; `consume()` reads once relative to the held parent and returns bytes-or-missing; `close()` is idempotent; invalid transitions are programmer errors. Coverage reads at most 16 MiB, decodes once, maps missing to `NO_MEASUREMENT/EMPTY_COVERAGE`, and injects a parsed profile into `evaluate_r1` so `run_lane` never reopens its output by pathname. | Calling the reservation “immutable” while also saying “consumed once” left ownership undefined and risked reuse of a closed integer descriptor after the OS reassigned it. Path prechecks plus `read_text` cannot bind a parent, inode, type, size, or current invocation. The explicit API/skeleton transfers those decisions to the packet and gives P26 one reusable bounded input seam instead of a second race-prone implementation. |
| A-175 | **Post-command Git-visible dirt is checked before output consumption or any R1/R2/R3 work.** With higher rigor, preserve the command's real R0 claim and render every higher declared claim `NO_MEASUREMENT/DIRTY_TREE`; for R0-only, R0 carries that terminal. Preserve the resolved command plan and timing, leave consumer state untouched, and add no v3 return-code field. | The pre-run cleanliness check cannot prove the command left the recorded commit intact. Checking only source roots let a command mutate a tracked test/support file and retain PASS. Running coverage or repeated rigor before the post-check produces claims already known to be bound to stale repository state. The handwritten v3 artifact fixes precedence and prevents implementers from inventing a schema change to preserve an otherwise unrepresentable detail. |
| A-176 | **P20 is READY only with the carver-owned assets under `nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/P20/`, frozen against input anchor `8aad3dc3b190915bb27881a0f3004b339aeef9c2` in the commit containing `reports/assay-P20-JIT-CARVE.md`, and forbidden to the implementer.** Its skeleton compiles, every acceptance test is a witnessed controlled red on the anchor, the Git tracer passes in `tester-unified`, and AUTHORING's exact pre-dispatch adversarial specification review is recorded in that report. | The P15–P19 failure was common-mode interpretation: implementer, tests, and reviewer all inherited the same convenient examples. A prose-only plan would repeat it. Independent locked inputs let Sonnet execute a fixed solution while Opus can still add a new combined-axis attack; forbidding edits prevents implementation pressure from weakening the acceptance contract. |

## Decided — session 28, 2026-08-09 (P20 routed review correction)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-177 | **For worktree cleanliness, a clean per-directory `.gitignore` is committed repository policy; `.git/info/exclude` and global/system/configured exclude files are not.** `dirty_paths` unions NUL-safe porcelain records with `git ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore -z --`, under P20's sanitized boundary. A dirty/untracked `.gitignore` is itself a refusal; a clean committed one may exempt the declared coverage artifact. Both Git output streams have fixed byte bounds and crossing either kills the child with `ERROR/GIT_FAILED`. | The reviewed P20 branch neutralized `core.excludesFile` but `.git/info/exclude` still made unrelated untracked dirt disappear. Enumerating every ignored path and subtracting only the artifact was unnecessary and would reject paths the repository deliberately excludes. The exact `ls-files` form was probed against a real repository: with `.git/info/exclude = *`, it returned `leftover.bin` while still omitting committed-`.gitignore` path `cov.json`. Merely capping retained stderr also left work unbounded: a child could write forever while Assay kept draining and discarding it. |
| A-178 | **Verdict v4 distinguishes a clean post-command HEAD move from worktree/index dirt with `NO_MEASUREMENT/HEAD_CHANGED`.** Dirt retains `DIRTY_TREE` precedence; when the tree/index is clean but resolved HEAD differs from the pre-run OID, R0-only carries `HEAD_CHANGED`, while a higher-rigor lane preserves the actual R0 claim and marks each later declared claim `HEAD_CHANGED`. P20 keeps the v3-compatible `DIRTY_TREE` spelling until P21 performs the one active schema migration. | The P20 reviewer correctly closed a false PASS without inventing a v3 reason, but `DIRTY_TREE` is false diagnosis for a command that commits and leaves a clean tree. P21 already owns the only pre-adoption reason/schema migration, so it can make the distinction without another version bump. The precedence fixes the combined case rather than letting evaluation order choose its label. |
| A-179 | **Assay's registered gate is controller-owned and emits a complete outer receipt.** `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh` derives the host repository source from the running devcontainer's Docker mount or reads the explicit `ASSAY_GATE_HOST_REPO_ROOT`; it never hardcodes or locally stats the foreign host path, and Docker `--mount` fails if it is absent. The inner gate emits markers after wheel install, the installed-wheel lane, and independent self-hosting; the outer script emits `ASSAY_REGISTERED_GATE_COMPLETE=1` only after Docker exits zero. Implementer/reviewer children run focused diagnostics; the controller runs and hashes the authoritative gate log. | P20's implementer log showed the inner seven-test result but not a separately identifiable outer self-hosting completion, and the `--rm` container was already gone. Re-running recovered evidence but is needless recurring cost. The old inline argv also hardcoded the current host bind source, violating the estate's derive/read/fail rule. Explicit phase and outer markers make the absence mechanical, while a raw log/digest plus exit remains the evidence; preserving a stopped container adds nothing. |
| A-180 | **P21, not P23, lands the bounded common/Python `MutationSite` seam that makes `max_mutants` truthful.** Adapters receive selected operators and a remaining limit; Python retains only the smallest bounded UTF-8 byte-site descriptors; collection stops at `max_mutants+1`; full mutated files and the old `Mutant`/`generate_mutants` compatibility surface are deleted. Wire identity comes directly from the syntax site, never a minimal diff of two full texts. This supersedes A-168's package-owner wording; P23 consumes the landed seam only, and P29 makes Go implement it. | The JIT tracer disproved the provisional P21 construction twice. The current adapter builds the complete tuple of full source copies before any cap can run, so a limit applied afterward is not a work/memory bound. Also, minimal text diff is not syntax-site identity: `<`→`<=` collapses to a zero-width insertion, while `True`→`False` collapses to `Tru`→`Fals`. Deferring the repair to P23 would ship two packages with a knowingly false v4 cap. Moving the already-designed seam earlier keeps one large solution-bearing package and avoids an extra orientation/review cycle. |
| A-181 | **An explicit verdict file is reserved before HEAD, adapter, or consumer-command work through `output.VerdictOutput`; lane-config failure remains earlier.** Relative targets belong to the CLI process cwd, never the project root. Reservation descriptor-walks real parents, records absent-or-regular destination identity, proves parent write access with a transient exclusive sibling probe, removes that probe before execution, and holds no visible temp across the lane. Emission revalidates identity, atomically replaces from a fresh same-parent temp, preserves any appeared/changed object, and maps expected I/O failure to process exit 2 with `OUTPUT_WRITE_FAILED`; no fallback artifact is invented. | A persistent pre-created temp inside a consumer repository can itself make P20's dirt guard fire and is observable to the command. A path-only `is_writable` check is a TOCTOU default, while pre-creating/truncating the destination destroys the old-artifact preservation contract. Holding the parent and observed destination, with create/unlink as the preflight proof, is the narrow construction that tests readiness without changing consumer state during execution. The physical output failure still cannot be written to the failed channel, so stderr/exit is the truthful terminal. |
| A-182 | **V4 assigns local grammar to model+Schema+raw verifier and cross-object/temporal relations independently to model+raw verifier; no layer is credited with a relation it cannot express.** `Mutation` has only normal arithmetic or the exact max+1 sentinel bound to `judgment.r2.max_mutants`; killed is an identity list and identities are unique across all buckets. Canary/mutant paths have a normalized slash-component grammar. Coverage capability is derived from unanimous parser evidence; mixed `None`/reported records are unreadable, and unavailable capability carries no exclusion detail. Timestamp order compares parsed offset-aware instants, not strings. V1-v3 return exactly one version diagnostic before any foreign-field check. | Draft 2020-12 has no `$data` comparison for `ended >= started`, canary-target equality, or `candidate_count == max_mutants+1`; saying “all three layers reject” would be another hollow contract. Conversely, leaving these only in producer constructors lets a raw verifier share producer trust. The canonical offset example (`12:00+01` to `11:01+00`) also proves lexicographic comparison wrong. Explicit ownership gives the acceptance engineer a different implementation path without inventing a fake schema capability. |
| A-183 | **P21 retains adapter-wide `"UNSUPPORTED"` on the bounded `generate_mutation_sites` seam and renders it as payload-free `INCONCLUSIVE/MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED`; supported empty discovery remains `INCONCLUSIVE/NO_MUTANTS` with an exact zero/zero payload.** Python never returns the marker—invalid syntax is `MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`. Go returns it unconditionally until P29 and is touched in P21 only to replace the deleted `Mutant` import/old method signature; no helper, tool declaration, syntax discovery, or R2 registration moves forward. Collection propagates the first marker without executor/scratch work and treats supported-then-unsupported behavior as a discovery error. This supersedes A-011's collapsed reason and A-114's invalid-Python use while preserving their INCONCLUSIVE/never-green and whole-adapter-call intent. | The first P21 Opus dispatch correctly BLOCKED: deleting `Mutant` while forbidding `go.py` made twelve Go test modules unimportable, and the new tuple-only API forced capability absence to masquerade as either valid zero sites or a failed analysis. Merely widening scope would leave the externally visible terminal invented. A distinct v4 reason plus absent payload preserves the fact an adapter never analysed sites, while the existing zero payload proves a supported analysis genuinely observed no candidates. The Go R2 producer remains unreachable through the registry, but keeping the adapter structurally truthful lets the migration stay atomic and gives P29 one frozen transition to real bounded sites. |

## Decided — session 29, 2026-08-09 (P22 JIT carve and proof freeze)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-184 | **P22 prepares one private committed-object seed per lane and materializes all base/replacement repositories from it; it does not re-transfer the consumer repository per repeated unit.** `SnapshotSpec` carries exact repo top, full commit, repo-relative project prefix, a caller-owned scratch root outside the consumer repository, and fixed product limits. `prepare_snapshot(..., timeout=remaining)` yields a concurrent `SnapshotRepository`; every seed read/materialization also requires P23's current remaining lane seconds, so neither package can reset or silently omit A-160's singular budget. No method works after the outer context closes. | The provisional stateless `materialize_snapshot(spec)` shape made the most convenient implementation re-enumerate and re-pack the full history for every mutant, while the opposite optimization—source alternates or hardlinks—violated isolation. The live vbpub closure at P21 is 26,074 objects / 273,578,621 uncompressed bytes even though its pack is only ~22 MiB, so this was material cost, not elegance. A prepared, unexposed seed pays source validation/transfer once; independent pack copies/reflinks with distinct inodes keep hostile consumer commands from affecting siblings. Requiring remaining time on every call gives P23 a usable enforcement seam instead of forcing it to wrap uncancellable Git work after P22 lands. |
| A-185 | **The snapshot source is a full SHA-1 reachable closure under P20's sanitized Git identity, and external/incomplete object topologies are refused.** P22 adds `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`, disables commit-graph/multi-pack-index acceleration for this trust boundary, and rejects local alternates, grafts, shallow/partial/promisor repositories, corrupt/missing objects, and non-SHA-1 formats. It freezes a full-OID inventory and uncompressed sizes before streaming exactly those OIDs through `pack-objects --stdout` (no `--revs`) into private `index-pack`, bounded by object and pack ceilings. The private seed is verified after the source is logically disconnected. | Clearing ambient `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` does not disable `.git/objects/info/alternates`; partial clones can fetch on a read; shallow/graft state changes which history “reachable” means. Any permits bytes outside the recorded source identity or a seed that silently depends on an external store. Conversely, omitting history breaks real build/version commands that inspect Git even when HEAD's file tree is present. The carver tracer proved the full-closure pack/child path in tester-unified and proved `git archive` both lacks `.git` and executes the committed hostile filter in the locked distribution. |
| A-186 | **P22 parses raw tree objects and materializes only exact regular/executable blobs plus lexically-contained relative symlinks; every other structural failure is `ERROR/GIT_FAILED`, every fixed limit is `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/SNAPSHOT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, and a replacement descriptor mismatch is `ERROR/MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED`.** Entry count and total path bytes join object/blob/pack bounds. UTF-8 newline/backslash names are preserved; any `.git` component, malformed/colliding path, unsafe symlink, gitlink, unsupported mode, or foreign object is refused before yield. A replacement path is repo-top-relative and writes one deterministic `commit-tree` child of the base with fixed `Assay <assay@invalid>`, epoch timestamp, and message; no checkout, archive, `git commit`, hook, filter, ambient identity, source write, or hardlink is permitted. | The provisional packet bounded unique objects but not tree entries: one blob may be referenced by arbitrarily many paths. It also left replacement path namespace, scratch ownership, clean index construction, and unsafe-state terminals for the implementer to invent. The closed v4 vocabulary already has truthful owners: a structurally unsafe committed repository is a Git failure, exceeding the dedicated snapshot policy is the reserved limit terminal, and a P21 mutation site whose expected bytes do not match the exact commit is a discovery-contract failure. Freezing these removes three likely “reasonable” but incompatible implementations. |
| A-187 | **P22 is READY only with the carver-owned packet under `nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/P22/`, frozen against P21 merge `678104ad32f26b9fbccdbb38b3298149a1d8f8e0` in the commit containing `reports/assay-P22-JIT-CARVE.md`.** The skeleton compiles and supplies exact constructor/timeout grammar; every still-unimplemented construction case is witnessed red; the independent tracer passes inside `tester-unified` under the validated background cgroup; the snapshot-limit v4 document passes Schema and direct raw verification; and AUTHORING's exact pre-dispatch adversarial specification review is recorded. P22 copies that document to ordinary fixtures and removes only its conformance exclusion. | P21's review explicitly routed the reserved terminal and raw-verifier independence forward. A prose packet would let a source re-read, archive/filter path, project-relative replacement, or partial snapshot satisfy its own tests. The locked source-removal, hostile config, inode, literal-manifest, unsafe-tree, limit, and deterministic-child distributions make those shortcuts observable before Opus sees the implementation. |

## Decided — session 30, 2026-08-10 (P23 JIT carve and P22 successor adjudication)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-188 | **P22's fixed replacement identity is applied last and is the cause of deterministic child commits; the closed child environment protects object/index/replace-ref location, not author/committer identity.** P23 must retain both controls and must never describe environment closure as the identity proof. | P22's first environment-merge probe returned green because `_P22_REPLACEMENT_IDENTITY` overrides ambient author/date values afterward. The identity-specific breaks were the ones that turned red. Carrying the wrong causal explanation would make a future environment refactor look safe while silently changing child OIDs. |
| A-189 | **P23's committed-snapshot state machine applies to any lane declaring R1, R2, or R3. An R0-only lane retains the existing direct execution path; this is an explicit product boundary, not a fallback after snapshot failure. Assay's registered `tester-unified` lane is permanently R0-only and is therefore the declared self-hosting exception. Every higher-rigor lane, including disposable P23 fixtures, must use P22 and must fail on P22's complete refusal set.** | The vbpub commit intentionally contains three absolute symlinks under `topos` security fixtures. P22 correctly refuses them under A-186's whole-topology contract, so the monorepo cannot be snapshotted for Assay's own R0 gate. Silently filtering those paths or weakening P22 would invalidate the security boundary. Keeping the producer's R0 self-hosting proof direct while exercising P23 through a hostile, symlink-safe committed fixture makes the exception named, narrow, and testable. It does not qualify any external project or permit higher rigor to bypass snapshots. |
| A-190 | **P23 owns closure of P21/P22's reachable `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/SNAPSHOT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` audit pair.** It copies the locked P22 complete R0-only artifact byte-for-byte into ordinary verdict fixtures, removes only that pair from `EXCLUDED_ENTIRELY`, and proves Schema, direct raw verification, and merged verification without editing their owners. | P22's controller scope correction correctly forbade the conformance file and reverted the attempted closure, leaving a real reachable terminal unowned. P23 already owns the lane refusal before baseline and has `tests/**` scope; leaving the exclusion would repeat A-141's capability-without-audit defect. |
| A-191 | **P23 treats snapshot data per tree entry and repo-top-relative path, never as an OID-to-path map.** It reads mutation source bytes through `SnapshotRepository.read_regular_file`, supplies whole expected/replacement blobs to P22, and lets P22 preserve each entry's mode and path multiplicity. It also retains P22's per-object open/close discipline and accounts scratch for copied seed packs per independent unit. | P22's review found identical sibling trees and shared blobs at multiple paths; a per-OID integration cache would either duplicate content or lose entry mode/path identity. The same review found that retaining one descriptor per path exceeded ordinary `RLIMIT_NOFILE`. P23 must consume the repaired substrate, not reintroduce either assumption while adding parallel units. |
| A-192 | **Every lane's `rigor` is an ordered subsequence of `R0,R1,R2,R3` and begins with R0; uncovered-line R3 additionally requires R1.** `R0,R2` and `R0,R1,R3` remain valid, while `R2`, `R1,R0`, and `R0,R3,R2` are load-time errors. | “R0 is present/first” still allowed a declaration order different from the runner's fixed execution and claim-construction order. That creates either an artifact-order mismatch or an implementer choice about whether declaration or implementation order wins. Canonical subsequence grammar removes both and makes the state machine directly checkable. Uncovered-line's expected cause is R1 coverage failure, so omitting R1 makes that canary impossible to judge for its declared reason. |
| A-193 | **P23 freezes one extended immutable `CommandPlan`, a required-plan `execute_plan` seam, and one shared injected `LaneDeadline`.** Higher-rigor `run_lane` resolves the plan once and passes the same object to baseline, every mutant, and both canary halves; `execute_command` remains a direct-R0 resolve-and-delegate wrapper. The deadline samples one monotonic source at lane start and supplies a newly observed positive remainder immediately before every P22/process boundary. UTC `clock` remains timestamp-only. | An optional `plan=None` lower-level API would make silent re-resolution the default on the exact paths P23 is meant to close. Reusing `lane.budget_seconds` per process multiplied the declared budget. Separating UTC evidence time from injected monotonic enforcement makes expiry deterministic in tests and prevents clock adjustment from changing a gate. |
| A-194 | **P23 accounts snapshot cost without a racy free-space oracle.** One seed is prepared; with `U` attempted units, total pack-write I/O is bounded by `(U + 1) * P22.max_pack_bytes` (one seed plus U independent copies), and peak pack space by `(1 + max(1,jobs)) * max_pack_bytes` (seed plus live children). The conservative materialized-tree bound is `max_entries * max_blob_bytes` per live child. Hardlinks remain forbidden. Actual scratch I/O failure preserves P22's `ERROR/GIT_FAILED`; no guessed minimum free-space check or fallback scratch path is added. | P22's review correctly noted O(units × pack bytes), while its contexts also let P23 keep peak live children bounded by concurrency. Omitting the persistent seed from either formula understates real scratch use. Filesystem free-space observations race allocations and cannot promise a future write; treating one as permission would be another masked default. The explicit worst-case formulas let operators size storage and tests bound lifecycle without claiming a racy preflight is authoritative. |
| A-195 | **A disposable snapshot prevents consumer writes but does not excuse a unit that changes its named Git state.** After every baseline/canary unit, and every mutant where the result is used, P23 checks dirt once and HEAD only on the clean branch against that unit's expected commit. `DIRTY_TREE`/`HEAD_CHANGED` stop later work; every child/future closes before the prepared seed, and an earlier P22 `AssayError` is never replaced by the normal-exit live-child `RuntimeError`. | Merely proving the consumer tree stayed clean lets a command modify/commit support files inside the snapshot and then have higher rigor judge a state different from the artifact's commit—the same false-PASS P20 already fixed on the live path. P22's independent review also demonstrated that cleanup ordering can mask the real terminal unless failure unwinding and normal-exit leak detection are distinguished. |
| A-196 | **P29/P30 preserve P22's per-path identity across the Go helper boundary.** Helper discovery is invoked for one repo-top-relative target path at a time; identical source bytes or shared blob OIDs at two paths remain two path-keyed target batches and two independently named mutation experiments. Neither helper caching nor P30 execution may collapse them into an OID-keyed result. | Git permits one blob at several paths and modes. P22's review found that topology in real fixtures. A language helper can safely memoize syntax facts by bytes only if the caller still reapplies them per path; using the cache key as artifact identity drops a target and makes a multi-path change appear fully tested when only one path ran. |

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-198 | **P24's Python >=3.11 offline build closure is exactly five hash-bound wheels: `setuptools==84.0.0`, `wheel==0.47.0`, `setuptools-scm==10.0.5`, `packaging==26.3`, and `vcs-versioning==2.2.4`.** All five appear verbatim in `[build-system].requires`; the gate installs the locked requirements with pip's `--require-hashes` from the committed wheelhouse before using `--no-build-isolation`. | The provisional three-package list was not a closure: wheel and setuptools-scm require packaging, while setuptools-scm requires vcs-versioning. Neither plugin nor its closure exists ambient in tester-unified, which is why the old gate silently built `0.0.0`. The provisional setuptools 82.0.1 pin also has a published vulnerability fixed in 83.0.0; greenfield distribution work must not freeze a known-vulnerable backend merely to match an older estate convention. The complete 84.0.0 closure passed the real Python 3.14 offline probe. |
| A-199 | **Gate and release builds use committed inputs without ignored residue.** The self-hosting gate makes a private no-local sparse clone at the exact reviewed OID and builds `clone/assay`; the synthetic release/no-VCS witnesses copy only Git-tracked `assay/pyproject.toml` and `assay/src/**` paths. A clean `assay-v1.2.3` build is `1.2.3`, an uncommitted tracked mutation is a distinct `.dev…+g….d…` identity, a no-VCS copy is the explicit `0.1.0` fallback, and the actual untagged monorepo build is a non-placeholder SCM development identity. | The first real probe naively copied `src/**`, then committed ignored `__pycache__` and egg-info residue into the synthetic repository; setuptools-scm's Git file finder consequently produced a wheel more than twice the correct size while both repeated builds remained reproducible. Reproducible contamination is still contamination. A clean source export/private clone makes source selection an observable fact rather than an ambient-directory assumption. |
| A-200 | **A consumable release is bound by the closed manifest `{schema_version,filename,version,sha256}` and the standalone stdlib helper in `gate/distribution/release_wheel.py`.** `manifest` creates one canonical new file from an exact release wheel; `verify` safely bounds and opens the manifest/wheel, streams sha256 before ZIP metadata, requires exactly one matching Assay METADATA member, and emits one exact PEP 508 line. Consumers pass that line to offline `pip install --require-hashes`, so pip rechecks the bytes it actually opens. P24's v1 release grammar requires a canonical PEP 440 spelling with at least a three-component release segment; source-only `0+unknown`, the no-VCS fallback, and SCM development builds are not release manifests. | A separate pre-install hash check followed by ordinary `pip install <path>` has a check/use race: bytes can change between the two opens. Feeding the independently verified identity/hash into pip's own hash mode closes that gap without making the helper an installer or adding a runtime dependency. Closed grammar, regular-file/bounds rules, duplicate METADATA rejection, and canonical output prevent the consumer from searching, guessing, or accepting two identities for one artifact. |
| A-201 | **P23 finding F8 is promoted intact into P26, not hidden in P24 distribution work.** P26 owns threading P23's one lane-wide `LaneDeadline` through `runner`/`measurability` into every in-snapshot `git.py` call, with the remaining positive budget enforced as a real process-group wall-clock deadline and no reset per Git command. | P23 truthfully covered P22 preparation and every Assay-managed test process but its existing Git evaluation calls remained byte-bounded only. Closing that requires a coordinated API change across `runner.py`, `measurability.py`, and `git.py`; it is not a minor P24 packaging correction. P26 already owns the next broad sanitized Git extension and comes before external adoption, so folding F8 there avoids a new handoff while preserving serial ownership and one deadline source. |

## Decided — session 34, 2026-08-10 (P26 JIT carve and proof freeze)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-209 | **Tier-3 evidence is independent of computed rigor in the lane grammar as well as the verdict. `judge.attestation_dir` and the nonempty ordered `judge.evidence` array form one both-present-or-both-absent pair on any lane, including R0-only. An R0-only lane still refuses every computed judge field.** This supersedes A-048/A-062 and DESIGN-GUIDE §12 only for this consumed external-evidence pair; an R0 lane with no evidence still has no `[judge]`. | The provisional P26 grammar placed evidence under HOW while the landed loader prohibited every judge field on R0. Restricting attestations to R1+ would contradict A-074's separate axes and make Assay's permanently R0-only self-hosting exception unable to declare review evidence. Moving the pair to top level would contradict D7's WHAT/HOW split. The narrow exception preserves both boundaries and keeps inert computed policy illegal. |
| A-210 | **Attestation input is one closed, staged, descriptor-safe batch.** `attestation_dir` is canonical project-relative POSIX spelling bounded to 4,096 UTF-8 bytes and 128 components; evidence keys are ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,63}`; records are closed JSON with duplicate member names and non-UTF-8-encodable strings refused, plus the fixed full-OID/path/producer bounds. `safeio.read_bounded_input` treats `ENOENT` at any component as absent and every symlink/type/permission/size defect as unreadable. All files are read/parsed before Git, and `2 * sum(valid reviewed_paths) <= 4,096` is enforced before the first query; on excess, every otherwise-valid record is unreadable while already-missing/malformed identities retain their own result. The loader samples the lane remainder at entry, after every bounded read/parse, and before return even when all records are missing. | P20's existing reader maps a missing parent to unreadable, while a declaration whose producer supplied no directory is the same absence as a missing final file. Path precheck/reopen is racy. Ordinary JSON parsing also silently chooses a value for duplicate identity fields and lone surrogates evade a nominal UTF-8 byte limit. Without a post-read sample an all-missing batch can consume the deadline yet return ordinary missing evidence. The provisional packet bounded a path's bytes but not directory traversal depth, and called 4,096 reviewed identities “Git comparisons” although each consumes both `ls-tree` and `diff`. Staging gives the aggregate ceiling real before-Git meaning and preserves per-identity diagnostics without order-dependent partial Git work. |
| A-211 | **Attestation currentness uses four narrow sanitized Git operations with a required `Remaining`: exact full-commit verification, `merge-base --is-ancestor`, one raw exact `ls-tree -z` blob/tree identity per reviewed path, and one literal `diff --quiet --exit-code` per path. The helpers retain P20's generic boundary and global `--literal-pathspecs`; no bespoke child may omit it.** Structural/unresolvable/unrelated/path-missing Git results become that identity's `ERROR/UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`; diff exit 1 alone is `NO_MEASUREMENT/STALE_ATTESTATION`; `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/LANE_TIMEOUT` is never remapped. Within each record, every existence query precedes any staleness query; records are then resolved in declaration order. | The old newline/display-name set gives a changed reviewed directory PASS and cannot represent every Git filename. Exact per-path queries make directory descendants and literal metacharacter/newline names Git's question, not Assay's decoder. A decoy that matches a reviewed filename as a pathspec makes “literal” independently observable. Validating every path in one claim first prevents an early stale path from hiding a later path that never existed, while retaining Tier-3's independent-result rule and declaration-order resolution. |
| A-212 | **One CLI-started `LaneDeadline` spans every lane-owned Git/process boundary, including initial HEAD resolution, Tier-3 evidence, direct R0, and higher rigor.** CLI starts it after lane/output validation and passes the same bound method through generic Git bootstrap and substantive calls. `run_lane(deadline=None)` is only a library convenience that derives and starts the same object immediately; it never omits enforcement. Lower non-lane Git callers may explicitly have `remaining=None`. Generic Git children start a new session; expiry kills the full process group even if the direct child has already exited while a descendant retains a pipe, boundedly drains/closes, reaps, re-raises the original timeout object, and launches no successor. P22's already-required per-operation `timeout=deadline.remaining()` seam is unchanged. This supersedes A-189 only where it said direct R0 has no `LaneDeadline`; direct R0 still has no snapshot. | P26 would otherwise add many unbounded Git calls to the exact R0 lane Assay self-hosts, and CLI's existing pre-run `head_rev` already sat outside P23's later deadline. Beginning the clock inside the higher-rigor scratch context also excluded repo identity, cleanliness, and base resolution. A callable avoids a `git -> runner` import and prevents command-by-command duration resets; group cleanup independent of direct-child state closes the witnessed descendant-held-pipe hang. |
| A-213 | **CLI resolves the declared attestation batch once, before adapter/command work, and threads the resulting ordered declarations/evidence through every normal and refused verdict. At entry, `run_lane` and `refuse_lane` derive the authoritative ordered identities from `lane.judge.evidence` and require exact equality with both supplied tuples before any Git/plan/adapter/command work; `assemble_verdict` repeats the binding at the final public sink. Their empty defaults are legal only when that source is empty.** Missing/malformed/stale evidence does not skip computed work. An attestation deadline is atomic: it launches no adapter/command and emits every computed claim and every declared evidence identity as payload-free `BUDGET_EXCEEDED/LANE_TIMEOUT`; a later runner timeout preserves the already-resolved evidence and refuses the affected computed work. A timeout before HEAD remains the existing no-artifact pre-identity terminal. | Adapter refusal must not erase already-declared external evidence, and a bad review must not prevent Assay from recording the computed evidence it did obtain. An unchecked empty tuple at a runner or assembly boundary is a shadowing default because it can substitute for declarations already known from the lane; entry binding fails before effects, sink binding protects direct assembly callers, and empty remains correct for old no-evidence calls without mechanical churn. Conversely, continuing after the singular budget expired or converting timeout to unreadable would lie about the root cause. Atomic batch failure supplies the schema's exact evidence coverage without presenting a partially completed batch as complete. |
| A-214 | **P26 is READY only with the carver-owned packet under `nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/P26/` and `reports/assay-P26-JIT-CARVE.md` at the Sol freeze commit.** The packet pins input `233926cedd26a6e34512806e267b7141377913b2`, exact interfaces/bounds/lifecycle, four complete v4 templates, a controlled-red 41-test quick suite, and the premise probe. Implementers/reviewers may not edit it; the registered gate runs the locked suite from the installed wheel and emits `ASSAY_GATE_PHASE=attestation-hardened`. | Current code reproduces both named false PASSes, maps absent parent as unreadable, leaves a descendant outside generic Git's ownership, accepts duplicate/lone-surrogate record strings, and exposes omission-prone evidence flow. A prose-only handoff would again let config placement, missing semantics, aggregate work, timeout precedence, process cleanup, JSON identity, source binding, and installed-vs-source execution be independently invented by implementation and tests. Locked independent expectations make those choices visible and reviewable before dispatch. |

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-215 | **B001 becomes a Sol design/probe checkpoint after P28 and before P29. Its preferred hypothesis is a source-oriented SQL/DDL adapter/helper that returns bounded byte-span `MutationSite`s over changed tracked DDL; Assay applies them through the existing immutable snapshot and command-plan pipeline, while the project command alone owns fresh test-database provisioning, DSNs, and cleanup. Assay and its adapter never connect to or mutate a live database.** The checkpoint must settle language-qualified operators, R2-without-R1 protocol shape, source-to-schema identity, database-isolation proof, and any truthful R3 mechanism before carving. If source mutation cannot represent the deployed schema, a reusable external PostgreSQL mutation auditor plus Tier-2 adjudicated evidence is the fallback. | SQL fits the same architectural boundary as Python and Go only when the adapter learns syntax while the core retains source identity, bounds, isolation, and execution. The original catalog-introspection proposal does not map live catalog rows to changed source spans and would make an adapter an environment tool. Deferring the checkpoint until real Go R1/srdm qualification preserves the current validation goal; holding it before P29 avoids freezing a second-language mutation/operator contract that SQL would immediately force us to revisit. |

## Decided — session 36, 2026-08-10 (wave re-platform after Codex withdrawal)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-216 | **The remaining wave (P27, P28, the B001 carve, P29–P32) runs Claude-only under `WAVE-CONTROLLER-PROMPT.md`, which supersedes `FROZEN-WAVE-CONTROLLER-PROMPT.md`.** Four changes: (1) the carver is `C-sol-1`, a long-lived Opus xhigh Claude Code session, replacing the withdrawn Codex thread `C-sol-0`; (2) the controller is `L-assay-wave-1` at Sonnet **high**, and it keeps the registered gate and the serial `--no-ff` merge — the carver never ships; (3) frozen implementer/reviewer bases are retired in favour of a fresh child per package, while `bases.yaml` and `invocations.jsonl` history stay immutable and telemetry collection continues; (4) **every carve gets a mandatory pre-dispatch adversarial specification review** by a fresh Opus xhigh child forked from a new frozen `CR-opus-0` orientation, commissioned and adjudicated by the controller rather than by the carver, using AUTHORING's prompt verbatim, with NOT READY routing back to the carver and blocking dispatch. This decision also records, rather than silently loses, the fact that carver, implementer, and reviewer are now the same provider and model family. | Codex/GPT withdrawal removed both the carver thread and the property WORKFLOW.md names as the highest-yield act in this project: adversarial review by a *different frontier model*, which found 23 defects in an earlier carve that a same-family reviewer had passed. That independence axis cannot be recovered inside one provider, so it is replaced by role isolation that is actually enforceable — blind-first phase 1, a fresh child per package, and a carve reviewer the carver may neither commission nor overrule. A carver that reads its own reviewer's verdict and decides which findings bind is grading its own homework, which is why the controller owns that leg. Mandatory carve review is aimed at the observed failure mode, not a hypothetical one: P25 and P26 each reached the registered gate mechanically blocked by a carver-side defect (an out-of-scope `assay.toml` budget coupling, then a gate phase-order defect), both of which a specification review reads directly off the handoff before an implementer is paid for. Retiring the frozen bases costs the measured ~128k-per-child orientation saving, but its comparison arm is gone and its complexity — base creation, rotation, TTL keepalive, experiment allocation — was most of the controller's size and the reason it could not run below high effort. Telemetry continues because the P20–P26 warm-fork rows are uninterpretable without the fresh-child baseline they never had; every fresh dispatch from now on supplies it. |

## Decided — session 37, 2026-08-11 (A-O19 ruling and A-172 closure)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-217 | **A-O19 is ruled: option 2. Assay obtains real Go statement positions from a source-side oracle rather than deriving them from cover-block extents.** The operator's ruling, not the carver's selection. Consequences that land with it: (a) P27 must be re-carved around the oracle and is not dispatchable until then; (b) `src/assay/coverage_parsers/go_cover.py` gains an explicit scope status — as frozen it appeared only under "Context to read first", which silently permitted option 1 alone and structurally blocked options 2 and 3; (c) O3's negative is rewritten, being self-contradicting under one reading and unfalsifiable under the other; (d) any line-attribution oracle is checked against `carve-assets/P27/witness/seg.go` and the `collision-colA/colB` pair, not only the original four witnesses; (e) the helper-protocol boundary A-163 assigned to P29 now reaches back into P27, which is an accepted cost of the ruling rather than a scope violation to be discovered later. A-087 does **not** transfer as a counter-precedent: P08's hand-written Go lexer was permissible because `has_executable_code` may be conservative — it returns strict `true` on anything it cannot parse, so a wrong answer is a false FAIL that fails closed — whereas a statement position must be exact, and a wrong one is a wrong published verdict with no fail-closed direction. | The evidence is an impossibility proof, not a preference. Two `gofmt`-clean files (`carve-assets/P27/witness/collision-colA.go`, `collision-colB.go`) compile and execute under the pinned `go1.25.12` and emit byte-identical profiles (`f6c593a961f7…4da8`) while their statements begin on different lines — `{4,6}` versus `{4,5}`. A rule is a function of the profile; identical input forces identical output; the two correct answers differ; so every profile-only rule is wrong on at least one. Option 1 would therefore have shipped a knowingly-wrong line attribution, and its cost is not confined to the permissive direction the first carve named: a brace- or comment-only diff inside an untested function reports 0/1 and FAILs at `fail_under = 100.0` where statement truth is 0/0, which consumers will meet far more often than the false-PASS case. Option 3 is a v5 schema migration owned by P21 and forbidden here (A-138/A-170). Option 2 is also what rescues P28: covergate shares the inclusive convention, so binding P28's tuple at block granularity would make the two implementations agree *by construction* — satisfying A-208 in form while defeating it in substance, and institutionalizing precisely the circularity A-172 exists to prevent. Real statement positions make the hand manifest comparable at statement granularity, where A-208 always intended the shared tuple to be checked. Implementation note for the re-carve: `golang.org/x/tools/cover`'s public `Profile.Boundaries()` only interpolates byte offsets between block positions and does no AST work; the algorithm that *produces* these blocks is `cmd/cover`'s own instrumenter (golang/go `src/cmd/cover/cover.go`, BSD-3-Clause, `Visit`/`addCounters`/`statementBoundary`), which is deterministic and reproduces the same blocks byte-for-byte — retaining each statement's position instead of discarding it into a count is a small adaptation. Adapt, do not invent. |
| A-218 | **A-172's stated premise is disproved and closed: an end-column-1 fixture does not distinguish inclusive from half-open expansion, and the position half of A-172 has no observable consequence.** `go_cover.py`'s existing `range(start, end + 1)` is correct under either reading, and no freeze of the *position* grammar is owed. What A-172 correctly required — that the mapping be probed against real bytes rather than adopted from the current parser or srdm — is discharged by the P27 probe and by A-217. | An end column of 1 requires a block to end at a token in column 1. `Rbrace + 1` is always ≥ 2; `if`/`for`/`range`/`switch`/`select`/function bodies cannot have their `{` begin a line because Go's semicolon insertion forbids it; every other block end is `stmt.End()`, column ≥ 2. That leaves only a bare block statement's `{` at column 1 — reproduced in `carve-assets/P27/witness/edge.go`, and requiring source `gofmt` rewrites — and that identical position always begins the successor block, so the line is attributed either way. Verified mechanically: inclusive and half-open produce identical line sets on every witness. Recording the closure matters because A-172 framed the whole question as inclusive-versus-half-open, and a future reader who reopened that axis would spend the effort to rediscover that it is inert while the real defect — block extent cannot express statement identity — went unaddressed. |

## Decided — session 54, 2026-08-16 (wave 1 carve: branch coverage, whole-target judge, verdict schema v6 — B005/B006)

Recorded from `nyxloom-trove/W1-CARVE-branch-coverage-and-whole-target.md` §0,
"The rulings this wave rests on". A-257 through A-263 are operator decisions
taken 2026-08-16. A-264 and A-265 are **not** operator decisions: both were
forced by the independent `codex gpt-5.6-sol` high-effort pre-dispatch review
of the carve (Addendum B, findings 5 and 2/3 respectively), which returned
"NOT READY — 9 blocking, 2 non-blocking" against the worktree at `af918715`;
the operator accepted the review's findings into the carve rather than
proposing them. **A-266 is a third kind again:** it was ruled UPSTREAM, in
`main` `c7bc9b59` on 2026-08-17, while this wave was already implementing
against the previous requirement — it supersedes a design this very carve had
specified and had begun to build.

**A-269 is a fourth kind, and the rarest: a ruling taken because the previous
ruling's design would not converge.** A-266/A-267/A-268 describe a
project-prefix-plus-`inputs` boundary that was reviewed three times and got
worse each round (8 → 9 → 11 blocking). A-269 does not patch it; it withdraws
its public contract and replaces it with a smaller one, reached by handing a
fresh carver the consumer problem instead of the specification. The rows above
are kept unamended rather than rewritten, because the measurements inside them
— the skip-worktree mechanism, the no-sandbox honesty, the restore-is-possible
narrowing — all survived into A-269 and are the reason it is shaped as it is.
What was discarded was the contract, not the evidence.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-257 | **The coverage model gains branch data, in all four formats.** A format that genuinely cannot express branch arcs declares that fact rather than being omitted from the work: `go-cover`'s branch capability is an explicit, tested `"unavailable"`. | A Go cover profile carries statement counts and no arcs at all; faking one would be the exact `declared_unverified`-class lie A-O12 was corrected for. |
| A-258 | **Branch coverage is judged whenever the artifact reports it. Not opt-in.** A changed line that is a branch source with an untaken arc lowers the percentage in every lane whose artifact carries branch data. | This changes what PASS means for existing R1 lanes, which is precisely why it lands with a schema major bump and not before. |
| A-259 | **`judge.require_branch` (default `false`) governs ABSENCE, never presence.** With it `true`, an artifact whose branch capability is `"unavailable"` is refused (`NO_MEASUREMENT`/`BRANCH_UNAVAILABLE`) instead of being judged on lines alone. | It exists because the alternative is a silent rigor downgrade: drop `--cov-branch` from the argv and a line+branch gate quietly becomes a line gate that still says PASS. |
| A-260 | **The whole-target judge is a MODE of R1, not a new rigor level.** One lane declares one mode. A consumer wanting both a changed-line gate and a whole-module floor declares two lanes. | Inventing an R1.5 or a second R1 claim would break `_check_claims_cover_declared_rigor`'s one-claim-per-level contract for no capability gain. |
| A-261 | **Verdict schema v6, hard cut.** `assay verify` refuses a v5 document exactly as it refuses v4 today. No dual-version verifier, no compatibility shim. The producer emits v6 only. | The major bump A-258 requires (branch judging changes what PASS means for existing R1 lanes) is also the moment A-262's rename can land honestly; no exception is carved for a v5/v6 dual-read the way none exists for v4/v5. |
| A-262 | **`changed_executable` is renamed to `executable` in the v6 coverage payload.** This is the one rename v6 takes. | Under the whole-target mode the denominator is a target's executable lines, which were not "changed" by anything; keeping the old name would put a false statement on the wire. It is taken now because a major bump is the only honest moment for it. |
| A-263 | **`pct` is the COMBINED line+branch percentage:** `(covered + branches_covered) / (executable + branches_total)`, exactly `coverage.py`'s own `summary.percent_covered` under `--cov-branch`. `covered` and `executable` stay line-only; the branch side gets its own two integers (`branches_covered`, `branches_total`), so an independent consumer can still re-derive `pct` from the payload alone. When branch capability is `"unavailable"`, `branches_total` is 0 and the formula degenerates to today's line-only value with no special case. | The METRIC is identical to coverage.py's; the COMPARISON is not, and the difference is stated rather than glossed: coverage.py compares a value rounded to the configured precision (`round(total, precision) < fail_under`), assay compares the unrounded value, which is stricter and precision-independent. At the floor the consumer actually uses they agree — measured: `coverage report --fail-under=100` over a 99.60% module printed `99%` and exited 2. |
| A-264 | **Forced by the independent pre-dispatch review, not the operator.** R1 records its policy whenever R1 was ATTEMPTED, for exactly two new terminals: `judgment.r1` is present iff the R1 claim carries a coverage payload OR the R1 claim's reason code is `BRANCH_UNAVAILABLE` or `TARGET_NOT_MEASURED` — a CLOSED set of two, mirroring R2's existing widening for `MUTATION_UNSUPPORTED` (A-183, `verdict.py:2256`: "R2 policy is recorded whenever R2 was actually ATTEMPTED, which is one case wider than rendered a payload"). Every other payload-free R1 terminal — `DIRTY_TREE`, `BASE_IS_HEAD`, `GIT_FAILED`, `EMPTY_COVERAGE`, `FORMAT_MISMATCH`, `UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` — keeps today's rule (`verdict.py:2242`: "judgment.r1 is present but no R1 claim rendered a coverage payload") and must have a negative test proving it. | Addendum B finding 5: "the two new payload-free refusals could not record the policy they refused under." Without this widening, `BRANCH_UNAVAILABLE` and `TARGET_NOT_MEASURED` cannot record which target was attempted or which floor was asked for, which would gut B005's whole reason for existing — a rigor decision invisible to the verdict is the thing it replaces. |
| A-265 | **Forced by the independent pre-dispatch review, not the operator.** An artifact's branch DETAIL is authoritative over its capability METADATA (coverage.py's `meta.branch_coverage`, Cobertura's root `branches-valid`); any disagreement between metadata and detail is `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`, never a silent resolution in either direction. Arc identities are validated for uniqueness and executed/missing disjointness BEFORE they are aggregated into per-line counts (see §3.1a and §3.2 of the carve). | Addendum B findings 2 and 3. The obvious alternative — "metadata decides" — has a false-PASS hole: an artifact with `meta` absent or `branch_coverage: false` but real arc arrays present would be read as `"unavailable"`, its branch evidence silently dropped, and with `require_branch = false` the lane would report a line-only PASS over an artifact that had measured branches all along. Aggregating `(covered, total)` per line throws arc identities away, so a duplicated covered arc that inflates the numerator while the stated totals are tampered to match would be undetectable after aggregation — the same reasoning `FileCoverage` already applies to executed/missing line arrays as three independent arrays from adversarial input (`model.py:47`). |
| A-266 | **Ruled upstream in `main` `c7bc9b59`, 2026-08-17, superseding this wave's own first design.** A lane declares its snapshot boundary AFFIRMATIVELY — `[lanes.X.isolation] snapshot_scope = "repository" \| "project"`, with `boundary_prefix` (renamed from `project_prefix` by A-268's amendment) plus an explicit `inputs` list under project scope — and assay materialises only that boundary. **The `allow_escaping_symlinks` allowlist this carve had specified for B006(a) is WITHDRAWN, not deferred**, together with the alternative of scoping the snapshot walk to `source_roots`. The effective scope, prefix and canonical expanded input set become a REQUIRED v6 attestation. Preflight refuses when any source root, coverage artifact, mutation candidate, canary target or command working directory falls outside the boundary, and the boundary is never broadened automatically. | The backlog's own words: "source roots do not include every test dependency, and an ignore list only hides a path from validation without proving the executed command cannot reach it." An allowlist is a promise about a path assay still puts in the tree the command runs against; a declared boundary is a statement about which paths exist at all, and only the second is provable or attestable. The requirement is not hypothetical: CMRU's higher-rigor lane cannot run at all today because assay refuses Topos's tracked `topos/tests/fixtures/inspect_files/_danger/passwd_link -> /etc/passwd` before CMRU's command starts, and CMRU has bounded itself to R0 in writing (`cmru/assay.toml`, `4b8009d5`) rather than route around it. B006's estimate moved `small` → `large` with this ruling. It lands in the SAME v6 bump as A-257–A-265, which is a saving rather than a coincidence: the attestation the backlog requires needs a schema version, and paying the 43-fixture migration and the locked-suite deselection twice would be the only alternative. |
| A-267 | **The boundary is a MATERIALISATION boundary, not a confidentiality sandbox — and the artifact says so.** Out-of-scope paths are never written into the working tree the command runs in, so nothing can open, follow or execute them; that is the whole property, and it is exactly what unblocks CMRU (Topos's `passwd_link -> /etc/passwd` is never created). Explicitly NOT claimed: the retained object closure still yields out-of-scope committed bytes to `git show` from inside the snapshot, and assay does not confine the process at all. A mount-namespace/Landlock sandbox is **rejected, not deferred**. Consequences ruled with it: `snapshot_scope` is REQUIRED on every R1+ lane and refused on R0-only ones (no default, and no inference from where `assay.toml` sits); the attestation gains a closed `materialisation` state so it cannot describe a boundary that never ran; one immutable `ResolvedSnapshotBoundary` is built once and shared by materialiser, preflight and verdict producer; and the backlog's "removing an input causes deterministic preflight failure" oracle is replaced by the command itself failing on the absent file. | Forced by the second independent adversarial review (13 blocking findings), whose central point was that the design promised what the substrate cannot deliver. Verified, not accepted on assertion: each snapshot receives the complete seed pack and a `read-tree` of the whole commit (`isolation.py:478/500/674`), so `git show HEAD:<path>` reads out-of-scope bytes and `skip-worktree` is a status bit rather than access control; the consumer command is a bare `subprocess.run(env=..., cwd=...)` (`runner.py:201`) with no namespace and no process group; assay's `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS`/no-hooks protections cover only assay's own Git children (`git.py:116`); and CMRU's own argv starts `/opt/tester-venv/bin/python`, outside every snapshot — so the strong claim was never true of any real lane. DESIGN-GUIDE §7/A-030 put the sandbox permanently outside this library; it belongs to the execution environment. Inference of scope from the `assay.toml` location was considered and rejected: it is the ambient discovery B006 forbids, and it would silently re-scope every existing R1+ consumer, whose `assay.toml` files all sit in subdirectories. |
| A-268 | **The boundary's guarantee is "assay never materialises an out-of-scope path" — and the command CAN restore one.** Narrowing A-267 again after a third review measured it: the snapshot is an ordinary repository holding the complete closure (`isolation.py:486`) with a full-commit index (`isolation.py:500`), so a `git checkout` after clearing a skip bit, or a `git worktree add`, recreates the excluded file — and a suite running `git stash`/`git worktree add` in a fixture can hit that by accident. Ruled with it: the mechanism IS a full index plus `skip-worktree` outside the boundary, named rather than left open; `project_prefix` need only CONTAIN the project root (the "must equal" rule is dropped); the runner preflight is defence-in-depth over a public API rather than the thing keeping the boundary honest; and the selection walk does not descend INTO an out-of-scope subtree, so a gitlink or unsupported mode inside one no longer fails every lane (an entry beside the boundary at a traversed level still refuses — deciding scope requires decoding the name). **AMENDED 2026-08-17 by the next review round, twice.** (a) The TOML key is **`boundary_prefix`**, not `project_prefix`: `SnapshotSpec.project_prefix` (`isolation.py:148`) and `refuse_lane(project_prefix=…)` (`runner.py:867`) already mean the project's own repo-relative path, which this ruling deliberately allows to DIFFER from the boundary, and three meanings for one name is how A-145's next incident starts. It must CONTAIN the project root — the prefix specifically, not the boundary as a whole, or an `inputs` entry could satisfy it while the attested prefix named an unrelated tree. (b) The clause originally ending this row — "the two pre-repository refusal paths record a module-level constant" — is **WITHDRAWN**: that constant is false on the only path that can reach it, since the adapter refusal (`cli.py:385`) is reachable only for R1+ lanes, which are exactly the lanes required to declare a scope. Every producer path derives the recorded boundary FROM THE LANE, which `refuse_lane` already receives and which `assemble_verdict` already does for `scope`/`enforcement`. | Preventing the restore needs either a pruned object closure — which B006.3 explicitly forbids ("retain the complete resolved commit, object closure, base resolution, and provenance") and which would break base resolution and mutation targeting — or a process sandbox, which A-030 and DESIGN-GUIDE §7 put permanently outside this library. So the honest move is to state the narrower property and test exactly it. The dropped "prefix must equal the project path" rule was worse than useless: it made every downstream containment refusal unreachable from any loadable `assay.toml` (`config.py:1280`/`:998`/`_load_canary` already guarantee containment) in a project that forbids `pragma: no cover`, and it could not be enforced at load anyway because `config.py` imports no git. The mechanism ruling is measured, not preferred: narrowing the index makes `git status` report a deletion for every omitted entry, which `_verify` (`isolation.py:590`) and the post-run dirt check (`runner.py:1120`) both refuse, and makes `write-tree` (`isolation.py:510`) build mutant commits that delete the rest of the monorepo. |
| A-269 | **B006(a) ships an explicit repository snapshot policy with exact, commit-validated omissions of P22-unsafe symlink leaves, not a project-prefix-plus-inputs boundary.** The policy is a materialisation selection, never a statement about process reachability. A-266's required `snapshot_scope = "project"`, `boundary_prefix`, `inputs`, expanded input attestation, and five boundary-containment preflights are **withdrawn**. The `snapshot_scope` key itself is replaced by **`snapshot_selection`**, closed to `"repository"` \| `"repository-minus-unsafe-symlinks"`; A-268's amendment (a), which selected the `boundary_prefix` spelling, is **moot** with that prefix's withdrawal. A-267's no-sandbox ruling and A-268's full-index-plus-`skip-worktree` mechanism **remain binding** in their narrower applicable form. Verdict v6 records the selected policy and its exact declared omissions, **not** an execution-phase/materialisation-state enum. If N×M maintenance later becomes material, the known escalation path is a new `snapshot_selection` enum value for a commit-owned repository policy under its own ruling; its known trap is that commit-derived omissions are unavailable to early verdict producers, so it must solve that derivability problem rather than reintroduce the underivable-value defect. The design of record is `nyxloom-trove/W1-CARVE-B006a-project-scope.md`; §1 of `W1-CARVE-branch-coverage-and-whole-target.md` is dead. | **The contract A-266 mandated failed three consecutive independent adversarial reviews at 8 → 9 → 11 blocking findings — diverging, not converging**, with five of the last round's eleven being defects introduced by, or left behind by, an earlier round's accepted fix. B006(a) was therefore taken back to the consumer problem by a fresh carver with explicit licence to change the shape, which the backlog's own words permit: "Exact TOML names are Assay's design decision", and the skip-worktree index is "**one possible** implementation". The new shape DISSOLVES the recurring defect classes rather than answering them. There is no dependency inventory to be incomplete, because everything except the named leaves is still materialised — so CMRU's measured repository-root reads (`cmru.project.sample.toml`, and `cmru.release.sh`, which `test_release_wrapper.py` actually executes) need no `inputs` key at all. There are no five containment preflights unreachable from any loadable `assay.toml` in a project that forbids `pragma: no cover`, only the one reachable coverage-artifact/omission collision. And there is no enum whose values no call site can distinguish, because the policy is lane config copied by `assemble_verdict`, hence derivable on every early producer path including `cli.py`'s two refusals. The vacuity hole B005 exists to close stays structurally shut: an omission must be a mode-`120000` leaf whose target P22 would otherwise refuse, so it can never hide a source file, a test, a mutation candidate or a B005 target. The first independent review of the new shape returned **READY WITH CORRECTIONS (5 blocking, 6 non-blocking) — converged** — and, given explicit licence to design a better shape, argued the case against and kept this one: every zero-maintenance alternative reopens silent evidence broadening, concretely a commit that swaps a quietly-dead source module for a tracked `/etc/passwd` symlink and still renders `PASS`; a pre-declared count misses renames; a digest is the path list minus readability; and a commit-owned repository policy file makes the omission set commit content, unavailable to the early producers. The one attack that partially lands is ownership inversion — a consumer's lane names paths in a tree it does not own — bought back by requiring the undeclared-link refusal to name the exact declarable spelling, and by an author-side inventory test recommended to the monorepo. Two premise corrections were measured on the way, both changing the work: Topos carries **three** tracked absolute `/etc/passwd` symlinks, not the one the backlog and A-266 name; and CMRU's suite is **not** green under the gate's `--network=none`, because `test_release_publish_rejects_response_without_upload_coordinate` stubs only `get_release_by_tag`, leaving `publish()` to call the unstubbed `update_release` against `api.github.com`. |
| A-270 | **Three documents, one job each, and no capability merges without them.** **`README.md` says WHAT assay does** — the user-facing feature surface. **`docs/DESIGN-GUIDE.md` says WHY it does it that way** — choices, rejected alternatives, implementation reasoning. **`docs/CONSUMERS.md` says HOW to adopt it** — worked examples and use cases for a real consumer. A README feature LINKS to its DESIGN-GUIDE section rather than re-arguing the rationale there, and CONSUMERS.md shows a lane a consumer could paste rather than describing one. **Any work item that adds, removes or changes a user-facing capability, a public config key, a closed vocabulary value, or a compatibility fact is INCOMPLETE until all three are in sync, and that sync lands BEFORE the merge, not after it.** Three mechanical checks make this a test rather than an intention, and every one of them must be able to fail: (a) **every TOML example in all three documents parses with the SHIPPED loader** and declares the current `LANE_SCHEMA_VERSION`; (b) **every value of every closed public vocabulary a consumer must type** — `isolation.snapshot_selection`, `judge.mode`, the rigor levels, the coverage `format` registry — **appears in at least one of the three documents**, so a capability cannot ship undocumented; (c) **every DESIGN-GUIDE anchor the README links to resolves.** A documentation example is a claim like any other and is held to A-232: it is evidence only if something executed it. |
| A-271 | **Canonical-spelling strictness is required where a duplicate can INFLATE a score, and not elsewhere. The two path grammars differ on purpose.** `judge.targets` and `isolation.unsafe_symlink_omissions` refuse non-canonical spellings outright (`./x`, `x//y`, `x/`, interior `.`); `safeio.reserve_output` accepts and normalises them for the coverage artifact and for CLI-supplied destinations. **This asymmetry is ruled correct and is not to be "fixed" into uniformity.** | Raised as an open question by the v6-cut implementer, having been measured by the controller as pre-existing and unchanged by B006(b). The instinct is that one product should speak one path grammar, and A-145 gets cited for it — but A-145 requires every boundary to say WHICH SPELLING it speaks (project-relative vs repo-top-relative), not that every boundary reject non-canonical forms. The strictness has a specific job, stated in §5 of the wave carve: `targets` is a LIST that is AGGREGATED, so `src/good.py` and `src//good.py` are two strings naming one file, a raw-string uniqueness check accepts both, the schema's `uniqueItems` accepts both, and evaluation then counts a well-covered target TWICE — raising the aggregate enough to carry a poorly covered sibling over the floor. That is a false PASS reachable from a plausible typo. `unsafe_symlink_omissions` is strict for the same structural reason (a declared list, compared for exactness against the index skip set). **The coverage artifact has no such exposure**: it is exactly one path, aggregated with nothing, and `PurePosixPath` normalisation is lexical with no traversal risk — every escaping form (`..`, absolute, symlinked component) is still refused loudly. Tightening it would also reach the CLI's `--verdict-json`, where `./out.json` is idiomatic and refusing it would be user-hostile for no safety gain. Measured before ruling: no live lane in the estate uses a `./` spelling today, so this is a deliberate choice rather than a compatibility excuse. |
| A-272 | **coverage.py's per-file `summary.num_branches`/`covered_branches` are NOT the cardinality of its own arc arrays, so wave 1's equality cross-check is WITHDRAWN.** `_check_branch_summary` in `coverage_parsers/coverage_py_json.py` refuses an artifact whose `summary.num_branches` differs from `sum(total for by_line)` or whose `summary.covered_branches` differs from the derived covered count. Measured against **real coverage.py 7.15.4**, both equalities are false on ordinary output: `topos/src/topos/ui/banner.py` reports `num_branches=108` while listing 106 arcs, and `covered_branches=2` while its `executed_branches` array is **empty**. Two of 94 files in one real artifact. Ruled: **the arrays are authoritative for per-line branch DETAIL, the summary is the tool's own aggregate, and NO equality between them is asserted.** The **arc-identity invariants STAY** — uniqueness within each array, and executed/missing disjointness — because those are properties of the arrays themselves rather than of a cross-tool agreement, and they are what actually catch a tampered artifact. A-265 is untouched: its detail-over-metadata rule governs *capability* (`meta.branch_coverage` versus the presence of real arc detail), a different question from per-file totals. | Found by the wave's own acceptance test, and it is a **false refusal of a valid artifact**, not a caught tamper: a real, tool-produced coverage.py file was rendered `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`, which is a hard refusal, so every consumer whose codebase contains the shape would have been unable to run R1 at all. Confirmed to be a wave-1 regression rather than a pre-existing condition by running the identical scenario against `main`'s assay, which parsed it and returned the expected `FAIL`/`UNCOVERED_LINES`. The invariant was written to catch an artifact whose stated totals were tampered to match a duplicated arc; it was never measured against the reference tool's real output, and the eight fixtures it was validated on were too small to contain the shape. The lesson generalises past this row: **an invariant asserted about another tool's output is a claim about that tool, and needs the same measurement discipline as a claim about our own** — the wave's own carve says a rule that cannot be witnessed in a real artifact is a rule the contract got wrong. |
| A-273 | **A-263's "exactly `coverage.py`'s own `percent_covered`" is AMENDED, because A-272 measured it false.** assay derives `branches_covered`/`branches_total` from the arc arrays, never from `summary`. Where coverage.py counts a branch as covered but does **not enumerate it** in `executed_branches`, assay cannot attribute it to a line and therefore does not credit it. The corrected claim: assay's `pct` is **identical** to coverage.py's whenever the arc arrays enumerate every branch the summary counts — the ordinary case, witnessed by all eight frozen fixtures — and **strictly LOWER otherwise**, by exactly the branches the tool declined to enumerate. Measured: `banner.py` gives coverage.py `2/108` ≈ 1.852% against assay's `0/106` = 0%; a scaled reproduction through the real parser gives coverage.py 60.0% against assay 50.0%. **assay is never HIGHER**, so the divergence always fails closed. This is a documented consumer-visible fact under A-270, not an internal detail: a consumer whose file has this shape will see a lower percentage from assay than `coverage report` prints, and must be able to find out why without reading the parser. | The alternative — take the aggregate from `summary` so the numbers match — was considered and rejected. assay judges what it can **attribute**: a branch nobody enumerated cannot be tied to a line, so crediting it would mean asserting coverage of a line assay cannot name, which is the same class of unattributed claim `unclassified_lines` exists to prevent. It would also be unusable in the mode that matters most, since `pct` is computed over the JUDGED subset — the changed lines, or the declared targets — and a whole-file summary cannot answer for a subset. Keeping the derivation and correcting the CLAIM is the honest move; keeping the claim and switching the derivation would trade a true statement about our own evidence for agreement with another tool's aggregate. Note the direction matters and is not symmetric: understating coverage can only cause a FAIL a consumer will investigate, whereas overstating it produces the silent false PASS this whole product exists to remove. |
| A-274 | **The v5→v6 migration needed a FIFTH bucket: RE-WITNESS. It is wrong for a fixture whose command reports branches, because **A-258 made branch judging unconditional**: P25's locked `coverage-witness.py` has always run pytest with `--cov-branch`, so a real v6 run now surfaces `branch_capability = "reported"` with nonzero `branches_covered`/`branches_total` and a different `pct` — while the transform emitted `"unavailable"` / `0`, describing a run that can no longer happen. Resolution: **re-witness `carve-assets/W1/expected/p25-*-v6-template.json` by running the real scenario and capturing its actual output**, then review the diff to confirm every change is exactly a branch field A-258 predicts. **Explicitly REFUSED: scoping branch fields out of `qualify()`'s comparison.** P25's whole point is that it compares the COMPLETE artifact; carving fields out of it to reach green is the weakened-negative move, and it would silently stop discriminating on precisely the payload this wave added. | The distinction that makes re-witnessing legitimate rather than cheating: `carve-assets/W1/**` are assets this wave CREATED, not historical frozen evidence, and the new values come from a real execution rather than from editing until the comparator agrees. The frozen-asset rule forbids editing evidence to make a gate pass; it does not forbid taking a fresh measurement when a ruling changed what there is to measure — and refusing to re-witness would mean freezing a template that asserts assay behaves in a way A-258 deliberately abolished. Worth recording because the migration's own fail-closed classifier could not have caught this: it refuses a file it cannot PLACE, and these files placed cleanly into `transform`. The unclassifiable case was never the risk; the confidently-misclassified one was. A future schema bump that changes measured values, not just field names, must sort those documents into re-witness before the transform runs. | Two defects, not two omissions, found by the operator reading wave 1's plan — after the plan had already been through a carve, three failed review rounds, a recarve and an independent review that all missed them. The README's headline bullet said "changed-line coverage, **not** whole-project coverage", which B005 makes false: the README would have shipped telling a reader the opposite of what the tool does. And `docs/CONSUMERS.md` — the file a consumer actually follows — was named in **no work item at all**, while its "adopt R1" paragraph predated the mandatory `[isolation]` table, so a consumer following it would write a lane that refuses `BAD_LANE_CONFIG` with no hint why. Both are the same failure: the trove documents (decisions, carves, backlog, STATE) are meticulously maintained because the process touches them every day, while the documents that face a HUMAN ADOPTER are touched only when someone remembers. The division of labour is ruled rather than left implicit because it was already drifting — the README had begun accumulating rationale, and the DESIGN-GUIDE feature lists. The mechanical half exists because this project's most expensive recurring defect is the check that cannot fail (A-124, A-131), and "we will remember to update the docs" is exactly that check. |
| A-275 | **B004's IMPLEMENTATION is DEFERRED, and `4-backlog.md` §B004's claim that "no verdict-schema change was needed and none proposed" is CORRECTED to false.** Two independent blockers, both measured rather than argued. **(a) It needs verdict-schema surface: exactly one new `ReasonCode`.** `verdict.py:_check_reason_code` requires a `reason_code` for every non-`PASS` outcome *and* requires it to be a member of `REASON_CODES[outcome]`; `adjudicated` evidence is forbidden the `(producer, attested_commit, reviewed_paths)` triple, so there is no field in which ciu's status string can be carried; and `MISSING_ATTESTATION`/`STALE_ATTESTATION` are hard-bound to `source == "attested"`.
