"The provenance tool returned a non-green verdict" has **no legal truthful spelling**. **(b) A green document is not producible by any live host in this estate.** `ciu provenance --json` in `/workspaces/dstdns` returns `overall: "mismatch"` over 20 containers — 16 `unlabelled`, and the 4 labelled ones carrying *upstream vendor* revisions (`otel` at `1400269f8ace…`, postgres at `refs/heads/master`, `skywalking-ui` at `9fc54aa1…`) that have nothing to do with dstdns's code. ciu compares **every** container's OCI revision label against its own repository's short hash, so `overall` is pinned at `mismatch` regardless of what dstdns builds. **What is landed instead:** the carve, the adversarial review, this ruling, the ciu follow-on ticket, and A-277's doc repair. **What is not built:** the reader, the adjudicator registry, and both terminals. | §B004 conflated *no new field* — true, and A-255's actual finding — with *no new enum value*, which is false; its own sketch's `mismatch → FAIL` **is** the closed-enum widening the same section lists as "not proposed". Deferring rather than routing around it is the wave-1 lesson applied twice over. On (a), every zero-schema escape was tested and died: green-or-refuse-at-load cannot commit-bind because HEAD resolves *after* load, and it emits a false diagnosis while suppressing the true one; mechanism-now/terminal-later **inverts** the `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL` precedent it cites, which reserved the terminal early and landed the mechanism late, and it would resurrect exactly the decorative registry A-078 deferred; truthful reuse is exhausted, two codes being mechanically refused and the rest already documented as other things, which is the A-145 name-collision family. On (b), building a feature whose only day-one observable is its failure mode is A-124/A-131's check-that-cannot-fire aimed at the **success** path — and adoption would brick any lane that declared the evidence. **One correction to the carve, in assay's favour:** its claim that the PASS branch has no witness *anywhere* is too strong. `ciu/nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/ciu-P01-worktree-isolation-primitives/provenance-verified-match.json` is a real green document, and it is **not** a fabrication of the wave-1 lesson-5 kind — `ciu/tests/tests/test_ciu_provenance_json.py:78` asserts ciu's own producer's `result.to_dict()` equals it, and the P01 handoff pins it at `sha256 b3243683e294…`. So a green-path oracle over real producer-pinned output is writable today; what is unreachable is a green document from a live host, which is what adoption needs and why (b) still blocks. |
| A-276 | **`PROVENANCE_UNVERIFIED` is RESERVED BY NAME as a `NO_MEASUREMENT`-class `ReasonCode`, to ride whichever bump another item already pays for — B001/P34 or B007 — and NOT a bump of its own.** No code, schema or doc changes now; this row is the reservation, so that the next migration's author folds one value in rather than discovering the need mid-carve. B004's implementation unblocks when **both** conditions hold: the reserved code has shipped, **and** ciu can emit `overall: "verified-match"` on a correctly deployed instance. | The estate absorbed v6 and lane-v2 days ago; a v7 carrying a single enum value for a feature that cannot yet demonstrate its own success path is a consumer migration bought at full price for no observable behaviour. Reserving by name is the cheap half of what B004 actually needs and it is the move `4-backlog.md`'s own revised sequencing note asks for — each remaining carve states whether it needs schema surface, so the estate pays one migration instead of two. Recording the unblock condition as a **conjunction** matters: shipping the code alone would make the feature buildable and still useless, and fixing ciu alone would leave it unspellable. |
| A-277 | **A-270's doc rule has found its first real defect, and it is exactly one code wide: `ALL_MUTANTS_EQUIVALENT` appears in NO user-facing document.** Enumerating all 30 members of `ReasonCode` against `README.md`, `docs/DESIGN-GUIDE.md` and `docs/CONSUMERS.md` leaves that one member matching nothing; every other code appears in at least one. It has been undocumented since v5 introduced it (A-223d). It also shows the rule catching **pre-existing** drift rather than only policing new changes, which is the stronger property — A-270 was written after the README had already begun accumulating rationale, so the interesting question was always whether it would surface debt it did not create. Landing it in an unrelated branch is deliberate: it is three lines, the branch is already touching the trove, and holding a known documentation defect for a tidier commit is how it stays undocumented for another two schema versions. |
| A-278 | **Wave 1's release embargo could not survive its own success, and is REPHRASED from the proxy to the property.** `test_no_assay_release_has_been_cut_since_wi1_landed` forbade any `assay-v*` tag descending from WI-1's landing commit `c56a13ea`, with **nothing that ever closed the window** — while the embargo's own header comment scoped it to "between WI-1 landing lane schema v2 and this branch merging with a green gate". Wave 1 merged green, `assay-v2.0.0` was cut from that merge, and the test went red on the very release it existed to protect. Replaced by `test_every_release_since_wi1_landed_carries_wi4s_policy_record`, which asserts the hazard's actual absence: **any tag descending from `c56a13ea` must itself carry WI-4's `snapshot_policy` record.** Keyed on the field NAME, never on schema version 6, so a v7 that keeps the record still discharges it. Paired with `test_wi1s_own_landing_commit_is_the_state_the_embargo_forbids`, a must-fail control running the identical reader against the one real commit that is hazardous by construction. | Measured, not reasoned: at `c56a13ea` the tree already carries `LANE_SCHEMA_VERSION = 2` while its `verdict.schema.json` is `urn:assay:schema:verdict:5` with **zero** occurrences of `snapshot_policy` — the exact state the embargo names — and at `assay-v2.0.0` the schema is `:6` with six. The new control matters more than the new assertion, because the failure mode being guarded against is precisely a test that passes for want of a subject: `assert tags` alone was never enough, since a non-empty tag list can still contain nothing in-window, so `any(descends)` now guards vacuity directly. Implementation note worth keeping: the in-window set is computed as an unconditional map and combined with `zip`, rather than as a filtered comprehension, because every tag that exists today is in-window and a filter's skip arc would be unreachable — an unreachable arc in a project at 100% branch coverage is A-124/A-131's defect wearing thoroughness as a disguise. |

Found by the controller reading §3.4 against §3.6, confirmed independently by the adversarial review, which extended it with the proof that acceptance oracle **O1's own expected `PASS` is unreachable too** — the carve contradicted itself across three sections. The oracle requirement matters more than the reorder: this survived a 1388-line carve that measured everything else on live PostgreSQL **precisely because no oracle in §7 exercised the feature's headline outcome** — O3 proves mutants are valid DDL, O4 proves residue becomes `equivalent`, O5 proves dump reproducibility, O8 proves existing lanes do not move, and none of them ever produces a kill. Wave-1 lesson 2 ("checks that cannot fire, including on the SUCCESS path") was handed to this carve **in writing** and recurred anyway. The general rule: **an oracle set that omits the feature's headline outcome is incomplete no matter how many failure modes it covers.** |

The qualification's command is a new file owned by P34: apply to a throwaway database, `pg_dump --schema-only --no-owner --restrict-key=<pinned>` to the declared path, then run the schema tests, writing the kill signal on failure. | Measured against the pinned blob itself, not inferred: it (a) contains **no `pg_dump` at all**, so it writes neither declared artifact; (b) **exits 2 immediately** under §3.4's `argv = ["scripts/schema-gate.sh"]` with `allow_argv_append = false`, because its first positional parameter is mandatory (`WORKTREE_ARG="${1:?usage: schema-gate.sh <worktree-path> …}"`); and (c) drives `docker inspect`/`run`/`exec` against the **deployed** app network and container names, none of which exists inside an assay snapshot. Under it every mutant is artifact-absent ⇒ `crashed` ⇒ the whole qualification renders `ERROR`. No work item assigned anyone to write a command satisfying the three consumer obligations, so the qualification's central deliverable was unowned — the implementer would have discovered this mid-W9, after the adapter had already shipped against a lane nobody could run. |
| A-281 | **SQL site retention mirrors `_generate_python_sites`' bounded worst-eviction heap** (`adapters/python.py:742-763`): every operator pattern's scan feeds ONE heap bounded at `limit` and keyed by `MutationSite.identity`, sorted by identity once at return. The carve's "walks the mask once per operator pattern in `MutationSite.identity` order and stops appending at `limit`" is **struck**. W2's bounded-retention test is strengthened accordingly: a fixture in which operator-listing order and byte order disagree, asserting the returned batch is identity-ordered and that at `limit=1` the identity-**smallest** site is the one retained. | `MutationSite.identity` is `(start_byte, end_byte, replacement_sha256, operator)` — **position-major, operator last** — so a per-operator walk cannot be "in identity order", and `_validate_sites` refuses any batch that is not identity-sorted. All three readings of the struck sentence fail differently: appending per operator and returning as-is produces an unordered batch on any file where a later-listed operator has an earlier `start_byte` (ordinary — a `NOT NULL` above a `CHECK`), yielding a spurious `ERROR`/`MUTATION_DISCOVERY_FAILED` on real files; sorting before return retains the first `limit` in operator-major order, a *different subset* from the identity bound; and collecting-then-sorting violates A-180's retention bound, which the carve itself forbids for its own prototype. The shipped precedent is none of the three. The carve's specified test (`limit=1`, "exactly one descriptor") cannot distinguish the wrong subset from the right one, which is why the test is strengthened and not merely the prose. |

| A-283 | **Two further lexical context rules, and one recogniser gap, are binding on the SQL adapter.** (1) `sql:drop-not-null` emits **no site where `NOT NULL` is immediately preceded by `DROP`** — the mirror of the carve's own `SET` rule, and the very text its own replacement emits. (2) `sql:drop-not-null` emits **no site inside a dollar-quoted body's `DECLARE` section** (between a leading `DECLARE` and the first `BEGIN`). Rule (2) is notable because **the carve's own phase-2 recursion created it** — recursing into dollar bodies imports plpgsql *declaration* grammar into scope, where a `NOT NULL` is not a column constraint; the carve handled the analogous body-grammar leak for `drop-trigger` and missed it for `drop-not-null`. The pattern is worth more than the four corrections: **every one of these citation errors is in a section about assay's OWN internals, while the sections about SQL, PostgreSQL and the real dstdns corpus were measured exhaustively and survived adversarial attack intact.** The carver verified the unfamiliar thing and trusted its memory of the familiar one — which is the same failure shape as A-272 (an invariant asserted about another tool's output, validated only on small fixtures) with the roles reversed. The standing consequence: a carve's claims about the shipped tree are checked by grep before an implementer is briefed, exactly as its claims about a foreign tool are checked by running it. `adapter is None` exactly when neither R1 nor R2 is declared, so both arcs of the ruled guard are reachable from real lanes and neither is a A-124/A-131 dead branch. |

The reading lives in exactly one function (`_replacement_is_safe`), is checked before every candidate site is returned, and is stated in `adapters/sql.py`'s module docstring where a reader meets it. | A new string literal cannot be written without a delimiting quote pair, so read literally the invariant forbids the very thing rule 3's all-string branch must construct, and that branch would be unreachable from any input whatsoever — in a project at 100% branch coverage, A-124/A-131's defect wearing thoroughness as a disguise. The invariant's actual purpose is stated in the carve itself: "a replacement carrying a delimiter could terminate the construct it sits inside, which is how a byte splice turns into a different file." A **balanced** pair containing no embedded quote cannot terminate anything, and the trigger form's own `;` is already an identical named exception — so this is the existing reading applied consistently, not a new licence. | A-288 | **A-279 is now MEASURED, not reasoned, and the pair is frozen at `carve-assets/W3/`.** Two repositories identical in DDL, lane and mutant, differing only in the ordering of the project-declared command, driven through the shipped `assay run`: `apply && dump && test` yields `killed=1`, `crashed=0`, lane `PASS` (exit 0); `apply && test && dump` yields `killed=0`, `crashed=1`, lane `ERROR`/`EXEC_FAILED` (exit 2). | The finding travelled the full ladder in one wave — reasoned by the controller from §3.4 against §3.6, confirmed by the adversarial review from `safeio.arm()`'s unlink semantics, and now **produced on demand from the shipped CLI with a paired control**. Recording it matters because the failure is invisible in every artifact a consumer would look at: the run is not silently wrong, it is loudly `ERROR`, which reads as "assay is broken" rather than "your command is ordered wrong", and a consumer will route around it. CONSUMERS.md therefore states the ordering as a requirement **with its consequence** rather than as an example. Also recorded from the capture: writing `--verdict-json` **into the repository under test** returns `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`DIRTY_TREE` before any mutant is attempted — the mechanism working, and a trap the next person to hand-drive a lane will meet. |
| A-289 | **The carve's §9 M6 "empty name delta" claim is CORRECTED: it holds only for EXPLICITLY-NAMED constraints.** An *unnamed* column-level `CHECK` gets an auto-generated name derived from the column it references, so replacing it with `CHECK (true)` — which references no column — changes `pg_get_constraintdef`'s generated name and the delta is **not** empty. W9's `drop-check` scenario therefore targets an explicitly-named constraint (`ck_outbox_events_published_timestamp`), and the divergence is documented at the scenario. Recorded with it, as bounds on what the qualification proves: `postgres:18-alpine` carries **no TimescaleDB**, so four real dstdns files (`08-create-hypertables.sql`, `11-create-continuous-aggregates.sql`, `12-retention-policies.sql`, and `10-analytics-procedures.sql`'s `time_bucket()` self-test) are excluded from the applied corpus — an environment limitation of the qualification, **not** a limitation of the adapter, which never connects to a database at all. | Found by the implementer building the harness, not by the carve or the adversarial review, and found only because the qualification actually applies mutants to a real catalog rather than reasoning about one — the precise reason W9 exists. It is a correction to the carve's *evidence*, not to its design: §9 M6's measurement was taken against a fixture whose CHECK constraints were explicitly named, and the claim was then generalised one step too far. The same shape as A-272 (an invariant about another tool's output, validated on a narrower input than it was stated for) and A-284 (a claim about a familiar surface, trusted from memory), now met a third time in a third place. Four further environment traps were measured and are documented at their call sites rather than here, because each is a fact about PostgreSQL tooling and not a ruling: `pg_dump --restrict-key` refuses a hyphenated key; `DROP DATABASE; CREATE DATABASE` in one `psql -c` fails because PostgreSQL implicitly wraps a multi-statement simple query in a transaction; and `pg_isready` returns ready before queries reliably succeed, so readiness must be a real `SELECT 1` round trip or a role-creation race appears as a flake. |
| A-290 | **B017's `--exclude-standard` change is REVERTED; A-177 stands.** `dirty_paths()` keeps `ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore`, not `--exclude-standard`. B017's filed premise — that a committed blanket `/.assay/` ignore also hid it from `dirty_paths()` — did not reproduce: measured on a repository with a committed `.gitignore` containing `/.assay/`, both flag choices return the identical (empty) result, because a **committed per-directory** `.gitignore` was already honored under A-177. `--exclude-standard` additionally honors `.git/info/exclude`, a repository-local file that lives inside `.git/` and is reported by nothing else (unlike an untracked or dirty `.gitignore`, which is itself returned by `dirty_paths()`); adopting it let a personal ignore rule there hide a brand-new untracked source file with no self-reporting trail, which is exactly the regression the pre-existing guard test `test_git_info_exclude_cannot_hide_untracked_dirt` exists to catch, and which B017 turned red. Also corrected, not merely re-stated: the earlier claim "Git itself cannot report a committed-but-deleted file that its ignore rules match" is **false** — on Git 2.55.0, `git status --porcelain` reports a staged deletion of a tracked file (` D <path>`) regardless of any ignore rule matching that path, and `dirty_paths()` returns it. The probe that produced the false claim used a test helper (`conftest.GitRepo.commit_all`, `git add -A`) which under a blanket `*` ignore never tracks the file in the first place, so its later deletion has nothing to report — a test-setup artifact, not a Git limitation. | A-177 correctly rejected re-enabling arbitrary excludes as a substitute for tracked-file integrity; B017 supplied no reproduced defect to justify revisiting that ruling and its replacement was measurably worse — a new, silent, unreported gap in exactly the guarantee A-177 was written to hold. Reverting restores the passing state of the named regression test rather than weakening it or replacing it with a different one. The false "Git cannot report a deleted ignored file" claim is corrected here because it was recorded as measured and would otherwise be trusted as a Git limitation by the next person who reads this ruling, rather than recognised as an artifact of the fixture helper used to probe it. |
| A-291 | **B016 is recorded as NOT REPRODUCIBLE rather than fixed blindly.** At the post-v2.3.0 HEAD, a literal repository fixture containing committed `.py` leaves beside an untracked `__pycache__/` materializes both sources and no cache. P22 already verifies index tree equals HEAD tree, status is clean, and skip-worktree equals declared omissions. The new explicit check walks `_Manifest.entries`: every non-omitted regular-file leaf must exist as a file and symlink leaf must exist as a symlink before yield. | The backlog's "suspected cause" was not evidence, and adding speculative writer changes would have manufactured a fix for an absent defect—the exact shape behind false certifications. The literal reproduction passed red-first against the *test setup* (no commit existed), then green against unchanged behavior. Hardening remains justified because B016 explicitly required direct manifest-to-disk presence verification; it converts a future omission into a loud pre-yield failure independent of what Git status happens to observe. |
| A-292 | **B012 resume owns state outside ephemeral snapshots and validates records before reuse; shard merging is a manifest set proof.** State lives under an authoritative caller-provided `.assay/mutation-state/` root, one bounded JSON document per deterministic candidate ID. Resume creates the root, treats an absent record as pending, refuses malformed/stale records, and merges restored buckets by candidate identity so `candidate_count == total`. Shards assign with zero-based keyed-digest indexes; `merge_mutation_shards` requires exact shard-index coverage, one schema/lane/commit/count, and duplicate-free IDs before returning their union. | Persisting inside a per-mutant snapshot would destroy resume by construction; persisting in the caller repository would violate the dirty-tree contract. The external authoritative root preserves both boundaries while keeping stale evidence loud. Bucket-count addition was rejected because it can certify partial or overlapping shard work as exhaustive; the manifest proof checks the actual assignment domain and identities. Worker identity remains executor-internal rather than inventing a public option Assay cannot populate truthfully on all executor paths. |
| A-293 | **B013 resolves infrastructure declarations at the plan boundary in the invoking context, never inside a snapshot.** The closed grammar is `required-env:NAME` and `derived:dotted.path`. `required-env` reads the invoking environment (injectable for tests); `derived` reads rendered `ciu.global.toml` at the project root. Values must be non-empty strings; dotted paths are validated at load and resolved fail-loudly at run. Injected names collide-refuse with fixed env and passthrough names, so every injected fact has exactly one owner. | Resolving inside the snapshot would either leak caller state or make required runtime facts unreachable—the exact B013 blocker. Reading CIU state directly preserves isolation because only selected scalar values cross the boundary as declared environment entries, while unrendered templates and host paths stay outside. Collision refusal prevents a fixed or passthrough declaration from silently overriding a resolved fact. |
| A-294 | **CIU V8 preparation is carved into Assay backlog B018/B019/B020 rather than absorbed into CIU-only design.** B018 requires judge name/version/digest identity in verdicts; B019 separates changed-line judging policy from branch-specific base identity so CIU can supply it per gate request; B020 remains design-first and explicitly refuses to turn Assay into a database provisioner. The B013/B012 capabilities already landed remove two proposal blockers, but these three remain open upstream contracts. | Judge distribution can verify a downloaded artifact, yet without producing-judge identity in each verdict it cannot prove that artifact emitted the evidence. Base-in-request fixes worktree drift without weakening merge-base provenance. SQL template optimization is infrastructure orchestration until a reviewed contract proves it can preserve per-mutant isolation; implementing it now would repeat the speculative-fix shape A-291 rejects. |
| A-295 | **B016's manifest-leaf predicate now enforces "regular file" by `lstat`/`S_ISREG`, not `Path.is_file()`, and is extracted into `_prove_manifest_materialized` so it is unit-testable independent of Git.** `Path.is_file()` follows symlinks, so an entry declared `_MODE_REGULAR`/`_MODE_EXECUTABLE` that materialized as a symlink to a byte-identical file satisfied the old check — the exact gap DESIGN-GUIDE.md's "must exist as a regular file" contract already claimed was closed. The check remains a defence-in-depth measure, independently confirmed unreachable through any real Git-backed materialization today: a clean `status`, a matching `write-tree`, and matching skip-worktree entries (the three checks that already run before it) are together sufficient, by Git's own consistency guarantees, to prove the working tree is byte-for-byte what the commit's tree records — so no corruption this loop could catch can survive those three checks in the current architecture. It is kept anyway, corrected, for the same reason A-291 gave: a future change to the writer or the earlier checks must not silently make this the only remaining guard while it is still wrong. | An independent adversarial review of the merged B016 change (not run before merge — see the operator's standing note that this wave initially shipped self-reviewed only) found the predicate wrong and, separately, that neither the shipped test nor deleting the whole new loop could distinguish a working implementation from a broken one (0 test failures either way). Extracting the check to a module-level function lets a test construct the exact corruption (symlink standing in for a regular leaf) directly, which no test reaching it through real Git materialization can do, since Git itself always reports that specific corruption first. |
| A-296 | **B012's shipped CLI surface had four defects independent of its design, all now fixed: two unimported names crashed every refusal path, `--shard` was 1-based against a 0-based config/schema/A-292, a sharded verdict never recorded which shard actually ran, and `merge_mutation_shards` accepted shards with no candidates and candidates cross-assigned to the wrong shard.** `cli.py` was missing `LaneConfigError` and `os` from its `.errors`/stdlib imports (the class and module both exist and are used correctly elsewhere); every `--operators`/`--shard` refusal path raised `NameError` instead of the intended typed error. `--shard`'s CLI parsing subtracted 1 from the supplied index at all four call sites (`cli.py`, `runner.py`) while `config.py`, `verdict.schema.json`, and this file's own A-292 all define it zero-based; the conversion is deleted, and the out-of-range bounds check (previously outside any `try`, an uncaught `ValueError`) now returns a proper `BAD_LANE_CONFIG` refusal verdict via `refuse_lane`, matching the existing `missing_required`/adapter-refusal pattern rather than raising past `main()`. `_build_judgment_r2` was called with `lane.judge.mutation.shard_index`/`shard_count` (the lane's static declaration, which selects nothing) instead of the function's own `shard_index`/`shard_count` parameters (the executed `--shard` value) — a sharded verdict therefore recorded an empty shard identity indistinguishable from a complete run. `merge_mutation_shards` now recomputes each candidate's `blake2b(id) % count` against the shard index its document claims and refuses a mismatch, and refuses a merge whose total candidate set is empty, so A-292's "checks the actual assignment domain" is now true rather than aspirational; it still cannot prove a candidate ID came from a real plan rather than a well-formed fabrication, because nothing produces or consumes a shard-summary document yet — filed as a new backlog item, not fixed here. | Found entirely by an independent adversarial review driving the shipped CLI (never by reading the diff): every one of these reproduces from `assay run`/`assay plan` with ordinary arguments, and none was caught by the merged tests, which never exercise `--shard`/`--resume` (`grep -rn` returned zero hits). The `refuse_lane` pattern is reused rather than invented because the surrounding code already establishes it for exactly this class of post-HEAD-resolution failure (the `missing_required` check a few lines above, and the adapter-resolution catch in `cli._run_reserved`); a new mechanism would be a second, inconsistent way to fail the same class of error. |
| A-297 | **B013 shipped non-functional: `cli.py` never imported `os` (used unconditionally to read `os.environ`) and never constructed `infrastructure_source`, so any lane declaring an `[infrastructure]` table crashed with `NameError` before running, and its `derived:` half was unreachable even once that import is fixed.** Both are now wired: `os` is imported, and `infrastructure_source` is set to `lane_file.project_root / "ciu.global.toml"` when the lane declares any infrastructure fact — the exact rendered, gitignored path ciu itself writes (`ciu/README.md`, `ciu/src/ciu/scaffold.py`; ciu never places it elsewhere). `resolve_command_plan`'s own "declares a derived fact but no source" refusal was a bare `ValueError`, uncaught anywhere; it is now `AssayError`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`, matching every other refusal in the same function. | Found entirely by an independent adversarial review driving `assay run` against a real lane file with an `[infrastructure]` table — the merged tests (`test_infrastructure_injection.py`) call `resolve_command_plan`/`run_lane` directly and never go through `cli`, so the missing import and the missing wiring were both invisible to the suite while the feature was completely inert through its only real entry point. The review's three seeded attacks against the resolution logic itself (dotted-path traversal, malformed TOML, non-scalar resolution) were separately refuted — that logic was correct; only the CLI plumbing around it was missing. A dangerous-ambient-env-name denylist and the passthrough-overwrite-order fragility the same review flagged are judged non-blocking (the lane file is already trusted, and `env_passthrough` already reaches the same values) and are filed as new backlog items rather than fixed here. |
| A-298 | **Round 2 review of A-295/A-296/A-297's remediation found the fix itself introduced two crashes and left a pre-existing one exposed; round 3 fixes all three plus a merge-proof gap, all now verified by driving the installed CLI.** (1) `assay run --shard I/N` **without** `--resume` crashed for every valid shard — `runner.py`'s caller only ever passed `mutation.run_mutation`'s required `state_project_root` when `resume` was set, while sharding needs the same authoritative root for its own record-writing regardless; the caller now passes it whenever `resume or shard_index is not None`. (2) `--shard 0/10001` crashed — `select_mutation_shard`'s bounds check reused `MAX_CANDIDATE_CEILING` (10,001, deliberately `max_mutants + 1` for a DIFFERENT purpose: the discovery-limit sentinel), while `JudgmentR2.shard_count` independently hardcodes `1..10,000`; a new `MAX_SHARD_COUNT = 10_000` constant now backs both, plus `merge_mutation_shards`'s own count bound. (3) the round-3 shard refusal (and, it turned out, three PRE-EXISTING `refuse_lane` call sites: `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`, a probe-result refusal, and `missing_required`) omitted `infrastructure_source`/`infrastructure_environment` when calling `refuse_lane`, so on any lane with a `derived:` fact the refusal's own internal plan-resolution re-triggered the SAME unresolved-source failure and crashed one level deeper — all five `refuse_lane` call sites in `run_lane` now forward both parameters, matching the one (`dirty_paths`) that already did. This resolved B025 for `run_lane`'s five call sites — round-3 review of THIS fix (see A-299) found two more in `cli.py`, since fixed too, so B025 is now fully closed for every resolvable-source case across the whole codebase. (4) `merge_mutation_shards`'s `covered_pairs` was a plain set, so two documents claiming the same `(shard_index, shard_count)` merged silently as long as their candidate ids were disjoint; a duplicate pair now refuses. Not fixed, filed instead as B026: the bad-`--shard` refusal (like every `refuse_lane` refusal) carries no free-text diagnostic — `run` and `plan` now disagree on how much a consumer learns — and `judge.mutation.shard_index`/`shard_count` are confirmed fully dead config, read by nothing. | Found entirely by round 2's own adversarial re-verification of the round-1 remediation — driving `assay run`/`assay plan` with `--shard 0/2`, `0/10001`, and `9/2` on a lane with a resolvable `derived:` fact, and re-running the merge-proof attack table (`B` cross-assigned, `D` all-empty, plus a new duplicate-index case `F`). Each round-3 fix was verified the same way: driven through the installed CLI before AND after, not read off the diff. The `MAX_SHARD_COUNT` split matters because `MAX_CANDIDATE_CEILING`'s `+ 1` is a genuine, load-bearing sentinel for candidate-discovery ("at least this many exist") that a shard COUNT has no analogous use for; reusing one constant for two different bounds is exactly the kind of accidental coupling that produces a mismatch nobody notices until the boundary value is hit. |
| A-299 | **Round 3 review of A-298's fix found two more `refuse_lane` call sites with the identical missing-parameter defect, in `cli.py` rather than `runner.py`; both fixed and B025 is now genuinely closed for every resolvable-infrastructure-source case.** `cli.py`'s attestation `LANE_TIMEOUT` refusal and its adapter-resolution refusal (`_run_reserved`) both call `runner.refuse_lane(...)` without forwarding `infrastructure_source`/`infrastructure_environment`, though `_run_reserved` computes both a few lines earlier and both are in scope. Proven pre-existing (present on `main`, not introduced by any round of this remediation) and driven A/B: a `language = "go"` lane (unregistered, so adapter resolution refuses) declaring a `derived:` fact that resolves cleanly still wrote no verdict and reported the wrong cause before this fix; both sites now forward the parameters, matching `run_lane`'s five. | Found by the same reviewer session verifying round 3's `run_lane` fix, by constructing the identical attack shape (a refusal reason unrelated to infrastructure, on a lane whose infrastructure table is itself perfectly resolvable) against the two call sites the `runner.py`-only fix could not have reached. `cli.py` and `runner.py` both build refusal verdicts via the same `refuse_lane` function but are edited/reviewed as separate files, which is exactly how a fix to one half of a shared pattern leaves the other half's instances of the same bug standing. |
## Decided — stabilization wave, 2026-08-25 (B008/B021/B022/B024/B025/B026/B027; verdict schema v7)

**Round 2 review correction:** A-295 through A-299 (the B012/B013/B016/B017
remediation, merged separately as `6e8a51fb`) were filed under the "session
54" heading above, dated 2026-08-16 and naming wave 1/schema v6 — already
stale by the time they landed, and left uncorrected until this heading was
added. The entries below are this stabilization wave's own, correctly dated
and versioned.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-300 | **B027 fixed: a mutant-induced pytest timeout crashed `execute_plan` instead of reaching `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`/`LANE_TIMEOUT`.** `subprocess.TimeoutExpired.stdout`/`.stderr` is `bytes` even under `text=True` — the one path CPython does not text-decode for it — while `_bounded_tail` is typed and documented to receive an already-decoded `str`, an assumption true of the normal-completion path but not this one. A new `_decode_timeout_stream` tolerantly decodes (`errors="replace"`, never raises) before both `_bounded_tail(...)` calls inside the `except subprocess.TimeoutExpired` handler, so a hung mutant's output tail reads the same way a completed one's does. | Filed from a dstdns P132 phase-1 code review, live-reproduced 5x deterministically (a `while` loop mutant that never terminates). Verified red-first: the pre-fix handler raises `AttributeError: 'bytes' object has no attribute 'encode'` on the same repro; a new test constructs a `subprocess.TimeoutExpired` with `bytes` `output`/`stderr` directly rather than relying on real timing, so it is deterministic. |
| A-301 | **B008 fixed: `resolve_base`'s merge-commit-vs-merge-base branch was silent — a routine main-sync merge on HEAD narrows R1/R2 evidence to first-parent with no record of which branch fired.** The two-branch algorithm itself is documented, deliberate design (not a bug), so the fix is transparency, not a policy reversal: a new `git.base_resolution_mode(repo)` reports which branch fired for the current HEAD, threaded through `_run_prepared_lane`/`_build_judgment_resolved` as an optional `base_resolution` field on `JudgmentResolved` — present (`"merge-base"` or `"first-parent"`) exactly when `base` itself is present, `None` otherwise, validated in `__post_init__`. Additive: existing v7 consumers ignore an unknown optional field; nothing about `resolve_base`'s own behavior changed. | A lane whose HEAD is a routine merge commit (main synced into a feature branch right before a gate run) silently judges only the feature branch's own pre-merge commits — correct per `resolve_base`'s docstring, but invisible to a consumer reading the verdict, who cannot distinguish "a small diff because little changed" from "a small diff because merge-base narrowing hid most of it." `base_resolution` makes the branch that fired an auditable fact instead of something you have to re-derive from `git rev-list --parents` yourself. |
| A-302 | **B021 fixed: a mutation-state record's `schema_version` mismatch was treated the same as every other field mismatch (raise `MutationStateError`) when it needed the opposite disposition.** `candidate_id`'s hash already folds in every OTHER required field (path, source hash, byte range, replacement hash, operator) — meaning the record's own filename already encodes agreement on those; a mismatch there means the file's *identity* lied and must raise. `schema_version` is the one required key excluded from that hash on purpose, precisely so a routine schema bump does not orphan every resumable in-flight record — so a `schema_version` mismatch must instead cause a silent rerun (`return None`), not a raised error. `_load_validated_state_record` now special-cases `schema_version` to return `None`; every other key keeps raising. | Found by re-reading `candidate_id`'s own hash inputs against `_load_validated_state_record`'s validation loop and noticing the ONE field excluded from the identity hash was being validated as if it were included. A resume after a routine, non-breaking schema bump would otherwise raise `MutationStateError` and abort the whole resume instead of just re-running that one candidate, turning a no-op version bump into an operator-visible break. |
| A-303 | **B022 fixed: `resolve_command_plan`'s `env_passthrough` loop ran after the infrastructure loop and unconditionally overwrote, so a passthrough name colliding with an infrastructure fact silently won at runtime** — even though `config.py`'s loader already refuses this exact collision at load time. Constructing a `Lane` directly (bypassing the loader) still reaches the runtime path, so the loader check alone was not a complete defence of A-293's "every injected fact has exactly one owner." The loop now raises `AssayError`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` on the same collision instead of silently overwriting. **No dangerous-ambient-env-name denylist was added** (a candidate fix floated in A-297's own review) — the lane file is already trusted, and `env_passthrough` already reaches the exact same ambient values a denylist would be defending against, so a denylist would add surface without closing any real gap; this decision was already reached at A-297 filing time and is restated here alongside the actual fix. | The loader-time check (`test_collision_with_env_or_passthrough_is_refused`) was mistaken for complete defence-in-depth; it defends the declared-lane-file path only. A lane constructed programmatically (as `make_lane` does for this exact test) reaches `resolve_command_plan` directly and was unprotected. Verified red-first via an `if False:` sentinel swap on the fix (a prior attempt at red-first verification via bulk Python string-replace silently no-op'd because its own `assert` didn't match — Edit-tool sentinel swaps are the reliable pattern going forward). |
| A-304 | **The W3 frozen witness (`carve-assets/W3/expected/dstdns-sql-r2-v6-witness.json`) was re-witnessed after A-301 added `base_resolution` to every verdict's `judgment.resolved` block** — per this package's own stated policy (§6/W9 in `W3-CARVE-P34-sql-adapter.md`), the witness is re-witnessed from a real run whenever a ruling changes what assay produces, never hand-edited toward green. Regenerated by running `capture_witness`/`normalize_verdict` for real (the exact functions `test_capture_witness_end_to_end_matches_the_frozen_witness` itself calls) against the pinned dstdns checkout at `/workspaces/dstdns`, then diffed byte-for-byte against the prior witness before writing: the ONLY change across the whole document is the one new `"base_resolution": "merge-base"` line. | The frozen-witness test failed after A-301 landed, with the real verdict and the frozen witness differing only in the new field — confirming the field addition is genuinely additive everywhere else in this witness's coverage and that no other unrelated drift crept in between the witness's original capture and today. Diffing old-vs-new before writing (rather than trusting the regeneration blind) is what makes this a legitimate re-witness rather than a hand-edit toward green — the same real-run-and-diff discipline the project's own docs already prescribe for this exact situation. |
| A-305 | **B022 item 3 decided: a numeric or boolean `derived:` TOML value stays refused, not coerced to a string.** `resolve_command_plan` already required the resolved node to be a non-empty `str`; this was undocumented rather than absent, so it reads as a gap. It is the right call as-is — silently stringifying a TOML integer or boolean would let a source's own type choice (e.g. a port declared as an integer) silently become an env-string fact with no record that a coercion happened, and a consumer wanting a numeric fact as text can already render it as a string at the source (`ciu.global.toml` is CIU's own rendered output, not assay's). No code change; this decision is the documentation the backlog item asked for. | Raised by round-1 adversarial review of B013 as one of four hardening items (B022). Coercion was considered and rejected: assay's own `required-env` half already only ever sees strings (`os.environ` values are always `str`), so making `derived:` accept non-strings via silent coercion would make the two source kinds behave differently for no capability gain, and would need its own tested formatting rule (how does a float render? a TOML datetime?) that nothing in this package currently needs. |
| A-306 | **B022 item 4 fixed: a resolved infrastructure value now has a runtime byte bound.** `resolve_command_plan` previously wrote a `required-env`/`derived` value into `env_effective` unbounded; a new `MAX_INFRASTRUCTURE_VALUE_BYTES = 65536` (`config.py`, alongside `MAX_INFRASTRUCTURE_FACTS`, which bounds declared fact COUNT rather than a resolved value's length) now refuses, by name and byte count, before the value ever reaches the command plan, instead of failing later and opaquely at `E2BIG` on exec. B022's remaining two items (the denylist decision and the passthrough-collision fix) are A-303; this closes B022 in full. | 64 KiB was chosen as comfortably above any real infrastructure fact this package's own consumers declare (ports, hostnames, tokens, small rendered JSON blobs) — no host-specific `ARG_MAX` was measured to derive the exact number; the bound only needs to be far enough below the multi-MB range where `E2BIG` becomes possible on any common Linux configuration to catch a genuinely pathological value (e.g. a `derived:` dotted path accidentally landing on a whole file's content instead of one field), not to hug a precise ceiling. **Round 2 review correction:** the commit message's comparison to B027/A-300 was a false analogy — B027 is a `bytes`/`str` `AttributeError` on the timeout path, unrelated to `E2BIG`; the two share only the general shape "fail at the subprocess boundary" and nothing more specific. |
| A-307 | **B024's sweep half is done: `python -m pyflakes` over the whole `src/assay/` tree (recursively) is clean, 0 findings (30 on the parent commit — round 2 review independently recounted and confirmed the exact figure).** Fixed: four unused imports, one needless `f"..."` prefix on a string with no placeholder, one genuinely dead local variable (deleted outright, not silenced), and 24 annotation-only undefined names in `canary.py`/`mutation.py` — real gaps for a type checker, harmless at runtime only because `from __future__ import annotations` defers evaluation. Resolved with real imports where the target module doesn't import back (`canary.py` already imports directly from `.runner`; extended that import and added a new direct `.isolation` import) and with a `TYPE_CHECKING`-guarded import where it does (`mutation.py` cannot import `.runner`/`.adapters.base` for real — both already import FROM `mutation.py`, so a real import would be circular). **Round 2 review correction: `mutation.py`'s new `from .isolation import SnapshotRepository` is a REAL runtime import, not `TYPE_CHECKING`-guarded like its two siblings in the same edit** — deliberately, because `isolation.py` does not import `mutation.py` back (verified: no cycle), so nothing forced the guard there. Called out explicitly because a lint sweep that changes the import graph is a different class of change from silencing an unused-import warning, even when both land in the same commit for the same reason. **The gate-wiring half is deliberately deferred**, not done: no project in this estate's shared `tester-unified` image closure depends on `pyflakes`/`ruff` today, so wiring a lint phase means adding the tool to that **shared** Docker image and rebuilding + re-validating every consuming project's gate (ciu, cmru, topos, nyxloom, cgroup-profiler) — a cross-project, shared-infrastructure change well outside a same-day, single-project stabilization pass. Filed as a narrower, scoped follow-up in B024 itself. | The sweep matters on its own even without the gate wired: `test_dependency_purity.py`'s own AST walk already runs over this exact source tree for a different reason (the zero-runtime-dependency invariant), so undefined names and dead code sitting in the same tree, unflagged by anything, was a real gap independent of whether a linter phase ever gets wired in. Deferring the image change is a scope judgment, not an oversight — recorded here so the next wave that touches `tester-unified` doesn't have to rediscover why assay's own gate has no lint phase yet. |
| A-308 | **B025 decided and fixed: option 1 (a refusal verdict honestly records less, rather than crashing), refined against a hard existing invariant the original three-option filing had not accounted for.** `LANE_RESOLVED_FIELDS`'s own contract (module docstring) is "all present or all absent" — so `env_effective` cannot be omitted alone the way the filing first imagined without breaking that group invariant. The actual fix keeps the WHOLE group present (never breaking that contract) and instead makes `env_effective` reflect only what's known for certain — exactly `lane.env`, never infrastructure or `env_passthrough` values, neither of which could be safely completed — paired with a new additive, optional `env_effective_incomplete: true` field (present only in this exact case; every other verdict this build has ever produced implicitly means `false`) so a consumer is never left inferring completeness from absence. Fixed at BOTH places an unresolvable infrastructure declaration could crash a refusal: `_run_higher_rigor_lane`'s own PRIMARY `resolve_command_plan` call (the single-cause case: infra is the ONLY thing wrong, previously propagated uncaught past `main()`'s handler with no artifact at all) now delegates straight to `refuse_lane` with the caught exception's own outcome/reason_code; `refuse_lane`'s SECOND, recording-only call (the two-unrelated-causes case B025's own filing centers on: refusing for an unrelated reason on a lane whose infra ALSO can't resolve) now falls back to the degraded plan in place rather than re-raising from inside the refusal path itself. Both driven through the installed CLI, both schema-valid, both verified red-first (a `ValueError` sentinel swap on each `except AssayError`, independently, confirming each test exercises its own distinct code path). | The `LANE_RESOLVED_FIELDS` all-or-nothing contract was found by reading `Verdict`'s own field-group invariant before touching the schema, rather than assuming the filing's three options were the only shapes available — it ruled out omitting `argv_effective`/`env_effective` individually the way option 1's literal wording first suggested, and pointed at the additive-optional-field pattern this same wave already used for B008's `base_resolution` instead. The two fix sites were found by tracing every path that reaches `resolve_command_plan` on a refusal, not just the one the original filing's own NOTE comment happened to mark — `_run_higher_rigor_lane`'s primary call (the actual origin of the bug, per its own now-removed NOTE) and `refuse_lane`'s internal recording call are two independent crash sites with two independent tests, and fixing only one would have left the other's exact original bug standing. |
| A-309 | **B026 N-4 decided: accept and document the three-way asymmetry between `run`+operators, `run`+shard, and `plan`'s own refusal shape, rather than close it this wave.** `reason_code` alone (never free text) is what a refusal names, deliberately closed (A-138/A-170); widening it or plumbing a detail string out of `run_lane` (whose public contract is `Verdict` alone) are both real API commitments the backlog's own filing already flagged as non-trivial, not one-line fixes a stabilization pass should rush. Documented in `docs/DESIGN-GUIDE.md`'s reason-code table section so the asymmetry reads as a known, decided fact rather than something a future reader has to rediscover from the code. | Consistent with this same wave's B024 gate-wiring judgment (defer an invasive, cross-cutting change; document why) rather than force an API-shape decision under stabilization-wave time pressure. Revisit if/when B018-B020 or the CIU v8 prep work gives a concrete reason a consumer needs the free-text cause, rather than widening the vocabulary speculatively now. |
| A-310 | **B026 N-5 decided: `judge.mutation.shard_index`/`shard_count` stay declared, validated, and documented as reserved-for-future-use — not wired as a `--shard` default, not removed.** Wiring would be a real behavior change (a lane with no `--shard` flag on the invocation would newly shard by default) that this build's own callers do not ask for; removing would force a lane-schema migration for a field this build never actually promised worked. Documented directly on the `MutationConfig.shard_index`/`shard_count` fields in `config.py` (the authoritative doc for a field never mentioned in `docs/CONSUMERS.md` to begin with — nothing there was stale, so nothing there needed correcting). The round-3-flagged fourth shard-count ceiling (`config.py`'s ~1784 check reusing `MAX_MAX_MUTANTS`, a DIFFERENT bound that happens to equal 10,000 for its own unrelated reason) is fixed regardless of the wiring decision: a new `MAX_SHARD_COUNT = 10_000`, deliberately duplicated from `verdict.py`'s identically-named constant rather than imported (`verdict.py` already imports FROM `config.py`, so the reverse would be circular — the same tradeoff `COMMAND_TAIL_BYTES` already makes between these two modules), is what a shard count is actually checked against now. | The drift risk was real independent of the wiring decision -- two unrelated ceilings that only happen to share a value today were one accidental future edit to either away from silently diverging, exactly the kind of coincidental coupling this project's own reviews have flagged before (A-298's `MAX_CANDIDATE_CEILING`-vs-`MAX_SHARD_COUNT` split is the identical shape, one module over). Fixing it now, even without wiring, closes the risk permanently rather than leaving it contingent on a decision that went the other way. |
| A-311 | **Round 2 review of A-308's fix found A-308's own "BOTH places" claim was wrong — two MORE `resolve_command_plan` call sites crash on an unresolvable infrastructure declaration, both now fixed.** `run_lane`'s direct R0-only path (its own `resolve_command_plan` call, previously unwrapped) and the `environment_command` probe's plan resolution (which never forwarded `infrastructure_source`/`infrastructure_environment` at ALL, unconditionally crashing on any lane pairing `environment_command` with a `derived:` fact, resolvable or not) are fixed the same way A-308's two sites were: wrapped in `try`/`except AssayError`, delegating to `refuse_lane` with the caught exception's own outcome/reason_code. **The R0-only site matters more than a fourth-site count suggests: `docs/CONSUMERS.md`'s own B013 example lane declares `rigor = ["R0"]`** — the documented shape for the whole feature was the one still silently swallowing its own refusal. Both B025 tests from round 1 used R2 lanes and never exercised it. Two new tests, one per site, both driven through the installed CLI and verified red-first. | Found by the same reviewer session that GO'd round 1's B025 fix, applying its own stated method — "grep for every call site of a pattern before accepting that N were fixed" — to `resolve_command_plan` itself rather than trusting round 1's own accounting. Six call sites exist in total; round 1 fixed two, this finds and fixes two more (a fifth, `canary.py`'s R3 path via `execute_command`, was checked and found to be a separate, pre-existing, structurally different gap — R3 has no infrastructure wiring at all currently — out of scope here and not filed, since nothing in this wave touches R3's infrastructure support one way or the other). |
| A-312 | **Round 2 review found a second, pre-existing `bytes`-decode crash B027's own new code claimed (falsely) to already match: the NORMAL completion path, not just the timeout path.** `default_process_runner`'s `subprocess.run(..., text=True)` carried no `errors=`, so undecodable child output raised an uncaught `UnicodeDecodeError` there too — measured directly (`printf '\377\376'` to stdout), independent of any mutation or timeout machinery. This directly contradicted `_bounded_tail`'s own docstring ("undecodable bytes have already become U+FFFD by then") and B027's `_decode_timeout_stream` docstring ("so a hung mutant's tail reads the same way a completed one's does") — both were describing a policy that was not actually in force on the path they were comparing against. `errors="replace"` on that one `subprocess.run` call makes both docstrings true instead of aspirational. | Pre-existing (present on `main` before this wave), but this wave's own B027 fix newly asserted parity with this path in two separate docstrings, so leaving it unfixed would have shipped a documented claim this same commit could be shown to falsify. Verified red-first: reverting `errors="replace"` reproduces the exact `UnicodeDecodeError` the fix removes. |
| A-313 | **Round 2 review's records-accuracy findings, bundled: seven corrections to what this wave's own commit claimed, none changing prior code fixes.** (1) B026's `Status` downgraded from `RESOLVED` to `PARTIALLY RESOLVED` — both defects named in its own heading remain by design (A-309/A-310 accept them), matching B024's own precedent for exactly this shape. (2) B008's ask was "a `base_resolution` field **+ a WARN**"; only the field shipped — now acknowledged explicitly in B008's status rather than silently claiming the ask was met in full, with the reasoning recorded (a literal WARN needs either a new `Outcome`/`ReasonCode`, the same closed-enum bar B026 N-4 already applies, or a free-text field this project's refusals deliberately don't carry). (3) A-306's comparison of the new infrastructure-value byte bound to B027 was a false analogy (B027 is a `bytes`/`str` decode crash, unrelated to `E2BIG`) — corrected, and its `ARG_MAX` sizing claim softened to what was actually reasoned rather than measured. (4) The pyflakes finding count was given three different, inconsistent numbers across the commit message, A-307, and B024's own text (~20/≈26/~29) — reconciled to the exact, independently-recounted figure, 30. (5) `README.md:92` said `VERDICT_SCHEMA_VERSION = 6` against the actual, current `7` (and the adjacent v5/v4 refusal example was one version behind too) — both corrected; pre-existing staleness, not introduced by this wave, but caught here. (6) `decisions.md`'s A-300 onward were filed under a heading dated 2026-08-16 naming wave 1 and schema v6 — a new, correctly dated "stabilization wave" heading now precedes them, with a note flagging that A-295 through A-299 (the separately-merged B012/B013/B016/B017 remediation) are ALSO misfiled under that same stale heading, left as a known pre-existing gap rather than rewritten here. (7) B021's acceptance criterion silently dropped `README.md` from the original filing's list of docs to update in the same edit that checked the box — now flagged as a deliberate, explained narrowing rather than a quiet edit. | All seven were caught by a dedicated records-accuracy sweep in round 2 review, run separately from the code-behavior verification pass — the same split this project's own review methodology already uses (mutation-test the code, separately audit what the commit claims about itself). None of the seven change any shipped behavior; all are corrections to what the wave's own documentation asserted about itself. |
| A-314 | **B028 filed: a lane-wide `LANE_TIMEOUT` also writes no verdict artifact, same family as B025, wider blast radius, deferred to its own design pass rather than fixed here.** `LaneDeadline.remaining()` raises a bare `AssayError`/`BUDGET_EXCEEDED`/`LANE_TIMEOUT` from roughly 16 separate call sites across `runner.py`/`mutation.py`/`canary.py` — every one a place the same uncaught crash could fire, not just the plan-resolution moment B025's fixes cover. Measured: a lane whose command simply outlives `budget_seconds` (no mutation, no infrastructure) writes no verdict, identical before and after this wave. Not fixed here because an outer catch boundary needs to reason about partial side effects (a materialized snapshot, a half-written mutation-state record) a `LaneDeadline` expiry can leave behind mid-operation — a real design question B025's simpler "always `BAD_LANE_CONFIG`, always safe to fall back to `lane.env`" shape did not have to answer. | Found by round 2 review driving the same "does this post-HEAD-resolution terminal path emit an artifact" question B025 answered, against a different trigger. Filed rather than fixed because the fix shape genuinely differs (one outer boundary per higher-rigor entry point, versus B025's per-call-site wrapping) and deciding between them needs the same care B025's own three-option filing got, not a rushed patch in the same pass that just closed a narrower, better-understood version of the same family. |
| A-315 | **B029 filed: R3's canary side-run has no infrastructure wiring at all, and reports a misattributed R3 claim rather than crashing — filed, not fixed, per round-2 review's own recommendation.** `canary.py`'s side-run resolves a second `CommandPlan` via `execute_command`, which has never accepted `infrastructure_source`/`infrastructure_environment` (its docstring now says so explicitly — see the same round's `execute_command` step-1 correction). Confirmed reachable and non-crashing: an `AssayError` from the unresolved fact is already caught where `run_isolated_canary` is called and turned into an R3 `Claim`, so a lane whose `derived:` fact resolves correctly everywhere else reports `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` on ITS R3 claim specifically — a consumer would chase a config problem that does not exist. `required-env:` facts silently succeed on this same path (falling back to `os.environ`), so the two source kinds diverge in behavior with no relationship to either one's actual resolvability. | Found while round 2 review verified B025's fix by tracing every `resolve_command_plan`/`execute_command` call site, not just the ones B025's own filing already named. Structurally a missing feature (R3 canaries were never wired for infrastructure) rather than a missing refusal the way B025's four sites were, so it needs the same kind of design decision B028 does — whether the canary side-run should share the main command's infrastructure world at all — rather than a same-pass patch. |
| A-316 | **The W2 locked schema drift-guard (`carve-assets/W2/verdict.schema.v7.json`) was re-witnessed, closing accumulated drift from B012 (pre-dating this wave) plus this wave's own B008/B025 fields — found by the registered gate itself at release time, not by any `pytest tests/` run.** `tests/test_acceptance_v7.py::test_shipped_schema_is_byte_identical_to_the_locked_v7_asset` lives under `nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/`, outside `tests/`, so it is exercised only by `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh` — never by this wave's own repeated `pytest tests/` runs, which is exactly why it went unnoticed until `cmru release` ran the real gate. The diff against the locked copy showed FAR more drift than this wave's own two fields: the whole B012 mutation-shard schema surface (`shard_index`/`shard_count`, `candidate_ids`, `progress_artifact`, the shard all-or-nothing conditional) was also missing from the lock, confirmed via `git log` to have never been touched since the v7 schema's original carve commit (`f3ce3d0a`) despite B012 shipping afterward. Re-witnessed by copying the current shipped schema over the lock (a plain file copy, not a captured-run normalization, since this drift-guard's own "witness" IS the shipped schema file) after diffing to confirm every change was additive schema surface from named, already-shipped or already-reviewed backlog items, never an unexplained or accidental change. | A-274's "re-witness when a ruling deliberately changes what assay produces" precedent applies directly: this file is a live drift guard for the CURRENTLY ACTIVE schema version, not historical evidence of a retired version the way A-222's v4-locked buckets are (nothing in `P33/migration-manifest.json`'s `locked_carver_owned` bucket, the only manifest naming such paths, names this file, and it predates v7's existence entirely). The v5/v6 locked schemas in `W1`/`P33` needed no equivalent fix — both are frozen, retired versions under this project's own hard-cut policy, so they have nothing left to legitimately drift. |
| A-317 | **A genuine bug, found by the registered gate at release time, not by any test this wave had already written: `assay.verify`'s hand-written raw-document reconstruction layer never learned about either of this wave's two new verdict fields, so `assay verify`/`verify_document` rejected a real producer artifact carrying either one.** `_reconstruct_judgment_resolved` built its `JudgmentResolved` without passing `base_resolution=raw.get("base_resolution")` through, and `_reconstruct_verdict`'s `lane_kwargs` never forwarded `env_effective_incomplete`; both omissions made `_reject_unknown_keys` correctly (from its own narrow view) report the field as unknown, since the reconstructed comparison dict never had it. Both fixed with a one-line addition each. This is a THIRD place a new verdict field must be registered, distinct from the JSON Schema file and the `Verdict`/`JudgmentResolved` dataclasses themselves — `assay.verify` reconstructs its own comparison object from the raw untrusted document rather than trusting it, by design (A-182's "the raw layer and the model layer are independently maintained, on purpose, so a producer bug and a verifier bug are never the same bug"), which is exactly why updating the dataclass and the schema was not enough. | Found because `cmru release`'s registered gate runs `gate/python/qualify_topos.py`, which drives a REAL `assay run` against a real project and compares its artifact through machinery that (indirectly, via the same reconstruction path `assay verify` uses) exercises this exact check — a path nothing in `tests/` reaches, since every test this wave wrote checked JSON-Schema-validity alone (`why_invalid(validator, document)`), never round-tripped a real artifact through `assay.verify.verify_document` itself. Two new regression tests close this specific gap directly (`tests/test_verify_layer_independence.py`), verified red-first. |
| A-318 | **Four frozen `carve-assets/W2/expected/*.json` templates (`sql-r2-v7-template.json`, `ca4-all-equivalent-v7-template.json`, `p25-pass-v7-template.json`, `p25-missing-v7-template.json`) needed `base_resolution: "merge-base"` added to their `judgment.resolved` blocks, re-witnessed for the same reason as A-316's W2 schema lock — the four are R1/R2 fixtures (each declares `judgment.resolved.base`), so a real run against them now legitimately carries the new field.** Two other W2 templates (`ca1-r3-no-base`, `missing-tool`) declare no `base` at all and needed no change — `base_resolution` is never present without `base` (enforced in `JudgmentResolved.__post_init__`). Added via a targeted text edit preserving each file's existing hand-formatted key order/indentation, not a full re-serialization, so the diff is exactly the one new line per file. | Found by `gate/python/qualify_topos.py::compare_complete_artifact`'s exact-equality check against these same four templates, the same gate run that surfaced A-317. Confirmed via the project's own `test_acceptance_v7.py` schema-validity tests staying green throughout (they never caught this, because an ABSENT optional field is still schema-valid) — this is a second, independent illustration of A-317's point: schema validity and byte-for-byte real-run fidelity are different questions, checked by different layers, and this wave's own testing only ever answered the first one for these four templates before the gate answered the second. |

## Decided — B030/B031/B032 observability remediation, 2026-08-25 (the `8a2a4731` review-gap wave)

Filed against `reports/assay-review-gap-audit-2026-08-25.md` §6 — the first
independent review of `8a2a4731` (shipped in assay-v2.2.0 as B010 + part of
B012). Every defect below was reproduced through the installed CLI against a
real Git repository BEFORE any code was touched, and re-driven through the same
CLI after; the before/after transcripts are in
`reports/assay-B030-B032-remediation-REPORT.md`.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-319 | **B030 fixed: `assay plan` discovers candidates against the CONSUMER's own project root, not a relocated snapshot root — the `_relocate_source_roots` call is deleted from `_cmd_plan` outright rather than repaired with a different argument.** `_relocate_source_roots` exists for ONE situation: a lane judged inside a MATERIALIZED snapshot, whose `judge.source_root_paths` must be respelled against that snapshot's own project root (A-149). `_cmd_plan` never materializes a snapshot — it PREPARES one (`isolation.prepare_snapshot`) and reads blobs straight out of the prepared seed via `_read_prepared_source_text`, handing both target resolvers `snapshot_repo_top=prepared.spec.repo_top`, which IS the consumer's real repository top. The roots those resolvers' containment gate must be compared against are therefore the lane's own declared roots, unrelocated. The commit's own test `test_plan_reports_candidates_without_executing` asserted `candidate_count == 0` against a fixture that genuinely yields one candidate; that assertion is corrected to the true count in the same commit, with the candidate id proved equal to `assay.mutation.candidate_id`'s own answer for the job a real run executes. A second test covers the `mode = "whole_target"` half. | `8a2a4731` passed `scratch_project_root=(prepared.spec.scratch_root / "unused")` — a directory literally named `"unused"` that is never created — so `resolve_mutation_targets`'s unconditional `any(abs_path.is_relative_to(root) ...)` containment gate could not be satisfied by ANY lane, and `assay plan` reported `candidate_count: 0` universally while a `whole_target` lane failed outright naming the phantom path. Deleting the call rather than passing a "correct" root is the honest fix: there is no snapshot project root to relocate against on this path, and inventing one (e.g. materializing a snapshot purely to have a root) would make `plan` do the very work its own contract says it does not do ("never runs the lane command or a mutant"). Measured before/after on one repository, one lane, one commit: `plan` `0` → `1`, matching `run`'s own `candidate_count` and the run's progress-artifact `candidate_id` byte-for-byte (`254df665…`). |
| A-320 | **B031 decided and fixed: R2 progress state lives where the CONSUMER names it (`assay run --progress PATH`) or nowhere at all, and `mutation.progress_artifact` is REMOVED from the dataclass, the wire payload and the JSON Schema together rather than left as an inert field.** Three defects, one feature. (a) `runner.py` wrote `Path(".assay") / f"{lane.name}.progress.jsonl"` unconditionally for every R2 lane into the consumer's LIVE worktree — reproduced: run 1 PASSes and leaves an untracked file, run 2 with nothing changed fails `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`DIRTY_TREE`, so an R2 lane passed once and refused forever. Progress is now opt-in, absent by default, and its destination is a CLI argument resolved against the invoking CWD, exactly like `--verdict-json`'s. (b) Nothing ever constructed `Mutation(progress_artifact=...)`, at the commit or on `main`. Applying A-292's own reasoning ("never the consumer's real worktree") to this feature leaves the field with no truthful value it can carry: its ONLY legal spelling is `_check_wire_path`'s repo-tree-relative grammar, i.e. precisely the forbidden location, and an absolute machine-local path in a portable artifact would be worse. So it is removed, not wired — the `--verdict-json` precedent is that assay does not record back a destination its caller chose. B012 requirement 1's "summarize the artifact path in the verdict" is therefore deliberately NOT met, and B012's box is corrected rather than re-checked. (c) `verify.py`'s `_reconstruct_mutation` never read `candidate_ids` either, so `assay verify` refused a schema-valid document carrying it; `candidate_ids` is registered there AND given its first real producer (a `--shard` run now records its own assignment domain). The lane-name path-traversal gap closes by construction — no lane name is interpolated into any path any more. Related, same edit: the progress stream opens with a `run` header (commit + start time) so a monitor tailing an appended file can attribute a `candidate_index`, and its whole-mutated-file digest is renamed `mutated_file_sha256` instead of colliding with the verdict's replacement-TEXT `replacement_sha256` under one name. | Removing a never-populated optional field is not a wire break: no producer at any released version ever emitted it, so no v7 document in existence can be invalidated, and a schema-version bump would be ceremony over a field that never existed on the wire. The alternative — keeping it and populating it — was worked through and rejected on its own terms rather than for effort: to be recordable at all the destination would have to be forced inside the project tree, which re-creates the exact `DIRTY_TREE` defect (a) exists to close, and making presence conditional on "the destination happened to be inside the repo" would leave ABSENCE ambiguous between "no progress" and "progress somewhere unrecordable". Keeping the writer but dropping the self-reference gives the consumer everything they can actually use (a live-tailable NDJSON stream at a path they chose) with none of it. `candidate_ids` is kept for the opposite reason: it is expressible truthfully in a portable artifact (digests, no filesystem coupling) and its producer is four lines on the shard path that already computes the ids. |
| A-321 | **B032 scope decided: exactly ONE probe outcome distinction survives into the verdict — timeout vs. everything else.** A probe that exhausts its own preflight cap reports `BUDGET_EXCEEDED`/`LANE_TIMEOUT`, exit 4. A missing binary (`ERROR`/`EXEC_FAILED` from `execute_plan`), a nonzero exit and a signal death (both `FAIL`/`COMMAND_FAILED`) all keep rendering `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`, exit 2. `_probe_refusal_terminal` is the one written copy of that mapping. | The four causes were collapsed into one hardcoded `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` by `8a2a4731`, which discarded `execute_plan`'s own correct classification — so a genuine budget overrun was recorded as an operator config error at exit 2 instead of exit 4. Measured on `main`: `budget = "30s"` with `environment_command = sleep 45` → `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`. That is the false-evidence case: a gate that RETRIES `BUDGET_EXCEEDED` and HARD-FAILS `BAD_LANE_CONFIG` — the estate's own run-gate shape — does exactly the wrong thing on a real timeout, which is why this is the distinction that must not collapse. The other three are NOT separated in the verdict because they mean the same actionable thing to a consumer ("the lane as declared cannot be honoured in this environment") and separating them would cost a closed-enum widening every consumer's schema copy would then reject — the price A-138/A-170 make deliberate. What actually differed between them was never the reason code; it was the diagnosis, which A-322 puts on stderr where free text belongs. |
| A-322 | **B032's other two halves fixed: the probe's 30 s cap is applied where `execute_plan` actually reads it, and B010's "clear message" ships as real bytes on a caller-supplied diagnostics stream.** `execute_plan` reads its `timeout=` ARGUMENT and ignores `plan.budget_seconds` entirely; `8a2a4731` set `budget_seconds=min(30.0, deadline.remaining())` and then passed `timeout=deadline.remaining()`, the FULL lane budget. One expression (`probe_timeout = min(PROBE_BUDGET_SECONDS, deadline.remaining())`, with `PROBE_BUDGET_SECONDS = 30.0` a named module constant) now feeds both, so the cap is enforced rather than decorative. Separately, `run_lane` grows an optional `diagnostics: TextIO | None` parameter that `assay.cli` passes its own `err` stream, and `_report_probe_refusal` writes the lane name, the specific cause, and the DECLARED WRAPPER to run via — plus the probe's own bounded stdout/stderr tails when it produced any. | The cap: measured on `main`, an `environment_command` of `sleep 45` under `budget = "5m"` ran the whole 45 s and then PASSED — a hung probe could spend the entire lane budget before the lane's own command started, which is the opposite of what a 30 s preflight cap is for. The message: B010's ask was verbatim "refusing with 'this lane's declared environment does not match the invoking one; run via `<declared wrapper>`' instead of surfacing the suite's raw traceback", and what shipped wrote **0 bytes** to stderr while emitting a `BAD_LANE_CONFIG` whose `argv_effective` names the LANE's command — which never executed — so the artifact actively misled. A stream rather than a verdict field because `run_lane`'s public contract is `Verdict` alone and refusals deliberately carry no free text (A-138/A-170, restated at A-309); the CLI already prints every other typed refusal it catches exactly this way. The timeout wording deliberately does NOT reuse B010's "does not match the invoking one": a probe that never finished proved nothing about the environment either way, and claiming a mismatch would be an invented fact. |
| A-323 | **A live, previously unfiled instance of the SAME registration-point drift, found while verifying A-320's fix: `verify.py`'s `_reconstruct_judgment_r2` never read `shard_index`/`shard_count`, so `assay verify` rejected a REAL `assay run <lane> --shard 0/N` artifact.** Reproduced end to end through the installed CLI — a sharded run emits `judgment.r2.shard_index`/`shard_count` (A-296 fixed that half), the document validates against the shipped JSON Schema cleanly, and `assay verify` on assay's OWN output returns `schema: unknown judgment.r2 field(s): ['shard_count', 'shard_index']`, exit 1. Fixed with the same one-line-each forward A-317 used, in the same commit as A-320's `candidate_ids` registration, and guarded by a test that round-trips both. | Found by driving a real sharded run and feeding its artifact straight back into `assay verify`, precisely the round-trip A-317 observed that `tests/` never performs — not by reading the diff, and not by any part of B031's own filing, which named `progress_artifact` and `candidate_ids` and stopped there. Filed as its own A-number rather than folded into A-320 because it is a different field group on a different object, shipped by a different commit (`7a4f6333`), and because the count matters: this is now the THIRD and FOURTH field to reach `main` schema-valid and verify-invalid from the same B012 work, which is evidence about the review process, not just about two fields. |
| A-324 | **A-320's no-bump justification restated: round-2 review found "no producer ever emitted `progress_artifact`" alone does not license removing it from schema v7 without a version bump — a hand-authored or third-party v7 document could still have carried the field, schema-legally, and would be invalidated by its removal. The load-bearing fact, confirmed independently and not previously stated in A-320: no released `assay verify` ever accepted such a document EITHER.** `verify.py`'s `_reconstruct_mutation` never read `progress_artifact` at any point in its history, so `_reject_unknown_keys` rejected any document carrying it regardless of schema validity — this is A-320(c)/B031(c) itself, restated as the reason the removal is safe rather than merely as a separate registration-gap finding. The field's status moves from "schema-legal, verifier-rejected" to "rejected by both"; nothing that ever successfully ran `assay verify` stops verifying. No schema-version bump is needed on THAT basis — not on the weaker "no producer" basis alone. Cross-reference, not resolved here: `progress_artifact` was originally requested by name in `dstdns/docs/proposals/tools/assay-mutation-requirements.md:58` ("The same records must be summarized in the final verdict JSON under a stable key such as `mutation.progress_artifact`") — A-320 is declining a named consumer ask, not merely deleting dead code the way A-320's own prose reads today. That fact belongs in the next assay→dstdns release-notify; this line exists only so it is not lost before then. | This file is append-only (see header); A-320's own row is left as shipped rather than rewritten, and this entry supersedes its reasoning without erasing it. The distinction matters because "no producer" is a fact about assay's own commit history, which a future producer could falsify by simply adding one; "no verifier ever accepted it" is a fact about every consumer that could have depended on the field, which is the actual question a no-bump removal must answer. Both facts happen to hold today, but only the second is the reason removal is SAFE. |

## Decided — B033/B034 remediation, 2026-08-26 (the `ba8908d6` / `126ef577` review-gap wave)

Filed against `reports/assay-review-gap-audit-2026-08-25.md` §5 and §1 — the
first independent review of `ba8908d6` (whole-target SQL mutation targets) and
`126ef577`/`6324548d` (B015's semantic Python operators), both shipped in
assay-v2.3.0 with zero decision records. Every defect below was reproduced
through the installed released CLI (`assay 2.4.1`) against a real Git
repository BEFORE any code was touched, and re-driven through the fixed CLI
after; the before/after transcripts are in
`reports/assay-B033-B034-remediation-REPORT.md`.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
