| A-325 | **B033 decided and fixed: `judge.mode` is a LANE-level judging SCOPE that R1 and R2 read together, not an R1-only strategy — and R2's whole-target resolver REFUSES a declared target that fails a containment gate, exactly as R1's does, rather than silently narrowing the set it judges.** Four consequences, one feature (`ba8908d6`'s `_mutation_targets_whole`/`whole_file_r2`). (a) **Refuse, never narrow.** `_mutation_targets_whole` `continue`d past an excluded directory, a non-matching `source_glob` and a test path; it now raises `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` naming the target and the gate, mirroring `evaluate._resolve_whole_target`'s own five refusals, plus a new one for a target ABSENT at the judged commit (previously an unnamed `GIT_FAILED` from the read one layer down). Reproduced on `main`: a lane declaring `["db/schema.sql", "db/tests/fixtures.sql"]` reported `PASS` having mutated only the first, with nothing anywhere in the verdict naming the second. (b) **No base for a comparison that never ran.** `compares_a_base` was `judgment_r1 is not None or judgment_r2 is not None`; a whole-target R2 skips both `check_base_is_head` and the `git diff`, and a whole-target R1 resolves no base either, so `judgment.resolved.base`/`base_resolution` are now omitted on any whole-target lane. (c) **`judge.base` is inert config on a whole-target lane of ANY language and ANY rigor** and is refused as surplus at load — the two `declared_language != "sql"` carve-outs are deleted, including the one that could never fire on an R2 lane and whose only live effect was forcing a SQL R1-only lane to declare a base `docs/CONSUMERS.md` calls FORBIDDEN. (d) **The vacuity guard applies to every language again**: `judge.targets` without `judge.mode = "whole_target"` is refused for SQL as it always was for Python, and `targets` is removed from the unconditional surplus exemption. `mode` now requires R1 **or** R2 rather than R1. The refusal cause is written to the caller's diagnostics stream (A-322's channel), because `judgment.r2` carries no `targets` field and gains none here. | (a) is R1's own docstring applied one tier down: "a directory target expanding to N files of which only one is measured would PASS while leaving the rest unjudged, which is precisely the vacuity hole this whole mode exists to close." R2 reopened it, and from the artifact alone "judged and clean" was indistinguishable from "never mutated" — the ALL-skipped case is honest (`INCONCLUSIVE`/`NO_MUTANTS`), the PARTIAL case renders a real PASS over a silently narrowed set. Silent narrowing was rejected on B006(b)/A-292's standing rule (never invent a fact, never silently narrow scope) rather than on preference. (b) is `_build_judgment_resolved`'s own docstring, which the shipped code contradicted: "recording one would be an invented fact rather than a missing one." (c) follows from (b) by A-062/A-260: `resolved_base` reaches exactly three consumers — `evaluate_r1` (which ignores it in whole-target mode), the skipped `check_base_is_head`/`git diff`, and the artifact — so under whole-target scope nothing reads it and a REQUIRED field nothing reads is the defect class this project exists to catch. **This is a deliberate, loud consumer break**: dstdns's `cw2b_schema` lane declares `base = "origin/main"` and must delete that line; the alternative was a config that demands a value the artifact then refuses to record, which is worse and less legible. (d) the guard's own error message names the hole the carve-out reopened ("silently declaring one is how a consumer comes to believe a floor is enforced when it is not"), and the carve-out was never needed: `mode = "whole_target"` is one line, dstdns's own SQL lane already spells it, and with it spelled the guard is satisfied for SQL exactly as for Python. **Schema v7 is NOT bumped, and that was checked rather than assumed** (A-324's test): the `base` conditional in the packaged schema, the raw verifier and the model all had to be relaxed from "required IF r2" to "required IF r1 in changed-line mode / forbidden IF r1 in whole-target mode", which is a pure WIDENING — no document that validated before stops validating, and no released `assay verify` rejects anything it used to accept. The narrowing half is now enforced only where the artifact can WITNESS it: `judgment.r1.mode` is on the wire and `judgment.r2`'s scope is not, so an r2-with-no-r1 document constrains neither way. Asserting a rule the document cannot witness is how a verifier starts rejecting honest artifacts — the very defect being fixed — so the gap is filed as B035 (give `judgment.r2` its own `mode`/`targets`, a schema-version change) rather than papered over. The `lines` off-by-one at the same site (`text.count("\\n") + 2` claiming a line past the end of every newline-terminated file) is fixed in passing; it was harmless because sites are byte-offset based. |
| A-326 | **B034 decided: `python:uuid-equality-swap` and `python:enum-comparison-swap` are WITHDRAWN — the producer and the lane-declaration surface are deleted, and the two names survive in `MUTATION_OPERATORS_BY_LANGUAGE`/the packaged schema's `oneOf` ONLY until the next schema-version bump, which is when the spellings go.** Withdrawn rather than redesigned, and the redesign was worked through on its own terms rather than declined for effort. Fixed together: `_semantic_comparison_sites`, `_is_enum_member_expression` and `_is_uuid_expression` are deleted (so the double-counting of every co-selected site and the stale-`left` mis-splice die with them), `config._load_mutation` and both `--operators` paths refuse either name by name with the reason, and the four producible `python:*` operators are the original A-112/A-114 catalogue again. B015's own status is corrected to WITHDRAWN, not IMPLEMENTED. | The measured finding is total, not partial: 87 sites over `src/assay/**.py`, zero that `python:compare-swap` did not already produce at the same byte span with the same replacement bytes, so no amount of de-duplication or predicate-tightening produces new coverage — which is the backlog's own stated bar and B015's own "reject or split accordingly" instruction, never carried out. **Redesign was evaluated and rejected on this project's own governance, not on difficulty.** A genuinely distinct UUID rule does exist in the abstract (splice `==`→`is` rather than `==`→`!=`: `compare-swap` never maps `Eq` to `Is`, and an in-place UUID construction makes the identity comparison provably False, so it is a strictly harder-to-kill mutant carrying information `Eq→NotEq` does not) — but it is NEW mutation-testing design, which A-112 forbids in as many words ("this package reuses ONLY the catalogue, never extends it") and which A-221 already invoked once to exclude a Go `falsy-swap` analogue as "new, unmeasured mutation-testing design"; it would also make an operator NAMED `uuid-equality-swap` stop swapping equality, i.e. a rename, which the vocabulary module's own docstring says is a version bump rather than a widening. The enum half has no redesign at all: `is` on an enum member is an EQUIVALENT mutant (members are singletons), a `.value` splice turns every false positive into a crashed mutant rather than a survived one, and no single-file AST can tell an enum member from `cfg.debug` without cross-module resolution — the shipped predicate matched any `name.attr`, and its own docstring's second example (`enums.Color.RED`) was rejected by the predicate it documented. **The spelling outlives the behaviour, and that asymmetry is A-324's test applied honestly.** A-320 could delete `progress_artifact` at v7 because no released `assay verify` had ever ACCEPTED a document carrying it. The opposite holds here, and it was re-measured rather than recalled: a real `assay run` on 2.4.1 emits `python:enum-comparison-swap` in a verdict and `assay verify` on that verdict exits 0. Deleting the names from the catalogue would delete them from the schema's per-language `oneOf` (the vocabulary IS the schema's source, asserted for membership AND order), so real artifacts already on disk would stop verifying — the exact breakage A-324 says needs a version bump. Bumping v7→v8 to withdraw two dead operators would invalidate EVERY v7 artifact, a far larger break than the one it prevents, and would drag the frozen `carve-assets/W2/verdict.schema.v7.json` acceptance assets into a restructuring that belongs to a carve, not a bugfix wave. So: behaviour now, spelling at the next bump. `vocabulary.py`'s own contract already draws this line — "membership here says a name is spellable, NOT that the lane declaring it may use it" — and the `go:*` family is the standing precedent for a name that is legal to spell and impossible to produce (`adapters/go.py`'s `generate_mutation_sites` returns `"UNSUPPORTED"` unconditionally, A-042/A-183). **Round-2 correction, checked rather than recalled:** this sentence originally cited `go:*`/`sql:*` together. `sql:*` does NOT belong in it — `adapters/sql.py`'s own docstring reads "Never `UNSUPPORTED` — SQL has an engine (the lexer)", dstdns's `cw2b_schema` declares all seven, and the registered gate qualifies them. The ruling is unaffected: one real precedent carries it, and citing a second one that turns out to produce mutants would have been exactly the unverified-citation defect A-105/A-112 already caught twice in this project. |

## Decided — B018/B019/B035 v8 synergy wave, 2026-08-30 (the CIU-V8 gate-proposal wave)

Filed against `nyxloom-trove/4-backlog.md` B018/B019/B035, all three traced to
`/workspaces/vbpub/ciu/docs/CIU-V8-TESTING-GATE-PROPOSAL.md` (§11.3, §10.10, and
A-325's own deferred gap). B018's identification logic was designed against four
REAL invocations built and run before any code was written — a wheel installed
into a venv, that same install shadowed by a source `sys.path` entry, a bare
`pip install -e .` checkout, and a `.pyz` zipapp — not against a reading of the
`importlib.metadata` docs; the transcripts are in
`reports/assay-B018-B019-B035-v8-synergy-REPORT.md`.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-327 | **B018 decided: `judge_provenance` is an OPTIONAL top-level verdict object carrying exactly five fields, all required when it is present — `name`, `version`, `artifact`, `digest_algorithm`, `digest` — and it is ABSENT-or-COMPLETE, never partial. (The four identity facts B018 asks for are `name`/`version`/`digest_algorithm`/`digest`; `artifact` names which of the two release files the digest is OF, which a consumer comparing digests has to know.) An invocation that cannot identify its own build artifact records nothing, says so on the diagnostics stream, and continues; `assay run --require-judge-provenance` turns that into an `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` refusal.** The resolution order is: `importlib.metadata.distribution("assay")` -> Name/Version -> **shadowing guard** -> zipapp branch (`zipimport.zipimporter.archive`) -> wheel branch (PEP 610 `direct_url.json`, `archive_info.hashes.sha256` with the legacy `archive_info.hash = "sha256=..."` spelling accepted). The digest is lowercased and then validated as 64 hex characters, so a malformed PEP 610 record yields UNIDENTIFIED, never a garbage digest. `identify_judge()` returns `(provenance \| None, reason \| None)` and raises for nothing. | **Every branch was measured against a real artifact, not inferred, and the measuring is what produced the design.** (a) The **shadowing guard is a genuine laundering hole found by measurement, not a hypothetical**: `importlib.metadata.distribution("assay")` succeeding does NOT prove the running code came from that distribution — a venv with assay installed plus a source tree on `sys.path` resolves the distribution's metadata while executing entirely different bytes, and this repo's own `gate/python/qualify_dstdns_sql.py` invokes the CLI in exactly that shape. Without the guard, a source-tree run would have recorded the installed wheel's digest, which is precisely the invented fact B018 exists to prevent ("REFUSE rather than invent a digest"). The guard compares the resolved `assay.__file__` against `dist.locate_file("")` and refuses when the former is outside the latter, naming both paths. (b) `Path(str(dist.locate_file("")))` and not `Path(dist.locate_file(""))`: inside a zipapp `locate_file` returns a `zipp.Path`, which is not `os.PathLike` and which `pathlib.Path()` rejects outright — this was a `TypeError` on the first real zipapp run, fixed and then commented in-code as measured rather than left to be rediscovered. (c) **Absent-not-refusing was chosen over a hard refusal because a hard refusal breaks assay's own test suite**: the source checkout is the third of the three real invocation forms, it can never carry a build-artifact digest, and making it fatal would make `pip install -e .` unusable. A silent absence is the other failure mode, so absence is loud on the diagnostics channel (A-322) and the opt-in flag exists for callers — the registered gate among them — that legitimately require identity. (d) Absent-or-complete rather than best-effort-partial follows A-051 ("omitted, never null") and the project's standing rule against half-facts: a `judge_provenance` carrying a name and version but no digest would read as identity while proving nothing, and a consumer comparing digests cannot tell "not recorded" from "recorded and different" if the field is present-but-hollow. |
| A-328 | **B019 decided: a lane DELEGATES its comparison base by declaring `judge.base_source = "request"` (closed vocabulary `{"declared", "request"}`, default `"declared"`), and the invoking gate supplies it per-invocation as `assay run --request-base REF` / `assay plan --request-base REF`. All three mismatches are LOUD `LaneConfigError` -> `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` refusals resolved in one place, `runner.resolve_base_declaration(lane, request_base)`, before any dispatch: a delegating lane with no `--request-base`, a `--request-base` handed to a lane that declares its own base, and a `--request-base` handed to a lane that reads no base at all (whole-target scope, or no R1/R2). Declaring BOTH `judge.base` and `base_source = "request"` is refused at load.** | **The naming is deliberate and was chosen against a specific confusion:** `judge.base` is the lane's own answer, `base_source` names WHO answers, and `--request-base` reads as "the invoking gate request's base" — the CIU proposal §10.10's actual subject — rather than as an override of `judge.base`, which it is emphatically not. **Precedence was REFUSED rather than picked, on A-062's own argument.** Both-present has two defensible readings (lane-wins is "config is truth"; request-wins is "the invoker knows the PR"), CIU's proposal wants the second — a gate that computes a merge-base per pull request and hands it down — but a lane that ALSO spells `base = "origin/main"` is then carrying a line nothing reads, and A-062's rule is that config nothing reads "cannot fail loudly if it is wrong", which is this project's named defect class (B033/A-325 deleted exactly such a field one wave ago). So the config is made unambiguous at load instead: to delegate, delete your `base`. Picking a precedence would have bought convenience with an inert line; refusing costs one deletion, once. **The third refusal — request-base to a lane that reads no base — is the same rule pointed the other way** and exists because the delegating gate is a script: a gate that starts passing `--request-base` to every lane it drives must be told which of its lanes cannot use it, not have the value silently discarded on the whole-target ones. All three are resolved in ONE function called once above the dispatch, so `run` and `plan` cannot drift apart in what they accept — `plan` calls it purely for the refusal and discards the value. |
| A-329 | **B035 decided, and it is THE v7->v8 cut: `judgment.r2` gains `mode` (required) and `targets` (optional), mirroring `judgment.r1`, so an R2-only verdict can witness its own judging scope. `judge.mode` being a LANE-level scope (A-325) is now enforced across the tiers: r1 and r2 present together must agree on both `mode` and `targets`, and the base rules are re-expressed against an r1-else-r2 witness rather than r1 alone.** Enforced in all three layers per A-182 — the frozen model (`verdict.Judgment.__post_init__`), the hand-written raw verifier (`verify._check_base_matches_the_tiers_present`, with deliberately different wording from the model's so a copy-paste stub cannot pass both), and the packaged JSON Schema (`judgment.allOf` rewritten with `anyOf` witnesses plus two mode-agreement implications). | This closes the hole A-325 filed against itself in as many words: "`judgment.r1.mode` is on the wire and `judgment.r2`'s scope is not, so an r2-with-no-r1 document constrains neither way. Asserting a rule the document cannot witness is how a verifier starts rejecting honest artifacts." A-325 could only RELAX the base rule (a pure widening, no bump) precisely because the artifact could not witness the narrowing half; putting `mode` on r2 is what makes the narrowing assertable, and asserting it is what makes this a break. **The break is real and was checked rather than assumed**: a v7 `judgment.r2` has no `mode`, `mode` is required under v8, so every existing v7 verdict stops validating — a hard cut in A-170's sense (no dual-version verifier, no upgrade-in-place), which is why B035 was batched alone as the wave's one version-bumping item. **The r1/r2 agreement checks are not decoration**: without them a document could claim `r1.mode = "changed_lines"` and `r2.mode = "whole_target"`, which is not a lane assay can produce and would make `resolved.base` simultaneously required and forbidden — the model names that contradiction explicitly rather than resolving it by precedence. `targets` is optional-and-agreeing rather than required so that a changed-line R2 (which declares none) is not forced to emit an empty list, keeping A-051's omitted-never-null. |
| A-330 | **Batching decided as three items in one branch but ONE version bump, and the v8 carve-assets go in a new frozen `nyxloom-trove/carve-assets/W4/` directory whose name is a WAVE identity, not a schema version — so `W3` is skipped rather than reused or renamed.** B018 is additive-optional under v7 semantics, B019 is lane-config and CLI only, B035 is the bump; all three land together and the artifact they produce is a v8 verdict. `W4/` carries `verdict.schema.v8.json` (byte-identical to the shipped file), `test_acceptance_v8.py`, six migrated `expected/*-v8-template.json`, and a `MANIFEST.md`. | **The convention was inferred from how W1->W2 actually happened rather than invented**: the P33(v5) -> W1(v6) -> W2(v7) generations are each a frozen directory named for the WAVE that cut them, never rewritten afterwards, each carrying its own full acceptance suite plus a `test_shipped_schema_is_byte_identical_to_the_locked_vN_asset` drift guard and a test rejecting every EARLIER frozen template. W4 follows that move exactly, including the differential negatives over W1+W2. `W3` is skipped because `W3/` already exists as a wave directory with its own contents — renumbering it to keep names and versions aligned would rewrite a frozen generation, which is the one thing this convention forbids; the `MANIFEST.md` records that explicitly so the gap does not read as a lost asset. **The frozen-asset lockstep is treated as a hard rule, not a habit**: this project has twice shipped a schema change whose frozen drift-guard copy fell out of sync, so every edit to `src/assay/schemas/verdict.schema.json` in this wave is followed by re-freezing `W4/verdict.schema.v8.json` in the SAME commit, verified with `cmp`, not by re-running the guard afterwards and hoping. One consequence is recorded rather than hidden: `carve-assets/W3/expected/dstdns-sql-r2-v6-witness.json` was migrated in place (schema_version 8, `mode` on its r2) and its filename left saying `-v6-`, following the v7 precedent set by `b6d9615c`; the name is stale in both generations and correcting it is a rename of a frozen asset, so it is flagged for the reviewer instead of taken unilaterally. |
| A-331 | **A-326's deferred obligation is DISCHARGED here: `python:uuid-equality-swap` and `python:enum-comparison-swap` lose their SPELLINGS at the v7->v8 cut — gone from `vocabulary.MUTATION_OPERATORS_BY_LANGUAGE["python"]` and from the packaged schema's per-language `oneOf`. `WITHDRAWN_MUTATION_OPERATORS` survives the deletion and its members are deliberately no longer a subset of `MUTATION_OPERATORS`, so the withdrawn-by-name refusal now runs BEFORE the unknown-operator check in both `config._load_mutation` and the CLI's two `--operators` paths.** | A-326 kept the two spellings for exactly one stated reason — released `assay verify` builds ACCEPT a v7 document naming either, and released `assay run` builds EMITTED such documents, so deleting them mid-v7 would have stopped real artifacts from verifying — and wrote its own deadline: "the spelling therefore stays until the next bump, where it is dropped". **B035 IS that bump**, and the compatibility being bought is already gone: under v8 those same documents are refused on `schema_version` alone, so the deletion costs nothing v8 had not already cost. Leaving it undischarged was the alternative and was rejected: an obligation whose deadline is a specific event, skipped at that event because a later brief did not name it, does not survive to the next one — it becomes the frozen-asset drift class in slower motion, and this wave is the second time in three that this project has had to correct a record that quietly went stale. **The refusal REORDERING is load-bearing and is the whole cost of the deletion.** Under v7 the two orders were indistinguishable, because the names were still in the catalogue and "unknown" could not fire on them at all; with the spellings gone, a consumer whose lane file still carries one is now literally naming an unknown operator, and answering them with a bare "unknown operator(s)" would name the wrong defect and omit the one thing they need — why it went away and that `python:compare-swap` already covers it. There are **three** call sites, not two — `config._load_mutation` plus a SEPARATE copy of the block in each of `cli._cmd_run` and `cli._cmd_plan` — and all three are reordered and pinned by parametrized tests over both names asserting the message says "withdrawn" and does NOT say "unknown operator". **Correction, round-1 review (M2), recorded rather than quietly fixed:** this sentence originally said "Both call sites are reordered and pinned by a parametrized test", which was wrong twice over — it undercounted the sites, and only the loader was actually pinned. The reviewer measured it the right way, by reverting BOTH CLI reorderings and re-running the full suite: 3354 passed / 11 skipped either way, not one test moved. The two missing tests now exist (`tests/test_cli_run.py`, one per verb over both names) and were verified the same way in reverse — reverting the reorderings turns all four red, and `cli.py` was restored to byte-identity with HEAD afterwards. The lesson is the one this project keeps relearning and A-105/A-112/A-326 already carry: a decision row asserting its own coverage is worthless unless someone has watched that coverage fail. Two consequential simplifications follow and are taken rather than left as no-ops: A-326's suggestion-list filters (`declarable` / `declarable_for_language`) can no longer remove anything and are deleted, because a filter that reads like a live guard and can never fire is itself the defect class; and `test_the_spelling_survives_in_the_v7_vocabulary_on_purpose` is REPLACED by `test_the_spelling_is_gone_from_the_v8_vocabulary` rather than deleted, so the flip is visible in the diff instead of looking like coverage that evaporated. **Flagged for the reviewer**: this is the one change in the wave not named by the brief's three backlog items — it is isolated to the vocabulary, the schema enum, two refusal orderings and their tests, and reverting it is a self-contained diff if the reviewer judges the scope call wrong. |

## Decided — B018/B019/B035 round-1 adversarial review remediation, 2026-08-30

Filed against `reports/assay-B018-B019-B035-v8-synergy-REVIEW.md` (ACCEPT-conditional,
independent reviewer, no implementation context). Every finding below was
re-measured here before acting on it, and two of the wave's own records turned
out to be wrong rather than merely incomplete.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-332 | **B018's identity is available for a DIRECT wheel install (file or URL), a zipapp, and nothing else — and assay will not synthesise one for an index install. Three changes: (a) defensive hardening, NOT a bug fix (corrected by A-334 — no real installer ever produced the input this guards against) — the URL fragment is now stripped before the `.whl` test as a precaution; `pip install https://.../assay-1.0-py3-none-any.whl#sha256=...` was already identified correctly before this change; (b) the refusal message names the index-install case first and says what to do instead; (c) `docs/CONSUMERS.md` carries a table of which install shapes yield an identity, because `--require-judge-provenance` is a hard refusal and the answer decides whether a consumer can use the flag at all.** | (a) was found while checking the reviewer's M3 and is the more consequential half. `_installed_wheel_digest` tested `url.split("?", 1)[0].endswith(".whl")`; PEP 610 records the requested URL verbatim, and `#sha256=` is how an index page's own links are written and how a gate pins a judge artifact by URL AND digest. **CORRECTED by A-334 — this sentence was false and is the most serious record error in the wave.** It originally read "Measured across the four real URL forms … only the fragment form was refused, and it is precisely the form CIU §10.5's verified-artifact path would use", and it claimed a live defect. It was measured against a hand-written `_FakeDistribution` string, never an installer. **pip does not write the fragment**: it builds the record from `link.url_without_fragment` (`pip/_internal/utils/direct_url_helpers.py:78,89`), confirmed here by real installs — a local `file://` install and a remote install over a loopback HTTP server, which is the exact pin-by-URL-and-digest shape this row invoked — both of which store the URL with the fragment already gone and are identified correctly by the PRE-A-332 parser. `uv` strips it too. So (a) fixes no reachable defect: it is defensive hardening for a shape PEP 610 permits and no current installer emits, kept because it costs one `split` and forecloses a future or third-party installer writing what the spec allows — the claim that this shape was CURRENTLY reaching consumers unidentified is exactly the retracted part; no installer measured here (pip, uv, local or remote) ever produced it. Both halves are pinned by tests, including a negative proving the widened parser still refuses an sdist. (b) the old message listed "an editable install, a directory install, and a source checkout carrying `*.egg-info`" and stopped, which reads as exhaustive while omitting the case that dominates in production: an ordinary `pip install assay` from an index writes no `direct_url.json` at all. An operator hitting this inside a runner image was sent hunting for a source-tree problem that does not exist. **(c) and the refusal to "fix" the index case properly is the actual decision.** There is no recoverable wheel digest after an index install — PEP 610 declines to write one, `RECORD` carries per-file hashes and not the archive's, and nothing else on disk names the artifact. The only way to emit *something* would be to synthesise a digest over the installed file set, and that is refused on B018's own terms: it would not equal the digest the consumer verified on download, so it would look like an identity while being uncomparable — an invented fact wearing the shape of a measured one, which is the exact failure `judge_provenance` exists to prevent, and a strictly worse outcome than an honest absence. The absence stays honest and the LIMIT is documented instead, with the operational fix named (install from the wheel file or its URL, or ship the `.pyz`, or mount the artifact — all three work today). |
| A-333 | **Two of this wave's own records were WRONG, not merely thin, and are corrected in place with the correction marked: A-331 overstated its own test coverage, and the B017 recurrence-3 entry stated a cause that does not reproduce.** A-331 said "Both call sites are reordered and pinned by a parametrized test" — there are three call sites, and only the loader was pinned. B017 recurrence 3 said `ciu.global.worktree.toml.j2` reds the gate's shell pre-flight — it does not; that pre-flight is pathspec-limited to `assay/` and the file sits at the worktree root. | **Both were caught by a reviewer doing the one thing that distinguishes a claim from a measurement: trying to make it fail.** For A-331 they reverted BOTH CLI reorderings and re-ran the full suite — 3354 passed / 11 skipped either way, not one test moved — which is proof the claimed coverage did not exist. The two missing tests now exist, one per verb over both names, and were validated in reverse: reverting the reorderings turns all four red, and `cli.py` was restored to byte-identity afterwards. For B017 they ran the pre-flight's actual query (`git status --porcelain=v1 -- assay`) with the file restored and got an empty result; the pre-flight failure this wave really saw came from an uncommitted edit to `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh`, which IS under `assay/`, and was misattributed. **The governing rule this establishes, since the project keeps paying for it (A-105, A-112, A-326's own round-2 correction, and now twice in one wave): a decision row asserting its own coverage is worth nothing until someone has watched that coverage fail, and a diagnosis reconstructed from a plausible mechanism is a hypothesis until the mechanism's own query has been run.** Both corrections are made IN the offending rows rather than appended as new ones, because a reader who finds A-331 must not read the false sentence and stop; each carries an explicit "CORRECTED after round-1 review" marker naming what it used to say, so nothing is silently rewritten. The same review also showed the diagnostic added for the B017 failure (`28d6e41d`) ran only `git status --porcelain`, the half that hides the `.git/info/exclude` class it was written for — which is why it printed nothing in the very run it was meant to explain. It now runs assay's own `ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore` beside it. |
| A-334 | **Round-2 review remediation, and the wave's own worst pattern named plainly: THREE confidently-stated root causes in this wave were reconstructions that the mechanism's own query would have refused, and two of them were written AFTER A-333 was recorded to forbid exactly that.** The three: (1) `ciu.global.worktree.toml.j2` reds the gate's shell pre-flight (it does not — the pre-flight is pathspec-limited to `assay/`); (2) a `#sha256=` URL fragment made a real wheel install unidentifiable (it does not — pip and uv both strip the fragment before writing `direct_url.json`, measured against real local AND remote installs); (3) a ciu invocation at 01:38 regenerated `ciu.env` and removed both render inputs, making the B017 failure "intermittent" (no ciu ran — that was the round-1 reviewer's own restore, stated in their appendix, which I had read and quoted). All three are corrected at every site they reached: A-332's text, `provenance.py`'s comment, the `CHANGES.md` entry (demoted from **Fixed** to **Changed**, so it no longer tells consumers they were exposed to a defect that never existed), `docs/CONSUMERS.md`'s table, the B017 recurrence entry, and the wave report. The `#fragment` CODE change is KEPT and rescoped as hardening; the transience claim is RETRACTED and B017's strong reading restored (on a ciu-created worktree lacking the committed ignore entries, it reproduces every time). | **The common shape is not carelessness about facts — every one of these was checked against SOMETHING — it is checking against a proxy that shares the hypothesis's own assumption.** (1) reasoned from a pre-flight failure I had really seen, without running the pre-flight's actual query; the failure was my own uncommitted edit to a file under `assay/`. (2) exercised `_installed_wheel_digest` against a `_FakeDistribution` I wrote myself, so the test could only confirm the parser did what I already believed PEP 610 said; one `pip install` and one `cat` refuted it, and that pair costs under a minute. (3) inferred a process from a single mtime while the true explanation sat in a document already in context. **The operative rule, sharper than A-333's: when the claim is about what an EXTERNAL system does — an installer, git, another agent — a test double is not evidence, because it is a recording of the belief under test. Run the external system.** A second rule earned here: a defect claim announced to consumers (a `### Fixed` entry, a "why" column) has a higher bar than an internal note, because it tells people they were harmed; when the harm turns out to be hypothetical the entry has to change category, not just soften. Also fixed under this row: the m2 gate diagnostic — on its SECOND attempt — still could not see the B017 class, because `run_self_hosted_lane` does `cd "$worktree/assay"` and `git ls-files` is cwd-scoped, so the root-level files listed nothing. Now `git -C "$worktree"` for both queries, which matches the repo-top paths `git.dirty_paths` actually reports; verified in a scratch repo reproducing B017's exact shape (two root-level untracked files, one hidden by `.git/info/exclude`), where the new form names both and the old form returns empty. And R2-m1: `uv` 0.12.1 writes `"archive_info": {}` with no digest even for a direct-URL wheel install, so the table's implicit rule "a direct install records a digest" is wrong — the rule is "the INSTALLER recorded a digest". Measured, documented as its own row, and pinned by a test asserting a clean refusal rather than a `KeyError`. |
| A-335 | **Six B018/B019 tests inherited their scenario from however the pytest process happened to be invoked, instead of controlling it. They now CONTROL it: `run()` calls `main()` in-process, so `provenance.identify_judge` is monkeypatched by two fixtures (`judge_is_unidentifiable`, `judge_is_identified`) and a test about the unidentified path GETS the unidentified path in every invocation shape. The one test that is legitimately about the live process is rewritten to assert COHERENCE in whichever shape it finds (exactly one of identity/reason; a complete identity when there is one) rather than asserting the shape itself, and `test_cli_run.py`'s shared stderr helper DERIVES which of the two correct outputs to expect by asking `identify_judge()` instead of hardcoding one.** Two CLI tests for the IDENTIFIED path are added, closing a real coverage gap: nothing had ever driven `assay run` through the CLI with an identity present, so "records it, and announces nothing" was unasserted, and `--require-judge-provenance` was only ever shown to refuse — never shown NOT to refuse when it should not. | **This is the FOURTH instance of A-334's pattern, on an axis none of the three review rounds checked: the real release pipeline's own install shape.** The registered gate does not run `tests/` against a source checkout. `build_offline_closure_venvs` → `install_wheel_into_run_venv` builds a real wheel and installs it into a clean `run-venv` with `--no-index --no-deps`, then runs pytest with THAT interpreter — a genuine PEP 610 install with a real `direct_url.json` and a real digest. So under the gate `identify_judge` correctly returns an identity, the CLI correctly records it and correctly prints nothing, and six tests asserting "no identity, and the notice on stderr" correctly failed. One of them said the quiet part in its own comment — *"the one line present is the judge-provenance notice this source-tree invocation prints on every run"* — which is a statement about the author's habitual invocation, not about the software. **The proxy that shared the hypothesis's assumption this time was my own hand-invocation of `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh`.** Running the gate script directly is not running `cmru release`, and three rounds of "the gate is green" all meant the narrower thing; the failure surfaced the first time the actual release pipeline ran, on top of main. A-334's rule generalises accordingly: *the external system to run is the one that will actually run it in production, invoked the way production invokes it* — for this project that is `cmru release --project assay`, not the gate script it happens to call. **Injection was chosen over shape-detection for the five behavioural tests, and shape-detection only where the subject genuinely IS the live process.** A test that says "an unidentifiable run refuses" should not silently become a test of something else when the interpreter changes; making the scenario explicit is what stops an environment change from turning a passing suite into a differently-scoped one without anyone noticing. The remaining shape-derived helper is honest because its callers assert "assay printed nothing UNEXPECTED", and what is expected genuinely does depend on the process's identity — so it asks, and its failure message names which shape it found. Verified in BOTH shapes rather than argued: 74 passed in the dev checkout AND in a purpose-built wheel-in-clean-run-venv reproducing the gate's own shape; and the pre-fix tests were re-run in that same venv to watch them fail first, reproducing exactly the six names from `logs/cmru/run-tests.log`. |

## Decided — B036, 2026-08-30 (the JavaScript/TypeScript adapter, R1 only)

Filed against `4-backlog.md`'s B036. Every claim below was MEASURED before it
was written, against real `vitest run --coverage` output produced for BOTH of
Vitest's coverage providers from the committed
`tests/fixtures/coverage/probe-js` project (see that directory's own
`PROVENANCE.md` section for versions and commands) — never by analogy to
Python's or Go's adapter, which is the mistake `adapters/go.py`'s own header
records A-172 catching once already.

**The id numbering deliberately skips A-327..A-339.** The concurrent
`feature/assay-b018-b019-b035-v8-synergy` wave was mid-review while this work
ran and had already consumed A-327..A-333 on its own branch (A-334 by the time
the round-1 review re-checked), with room to add
more before it merges. This file is append-only and its ids are cited from
source comments, so a COLLISION would have to be resolved by renumbering live
citations in one of the two branches; a GAP costs nothing and cannot be
resolved wrongly. The gap is the deliberate choice, not an accounting error.

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-340 | **The `judge.language` string is `"javascript"`, one adapter covering `.js`, `.jsx`, `.ts` and `.tsx` — not `"typescript"`, not a per-dialect split, and not a per-extension one.** `"typescript"` is explicitly NOT registered and is refused like any unknown language (`test_cli_run_javascript.py`). This is effectively permanent: `judge.language` is consumer-facing and lands in every verdict artifact. | `judge.language` names a LANGUAGE, the way `"python"` and `"go"` already do — neither splits by dialect, runtime or file extension. TypeScript is JavaScript's own declared superset and JSX/TSX are syntax extensions of the two; all four compile to one runtime language, and — the load-bearing measured fact — the coverage artifact makes no distinction between them: one `coverage-final.json` from one `vitest run` carries `.ts` and `.tsx` records with identical structure and no language field anywhere. Splitting would force a lane touching one `.ts` file and one `.tsx` file to declare two languages for a single measurement, and would make `source_globs` (an adapter attribute) redundant with the language name. The rejected alternative `"typescript"` fails the same test from the other side: it would leave plain `.js`/`.jsx` — which every React project still has — unnameable. |
| A-341 | **The absolute-path reconciliation belongs in NEITHER the new parser NOR `normalize_coverage_key`: the core already does it, and `JavaScriptAdapter.normalize_coverage_key` returns its key unchanged.** Istanbul keys every record by absolute filesystem path. `evaluate._to_repo_relative_key`'s ABSOLUTE branch (B006/A-145, added for real `coverage.py`'s own absolute-path fallback) resolves such a key against `repo_top` and returns its repo-relative identity, leaving a key that names nothing inside the repository untouched and therefore inert. | Checked, not assumed, in three places. (1) `CoverageProfile`'s own model contract says a parser keys files "exactly as that format's artifact names it (no source-root resolution, no path normalization against a project layout — that stays the caller's job)", and all four existing parsers obey it: `go_cover` leaves the package-qualified import path alone and `GoAdapter.module_path` strips it; `coverage_py_json` leaves its key alone and `PythonAdapter.coverage_key_prefix` strips it. So the parser is the wrong place by the model's own rule. (2) `normalize_coverage_key` is documented as the LANGUAGE-specific prefix STRIP, with the universal prefix-BOUNDARY reconciliation living in the core (DESIGN-GUIDE §11) — and an absolute path is not a language fact, it is a "where did this run" fact, identical for every language. (3) It already works: the end-to-end CLI test drives a real lane whose command writes `$PWD/src/app.ts` as its key and gets a correct PASS, because assay runs the lane's command with `cwd` inside the relocated snapshot, so a real coverage tool's absolute paths are absolute paths of the very tree `repo_top` names. Adding a strip in either place would have been dead code that looked load-bearing. |
| A-342 | **`requires_span_attribution = False`, and the gap it would otherwise paper over is closed in the PARSER instead: `coverage_istanbul_json` expands each `statementMap` entry across its own `[start.line, end.line]` extent, innermost extent winning, ties resolving by MAX count.** | Measured on both providers before deciding. `@vitest/coverage-v8` emits one single-line statement per executable physical line — no multi-line extent exists anywhere in its artifact, so `False` is trivially safe there. `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` emits REAL statement extents, several multi-line (`format.ts`: `[13,15]`, `[24,32]`, `[33,37]`, `[34,36]`), whose interior lines have no entry of their own — i.e. coverage.py's multi-line-statement gap, in a different format. So `False` is NOT trivially true in general, and the initial premise would have been wrong exactly the way A-172 found Go's was. Three options were compared. (a) `True` plus a real `statement_spans` needs a TypeScript/JSX parser written in Python from scratch — the categorically larger undertaking B037's own scope boundary exists to rule on, for a recovery the artifact can already supply. (b) `False` with no parser-side expansion silently drops every continuation line into `evaluate.py`'s rule 4, which is srdm's silent-excuse direction on lines a human actually edited. (c) — chosen — expand extents in the parser: the extent is the ARTIFACT's own claim about which source range one statement occupies, so this recovers exactly what Python's AST walk recovers, from data rather than from a re-parse, and leaves no line of a measured file unattributed. The known cost is stated rather than hidden: a line that is only structurally part of a statement (a lone closing brace) inherits that statement's status and counts toward the denominator — the visible-false-failure direction, chosen over the silent one. **Round-1 review correction (M2), measured rather than recalled: the phrase "and leaves no line of a measured file unattributed" above is FALSE and is withdrawn.** In the committed `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` artifact 23 non-comment lines across six files sit in neither `executed` nor `missing` — every function declaration line, every function-level closing brace, and `format.ts:12`, a genuinely executable `const date =` whose own recorded statement starts at 13 on its initialiser. Those lines take `evaluate.py`'s rule 4 and leave the denominator, so a diff touching only `roles.ts:17` reports `executable=0 → PASS` under istanbul against `executable=1` under v8. The parser's own docstring and `test_coverage_istanbul_real_fixtures.py` already said this correctly; only this row and the report overstated it. The TRUE guarantee — all that `requires_span_attribution = False` actually needs — is: **every line any statement extent covers is classified, so no line is left unattributed that the artifact carried the information to attribute.** Span attribution exists to recover a multi-line construct's interior lines; the expansion already recovers precisely those, which is why a `statement_spans` implementation would have nothing left to do. Measured size of what the expansion buys on the real istanbul artifact: 29 statement START lines become 54 classified lines. The test offered as the pin for the withdrawn claim (`test_no_line_of_a_measured_file_is_ever_unclassified`) was additionally VACUOUS — `evaluate.py:426` populates `unclassified_lines` only inside `if unattributed and adapter.requires_span_attribution:`, so it held for every artifact including a deliberately broken one. It is replaced by a literal ground-truth pin on the unattributed sets (which a start-line-only regression breaks — verified by mutating `_paint`) plus a denominator-consistency check whose own docstring states what it does NOT pin. **Innermost-wins is load-bearing, not a refinement**: in the real istanbul-provider artifact `branchy.ts`'s `if` extent `[2,4]` has count 1 while its own never-taken `return` at `[3,3]` has count 0, so go-cover's "executed wins" merge would report a provably-unexecuted line as covered — a false green. The MAX-count tie-break is `istanbul-lib-coverage`'s own `getLineCoverage` rule, adopted rather than invented. |
| A-343 | **`FileCoverage.excluded` is always `None` for this format, and `has_executable_code` claims only two cases: a `.d.ts`/`.d.mts`/`.d.cts` declaration file, and text that is empty once comments and whitespace are removed. Everything else is `True`, fail-closed — including a type-only `.ts` module.** | The exclusion half was measured: a real `/* istanbul ignore next */` hint (committed as `probe-js/src/hinted.ts`) leaves the hinted statement in `statementMap` with a live count and produces no `skip` marker anywhere in either provider's document. So by the time the parser sees the artifact, an "ignored" line is indistinguishable from a line that was never code — lcov's exact situation and lcov's exact answer. `frozenset()` would claim "zero exclusions, verified" when nothing verified it (A-008). The `has_executable_code` half is where the fail-closed rule earns its keep: `.d.ts` is decided from the PATH because TypeScript's own grammar decides it (a declaration file may hold only ambient declarations), and both real artifacts confirm no provider reports one. A type-only `.ts` module is deliberately NOT claimed as code-free, even though it genuinely has no executable content, because deciding it requires real TypeScript type-erasure semantics — a hand-written TS parser, the same overreach rejected in A-342 — and under the provider this build is wired for it never arises: `@vitest/coverage-v8` REPORTS such a module (measured: `typesonly.ts`, `"statementMap": {}`), so it reaches evaluation as a real record with zero executable lines and this method is never consulted. Under `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` the module is absent and would be reported as missing coverage — the visible direction, and filed as **B038** rather than papered over with a guess. |
| A-344 | **`FileCoverage.branches` is always `None` for this format, and that is a MEASURED refusal rather than an omission — even though the format HAS a `branchMap`.** | The two real producers of one format disagree about what `branchMap` MEANS, measured on one source file (`probe-js/src/branchy.ts`, two `if`s and a ternary). `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` emits three entries typed `if`/`if`/`cond-expr`, one location per ARM, one count per arm: 6 arcs, 2 covered. `@vitest/coverage-v8` emits four entries all typed `"branch"`, each with exactly ONE location and ONE count, describing v8's own executed/unexecuted RANGES — one spans the whole function, another starts at a closing brace: 4 "arcs", 1 covered. Same file, same tests, 2/6 versus 1/4. A lane declares the FORMAT, never the producer, so any single translation would put a number on the wire whose meaning depends on an undeclared fact — the `declared_unverified`-class lie A-O12 was corrected for, and worse than an absent number because a branch percentage looks authoritative. `None` is this project's own existing spelling for "this artifact carries no branch detail this parser can honestly read": `derive_branch_capability`'s own docstring already names lcov and Cobertura returning it for an artifact produced without branch tracking, so this is a use of the established meaning rather than a new one. Consequence, documented for consumers: `require_branch = true` refuses on a JavaScript lane. **B038** tracks real arc support once a producer can be declared. |
| A-345 | **Both canary mechanisms are plain trailing APPENDS, and both snippets are written in the subset valid in `.js`, `.jsx`, `.ts` and `.tsx` alike.** `inject_import_break` appends `throw new Error("assay-canary-import-break")`; `inject_uncovered_line` appends an EXPORTED, never-called `function _assayCanaryUnreached(value = 0)`. | JS/TS has an executable module top level like Python, so nyxloom's insert-after-the-prologue shape would port — but an ES module's `import` declarations are HOISTED and its imports evaluated first, so a trailing `throw` still fires during module evaluation, before any test can touch an export. Appending is therefore equally faithful to the contract ("reliably tripped by merely importing/loading the module") while needing none of Python's docstring/`__future__` insertion-point logic, which for JS/TS would mean parsing multi-line `import { … } from '…'` prologues — parser work with nothing depending on it. `adapters/go.py`'s two appends are the precedent. Both mechanisms were verified against a REAL `vitest run`, not asserted structurally the way Go's had to be: injecting the throw into `roles.ts` and importing it made the suite FAIL with `Error: assay-canary-import-break` at the appended line; injecting the canary function left the suite passing and produced exactly the intended coverage shape — the `export function` line counted 1 (reached by module load) with its two body lines and closing brace counted 0. Two constraints shaped the snippets and both are real defects avoided rather than style: these methods receive only `text` and never a path, so the SAME snippet is appended to a `.js` file, where a type annotation is a syntax error — hence `value = 0`, a default that is valid JavaScript AND lets TypeScript infer `number` so `noImplicitAny` is satisfied; and the canary function is `export`ed because `noUnusedLocals` flags an unreferenced module-level declaration, which a never-called canary is by construction, breaking the protocol's own "lint-clean" requirement. |
| A-346 | **`@vitest/coverage-v8` is NOT safe for a judged lane, and assay's documentation now says so in as many words: `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` is the only Vitest coverage provider a `judge.language = "javascript"` lane may gate on.** The parser is unchanged and still reads any `coverage-istanbul-json` document faithfully — nyc/istanbul and Jest are unaffected — but README, CONSUMERS and the worked lane no longer present the two Vitest providers as interchangeable, and the v8 one carries an explicit warning with the reproduction. Four real artifacts (both providers × Vitest 3.2.4 and 4.1.11) are committed as `probe-js-provider-defect`, and `test_coverage_istanbul_provider_accuracy.py` re-derives the defect from them on every run against those COMMITTED artifacts — including a test that cannot go red from an upstream fix on its own, only from a fixture manually regenerated against a fixed provider (B040's recheck item), which is the signal to revisit this ruling rather than a test to relax. | Found by the round-1 adversarial review and reproduced independently here before ruling. **The measurement:** in a project where ground truth needs no tool to establish — five functions, each guarded by `if (v === 0) return 0` on its second line, one test calling each with `0`, so every line below a guard provably never runs — `@vitest/coverage-v8` reports lines below the guard as EXECUTED whenever a CONDITIONAL (ternary) expression appears earlier in the same block. Driven through the shipped `evaluate_coverage` with those lines as the diff and `fail_under = 100.0`: **PASS at 100.0% under v8, FAIL at 0.0% under istanbul** — same source, same tests, same assay code. Four facts make this a ruling rather than a caveat: it reproduces on **both** currently-released Vitest majors (3.2.4 and 4.1.11), so there is no version to pin past; a **one-line** ternary triggers it too, so it is not a formatting hazard a lint rule could avoid; `coverage.experimentalAstAwareRemapping = true` does not fix it; and a multi-line binary expression, call and object literal are all reported correctly by the same provider in the same artifact, so the unsound records are not structurally distinguishable from the sound ones. **Why no code guard was built.** Nothing in the document separates a true `s` count of 1 from a false one — there is no internal inconsistency to detect, and `fnMap`/`f` corroborate the lie (the enclosing function really did run). The only mechanical alternative is to infer the PRODUCER from the artifact's shape, which is precisely the declaration-versus-sniffing collapse A-007 forbids, and which would in any case have silently stopped working between the two versions measured here: Vitest 4's v8 provider emits multi-line statement extents where Vitest 3's emitted single-line ones, so any shape-based discriminator written today would be wrong tomorrow while still returning an answer. So the remedy is documentary, and the reason is recorded here rather than left as a doc edit. **Why not drop the format, or refuse the provider.** The defect is the producer's, not the format's: the same `coverage-final.json` from nyc, from Jest, or from `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` carries no such error, and dropping `coverage-istanbul-json` would remove the only coverage format the JS/TS ecosystem actually standardises on. Refusing "v8-produced" documents requires identifying them, i.e. the sniffing above. **B040** carries the upstream report and the standing question of whether a lane should be able to declare its producer — the same mechanism **B038** needs for branch arcs, now with a second and much sharper reason to want it. **The process lesson, recorded because it is the more transferable finding:** this change measured both producers exhaustively for SHAPE — extent geometry, `branchMap` typing, key spelling, capability fields — and never once for ACCURACY, which is the single property an R1 judge rests on. The committed v8 fixture already contained an instance (`format.ts:17-18`) and the end-to-end test iterated over that exact file without asserting anything that could notice. A shape match is not a correctness check; A-334's "check against a real thing" has to mean the thing the verdict actually claims. |

## Decided — Wave A, 2026-08-30 (`WAVE-PROMPT-2026-08-30-js-consumer-producer.md`, assay-3.2.0)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-347 | **A coverage tool that deletes-and-recreates its own `reportsDirectory` (Vitest's own default `coverage.clean = true`) silently orphans `safeio.reserve_output`'s held parent-directory descriptor, and `assay run` reports a fully-covered real lane as `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE` — filed as B049, NOT fixed in this release. Wave A's mitigation: every committed JS `vitest.config.ts` (README, CONSUMERS) now declares `coverage.clean = false`, with the mechanism and the measured A/B stated in prose rather than left implicit.** | Found running B041(c)'s real-`vitest` qualification harness — the first time a real external coverage tool has run inside an assay snapshot; every prior R1/R2 JS test used a heredoc (A-334's own lesson, one layer further: a real CLI run through a test double still does not prove a claim the double never exercised). Measured, not reasoned about: a two-commit git fixture, `npm ci --offline` against a warm cache, `npx --no-install vitest run --coverage`, `fail_under = 100.0`, a fully-covered diff — `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE` with Vitest's default `clean: true`, `PASS`/`pct: 100.0` the instant `clean: false` is added and nothing else changes; a `cp .assay/coverage-final.json /tmp/…` appended to the SAME lane command, run immediately after `vitest` and before assay's own read, proves the artifact was genuinely complete and correctly keyed at the declared path throughout — the reservation failed to find something that was really there. Mechanism, cited by file:line in B049: `runner.py:1692` opens/creates the coverage artifact's parent directory ONCE, before the command runs, and holds that descriptor for the whole execution; `runner.py:1771`'s post-command read (`OutputReservation.consume`, `safeio.py:252`) looks up the declared basename relative to that SAME held descriptor (`safeio.py:319`) rather than re-opening the path fresh. A tool that recreates the directory (not merely truncates the file) leaves the held descriptor pointing at an orphaned, empty inode, so the lookup returns `FileNotFoundError` — read downstream as "the command never wrote anything," which is not what happened. **Not fixed here** because `safeio.py`/`runner.py` are core, language-free machinery shared by every adapter and every future one (Python R1/R2/R3, SQL R2, JS R1), the wave prompt scopes Wave A to the JS adapter's own consumer-facing gaps, and the real fix is a genuine design call among at least three shapes (re-open by name at consume time and lose part of `arm()`'s TOCTOU protection; detect a recreated directory and name a distinguishable reason instead of folding into `EMPTY_COVERAGE`; leave it fully consumer-owned via documentation) that this implementer is not positioned to make unilaterally mid-wave — B049 lists the options and asks for the ruling. Filed as a NEW defect rather than folded into an existing entry: nothing in `4-backlog.md` describes this failure mode (the nearest neighbour, the old B006 §(b), was the DIFFERENT and already-fixed "missing parent directory" gap, superseded by `create_missing_parents=True`, which this finding sits one level past). |
| A-348 | **B039's classified-line ceiling moves to ONE shared place, `coverage_parsers/model.py` (`MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES` + a new `ClassifiedLineBudget` class), and `go_cover.parse` now spends it before expanding a block's range — closing the exact unbounded-expansion gap `coverage_istanbul_json` was given a bound for and `go_cover` was not. Each parser module RE-EXPORTS the name into its own namespace (`from .model import MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES as MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES`) and passes it explicitly at each `parse()` call — never as a default baked into `ClassifiedLineBudget.__init__` — so each module's own pre-existing `monkeypatch.setattr(<module>, "MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES", ...)` test idiom keeps working untouched.** | The two live options were (a) a shared VALUE with each parser keeping its own budget-spending class, or (b) a shared value AND a shared spending class. (b), chosen: the two classes (istanbul's old `_Budget`, a hypothetical `go_cover` twin) would have been byte-identical except for the error message's format name — the exact copy-paste-with-a-different-string shape this project's own four-copies origin story (DESIGN-GUIDE §2) exists to refuse repeating one leaf lower. `model.py` already owns the shared `FileCoverage`/`CoverageProfile` types every parser returns, and already declared itself the correctness-invariant home (P15/A-067 finding 4); a shared CAPACITY invariant belongs in the identical place for the identical reason, and importing `assay.errors` there introduces no cycle (`errors.py` is itself a stdlib-only leaf, checked before importing, not assumed). **Why `remaining` is a required keyword with no default, rather than defaulting to `MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES` in `ClassifiedLineBudget.__init__` for callers to omit:** a default value is bound ONCE at class-definition time; if `ClassifiedLineBudget(format_name=...)` defaulted `remaining=MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES`, a test's `monkeypatch.setattr(model, "MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES", 12)` would silently stop mattering the moment the class was already defined (i.e. always, since it is defined at import time) — the identical shadowing-default trap AGENTS.md's own anti-pattern table names one layer down. Each call site instead reads its OWN module's re-exported name FRESH, inside `parse()`, exactly as the pre-existing istanbul code already did — preserving BOTH modules' existing test-patching idiom was a real constraint on the design, not incidental: rewriting `test_coverage_parsers_coverage_istanbul_json.py`'s `tiny_bound` fixture to target a different module would have been a needless, unrelated test-suite change riding along on a bug fix. The refusal MESSAGE changed from istanbul's own literal "classified statement lines" to the shared, format-agnostic "classified lines" (with the tripping format named in a `{format_name}: ` prefix, matching each parser's own `_malformed()` convention) — a real, deliberate wording change, not an accident: "statement lines" is meaningless for `go_cover`, whose own unit is a BLOCK's line range, never a "statement". Three pre-existing istanbul tests asserting the literal old substring were updated to match (`test_coverage_parsers_coverage_istanbul_json.py`), and `test_the_shipped_bound_is_the_one_shared_documented_value` (`test_coverage_parsers_go_cover.py`) pins `go_cover.MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES is model.MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES` — object IDENTITY, not merely equal literals, which is what "the ONE shared value" actually claims and what a future accidental re-literal (`MAX_CLASSIFIED_LINES = 2_000_000` typed again by hand in either module) would be caught by. |
| A-349 | **`assay lanes --json`'s inventory (B044) field set, and its stability rule: `inventory_schema` bumps ONLY when an EXISTING key's meaning changes; adding a key is additive and does not move it.** Fields: `name`, `scope`, `rigor` (declared), `enforcement`, `language`, `rigor_reachable` (registry-derived), `coverage` (`format`/`artifact`/`producer`), `mutation` (the `MutationConfig.as_declared()` projection or `null`), `canary` (`mechanism`/`target` or `null`), `base_source` (resolved — see below), `external_tools`, `argv0`, `env_required`, `environment_command` (boolean), `infrastructure_facts` (sorted key names, never resolved values), `budget`, `cwd`/`link_paths`/`coverage.producer` (`null`/`[]`, Wave-B keys not yet declarable), `snapshot_selection`. | Every field traces to exactly ONE producer — the loaded `Lane`/`JudgeConfig`, or `_built_in_registry()` — and nothing is re-derived from the raw TOML text a second, independent way (the spec's own requirement). Two fields are DERIVED rather than passed through raw, and both derivations are deliberate: (1) `rigor_reachable`/`external_tools` come from `built_in.entries.get(language)` — `[]` for a `language` this build's registry does not know at all, rather than raising, because `assay lanes` has never executed or refused a lane (A-054's own "must not execute", extended here to "must not refuse for a capability gap either") — a gate compares `rigor` against `rigor_reachable` ITSELF instead of discovering a mismatch only when a real `assay run` refuses; asking the judge's own registry this way is exactly what B044's motivating problem (CIU restating capability facts a second time, e.g. `[testing.lanes.<l>] request_base = true`) asks to stop needing. (2) `base_source` resolves `JudgeConfig.base_source`'s OWN documented default (A-328: absent means `"declared"`) instead of passing `None` through, and is `null` only where NO tier reads a comparison commit at all (no `judge` table, `mode == "whole_target"`, or neither R1 nor R2 declared) — mirroring `_load_judge`'s own three refusal shapes for the field (`config.py:1327-1350`) exactly, so the inventory's `null` and the loader's own refusal boundary can never disagree. This is the one place this function derives rather than reads, and it derives only an ALREADY-established meaning, never invents one — the alternative (pass `None` through raw) would force every consumer to reimplement A-328's own default rule to answer "does this lane need `--request-base`", which is the identical four-copies divergence this project exists to close one layer up. Sort order for `infrastructure_facts` (sorted, not declaration order) and JSON key order (`sort_keys=True`, matching `assay plan --json`'s own existing convention in `cli.py`) are both deliberate house-style consistency choices, not requirements from the backlog text. |
| A-350 | **The real-`vitest` qualification harness (B041(c), `tests/qualification/test_javascript_real_vitest.py`) is skipped in the REGISTERED gate unconditionally and unskipped only by `ASSAY_NODE_QUALIFICATION=1` with `node`/`npm` on `PATH` — never wired into `tools/tester-unified-gate.sh`.** | `tester-unified` genuinely has no Node toolchain (DESIGN-GUIDE §10: "assay's suite needs no Node toolchain for the same reason it needs no Go one" — mandatory, not a choice, since adding one would be exactly the cockpit-green pathway §9 exists to close). So this harness can never be part of `pytest tests -q` as run by the registered gate regardless of the env var's default, and gating it on an env var the registered gate never sets is the honest spelling of "real, but not everywhere" — the identical shape P25's own real-Python qualification (`tests/test_python_qualification.py`) uses for its OWN environment-gated half (`_real_gate_environment()`), except P25's gate DOES have the dependency it needs and wires its harness in via `gate/python/qualify_topos.py`; this one's gate does not and cannot, so there is no analogous wiring to add. Run here (this devcontainer has Node) as the A-335 proof the 3.1.0 wave lacked: `ASSAY_NODE_QUALIFICATION=1 python3 -m pytest tests/qualification/ -v -s`, transcript in the wave REPORT. |

## Decided — Wave B, 2026-08-31 (`WAVE-PROMPT-2026-08-30-js-consumer-producer.md` §"Wave B", assay-4.0.0, verdict schema v9)

| id | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| A-351 | **The coverage-producer vocabulary is closed PER FORMAT, never globally.** `COVERAGE_PRODUCERS_BY_FORMAT` maps a format key to its own tuple of declarable producer names; a name legal for one format is REFUSED for another, with a message naming the format it does belong to. | `coverage.py` is a perfectly real producer NAME — of a different format. A single global set would make the key answer "is this string a producer somewhere in assay's world?" while its own documented meaning, and the message a consumer reads, claim "this is the producer of THIS artifact". That is AGENTS.md's name-for-object anti-pattern exactly: a check that appears to validate the object actually validates only the name. Per-format closure makes the two questions the same question. `test_a_producer_from_ANOTHER_formats_vocabulary_is_refused` pins it. The cost is one nested table instead of one flat set, which is the honest shape of a fact that genuinely varies by format. |
| A-352 | **`producer` is REQUIRED for `coverage-istanbul-json` and optional for every other format.** A `coverage-istanbul-json` lane that omits it is refused at load. | DESIGN-GUIDE §5's own test, applied literally: a key may be optional only when a wrong implied value would fail LOUDLY. For `coverage-istanbul-json` an implied `istanbul` over a genuinely v8-produced artifact fails silently and in the worst direction — the run reports PASS over lines that provably never executed (A-346's measured defect), and nothing anywhere in the document is inconsistent enough to notice. For `coverage-py-json` there is exactly one producer, so an omission cannot pick the wrong one; for `lcov`/`cobertura` there is no open vocabulary at all, so there is nothing an omission could be wrong about. The asymmetry is therefore derived from the measured consequence per format, not from a general preference for required keys. |
| A-353 | **Refusal-by-NAME is checked BEFORE catalogue membership, and the three refusal grounds are kept deliberately distinct rather than blurred into one "unsupported producer" message.** `vitest-v8` and `c8` are refused on a MEASURED defect (each naming its own committed witness); `jest-v8` is refused as UNMEASURED. | Ordering: exactly `WITHDRAWN_MUTATION_OPERATORS`' precedent one field over. "That is not a known producer" would be a false statement — assay knows all three perfectly well and has fixtures for two of them — and a consumer given it would reasonably conclude they had typo'd rather than that their coverage is unsound, and would go looking for the correct spelling of a thing that must not be used at all. Distinctness: a reader who is told `jest-v8` is refused for the same reason as `vitest-v8` cannot tell that assay has a committed reproduction for one and not the other, which is the difference between "we measured this and it lies" and "nobody has checked" — and it is the second that a consumer might reasonably want to fund the measurement for. Each refusal carries its own reason string and its own fix, and each fix is a producer assay actually accepts (the message never offers a refused name as the remedy — pinned by a test). |
| A-354 | **`go-cover`'s `go-test`/`covdata` producer names are NOT shipped in this release**, even though B045's contract text lists them; a `go-cover` lane declaring any producer is refused with "this format has no open producer vocabulary". | B045's contract lists them and B047 item 3 assigns them to the Go wave; the two readings conflict, and this resolves toward B047. Shipping a closed vocabulary that nothing in this build can produce, check, or explain is the speculative naming DESIGN-GUIDE §5 forbids — and the failure mode is specific, not stylistic: a `go-cover` lane declaring `producer = "covdata"` would be ACCEPTED and then IGNORED by a parser with no producer-dependent behaviour, which is a key that looks honoured and is not. A refusal is recoverable in one line the day the Go wave lands; an accepted-and-ignored key is a silent lie for as long as it exists. **This is the B045 call most open to challenge** and is flagged as such for the reviewer rather than presented as settled: the alternative reading is that B045's contract authorises shipping the names now. |
| A-355 | **The coverage-parser module protocol WIDENS uniformly to `parse(text: str, *, producer: str | None)` — all five modules, keyword-only, NO default — rather than dispatching on the producer inside `assay.coverage`'s registry.** `load_coverage_profile`/`parse_coverage_artifact`/`read_coverage_artifact` gain `producer` with a `None` default and pass it straight through; `runner.py` and `canary.py` thread the lane's declared value to all three coverage-read sites. | Measured before choosing: only SIX direct `parse()` call sites exist in the whole repository (three in tests for `go_cover`, three for `coverage_py_json`), because everything else goes through the registry — so the "touches all five parsers" cost the seam map warned about is six one-word edits, not a refactor. That killed the main argument for the alternative. The alternative itself is worse on the merits: dispatching in `coverage.py` would put the fact "istanbul has arc-bearing producers" in the registry module, i.e. a FORMAT fact living outside the format's own module, which is precisely what the per-format module layout exists to prevent. **Keyword-only with no default** is the load-bearing half: a default of `None` would make "a new call site forgot the producer" indistinguishable from "this lane declared none", and the symptom would be a silently arc-less profile that still PASSES — the invisible direction. With no default it is a `TypeError` at the call site. Four of the five modules `del producer` with a comment saying why their format has nothing to branch on, which is a claim a reviewer can check rather than an omission they must interpret. Validation of the name against the format's vocabulary stays in `config.py` alone (A-068), so the rule has one home. |
| A-356 | **Istanbul arcs are keyed by each ARM's own `locations[i].start.line`, falling back to the ENTRY's line (`entry.line \|\| entry.loc.start.line`) for an arm that carries no line — a deliberate departure from `istanbul-lib-coverage`'s own `getBranchCoverageByLine`, which attributes an entire entry to ONE line.** | B045's contract says "keyed by each arm's `start.line`" and cites istanbul-lib-coverage's reduction as the authority for that; the two disagree, and the artifact settles it. **Measured:** real `@vitest/coverage-istanbul` output writes an implicit `else` arm as `{"start": {}, "end": {}}` — a location object with no line at all — and there are SEVEN such arms across the committed artifacts (`branchy.ts` 0/1, `format.ts` 0/5/6, `hinted.ts` 0, `roles.ts` 0). Strict per-arm keying is therefore not implementable as written. Of the two ways to finish it: dropping the lineless arms shrinks the denominator and reports a GREENER branch percentage than the artifact supports, and an untaken implicit `else` is precisely the arc a consumer most needs to see — the silent-excuse direction. Falling back to the entry's line records every arm the artifact declares while keeping per-arm detail wherever the artifact provides it (A-265: detail over metadata — a `switch`'s cases land on their own lines, where upstream's reduction would collapse them onto the `switch` keyword). The denominator claim is a TEST, not a comment: `test_the_derived_arc_totals_equal_the_artifacts_own_arm_count` re-derives `sum(len(b[id]))` straight from the fixture bytes and asserts equality with the parsed totals, on both real artifacts. Verified: all three `FileCoverage` cross-bucket invariants hold on both real artifacts under this rule. |
| A-357 | **A `branchMap` entry typed outside `{if, cond-expr, binary-expr, switch, default-arg}` REFUSES the whole artifact (`ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`) rather than being skipped.** The refusal message names the v8-range shape explicitly and points at `judge.coverage.producer`. | This is the second, INDEPENDENT layer of the producer check, and the only one that can catch a lane whose declaration and whose command have drifted apart — the by-name refusal (A-353) reads the config and cannot see what the command actually ran. Real evidence, not a hypothetical: the committed `@vitest/coverage-v8` and `c8` artifacts, declared as `istanbul`, are refused on their first entry, because both emit every entry typed `"branch"` with exactly one location describing v8's executed RANGES (A-344). Skipping unknown types instead would compute a branch percentage over an artifact assay could not read, and would compute a SMALLER, greener one — the same silent-excuse direction A-344 refused the whole feature for. Two properties keep the refusal honest: it is scoped to arcs, so with no arc-bearing producer declared these same documents parse exactly as they did under schema v8 (`test_a_v8_range_document_still_parses_with_no_producer_declared`), and the five accepted types are transcribed from babel-plugin-istanbul's own instrumenter rather than invented, with `switch`/`default-arg` — which neither probe source contains — covered by synthetic documents rather than left as an untested claim. |
| A-358 | **The type-only-module recogniser (B038(b)) is a lexer scoped to `.ts`/`.tsx`, splitting top-level statements at depth-zero `;`/newline and requiring EVERY one to begin with one of six literal prefixes, each carrying a TRAILING SPACE; anything it cannot lex to the end answers "has code".** | The direction is A-104/srdm's asymmetry: a wrong `False` silently drops a file from both numerator and denominator, a wrong `True` is a visible unmeasured-file failure. So the lexer's job is to be RIGHT about a narrow shape and loudly uncertain everywhere else. Two fail-OPEN defects in the first draft were found by PROBING it rather than by reading it, and both are now regression-pinned: (1) without the trailing space, `export type` is a prefix of `export typeGuard = 1`, and a runtime assignment parsed as a type declaration; (2) the splitter originally returned a best-effort split when it met a construct it could not follow, and `export type T = \`a${'b'}c\`` followed by a real `console.log(1)` swallowed the runtime statement into the type declaration's own segment. The fix for (2) is all-or-nothing: an unterminated literal, a `${` substitution, an unmatched closer, or a file ending with a bracket still open returns `None`, and the caller answers "has code" — if the scan loses the structure anywhere it has no basis for a claim about ANY statement. The known limitation is stated rather than hidden and is itself a test: a declaration split over several top-level lines (`export type Mode =` / `\| 'read'`) answers "has code", because recovering it needs to know where a type EXPRESSION ends, which is the TypeScript grammar B038(b) explicitly declines to implement. Scope is `.ts`/`.tsx` only: `type`/`interface` are not JavaScript, so a `.js` file containing them is not an erased module but a file that will not run, and calling it code-free would hide exactly that. Witness and control are the committed real fixtures `probe-js/src/typesonly.ts` (absent from the real istanbul artifact) and `probe-js/src/orphan.ts` (one runtime export), not two hand-written strings. |
| A-359 | **The v8 -> v9 cut is ONE bump carrying all four of B045, B046, B043 and B041(b), and it is cut BEFORE the features that populate its fields rather than after them.** Every new field is registered in three places in that single commit -- packaged schema, dataclass, `verify.py` -- and the native defaults (`JudgmentR1.coverage_producer` at `runner.py`, `producer = "native"` in `_build_judgment_r2`) are wired in the same commit. | Each of the four items puts a new key inside an object the packaged schema closes with `additionalProperties: false`, so each ALONE would already have been a bump; four bumps would be four releases of consumer churn for one wave of facts. Ordering it first is forced, not preferred: B046's whole substance is new `judgment.r2` fields, so it is unlandable and untestable until the schema admits them, and every intermediate commit would be red -- while the schema equally cannot be cut piecemeal, because `carve-assets/W5/verdict.schema.v9.json` must be byte-identical to the shipped file and the drift guard asserts exactly that, so the freeze must happen at the LAST schema-touching commit. Cutting once, first, with the final field set satisfies both.
