Wiring the native defaults in the same commit is A-323's lesson restated: a field that is declared but never populated is a field no test exercises, and `shard_index`/`candidate_ids` each shipped broken that way for a release. |
| A-360 | **`judgment.r2.producer` is REQUIRED and always emitted, and it forks the object: `native` requires `jobs`/`max_mutants`/`operators` and FORBIDS `producer_tool`/`survived_uncovered`/`discarded`/`lines_without_candidates`; `ingested` is the exact mirror, and additionally forbids `equivalence_artifact`.** Expressed as an `allOf` `if/then/else` pairing on the existing `judgment_r2` matrix's model, mirrored in `JudgmentR2._check_producer_fork`, and rejected in favour of the alternative "keep the three required and fill them from the report". | `judgment.r2` is documented as the effective R2 POLICY -- *what decided the claim*. For an ingested lane assay decided none of it: the foreign tool's own configuration chose the mutators, the concurrency and the ceiling. Filling those fields from the report would put that tool's configuration on the wire under assay's name, which is the same declared-vs-verified conflation A-230a keeps `helpers[]` clean of and which the north-star's "never conflate tiers" forbids. An honestly EMPTY policy field is absent, not backfilled -- A-230a's own precedent one object over. The forbidding runs both ways for the symmetric reason: a native document carrying `discarded` or `survived_uncovered` would claim a computation over an ingested report that never happened. The observed operators are not lost by this -- they remain fully on the wire, one per mutant, in `mutation.*[].operator`. |
| A-361 | **`producer_tool` is its own object copied verbatim from the report (`name`, `version`, `report_schema_version`), NOT a `helpers[]` entry, and `report_schema_version` stays a STRING.** | `helpers[]` records tools assay ITSELF invoked and resolved on PATH (A-230a); assay did not invoke Stryker -- the lane's own argv did, inside the snapshot. Putting an ingested tool in `helpers[]` would launder a declared fact into a verified one, which is the single conflation the whole tier vocabulary exists to prevent. The field's own description says "declared by artifact, not verified" so a consumer cannot mistake it. `report_schema_version` is a string because the upstream `schemaVersion` is one (`"1"`, `"1.0"`, `"2"` are all real spellings); parsing it to a number here would be assay inventing precision the artifact does not have. |
| A-362 | **The ingested operator namespace is admitted by an OPEN `pattern` branch in `mutation_operator`'s `oneOf`, whose pattern string is byte-identical to `assay.vocabulary.INGESTED_OPERATOR_RE.pattern` -- including its single-alternative `(?:stryker)` group, which is deliberately not simplified away.** `MutantOutcome` consults `is_ingested_operator` BEFORE the closed catalogue; whether an ingested name is legal for a given document is a cross-object question answered one layer up by `Verdict._check_operator_language_agrees`. | A foreign tool's mutator names are DATA assay records, not a catalogue assay closes -- Stryker adds mutators on its own release schedule and assay has no basis to refuse one it has never heard of. But an open branch beside three closed ones is exactly where a drift guard earns its keep, so the schema and the predicate share ONE source string and `test_verdict_schema_is_packaged` asserts the equality; keeping the redundant-looking group is what makes adding a second namespace a single edit that fails loudly until it is made in both places. The layering split is forced by the module graph: `MutantOutcome` cannot see `judgment.r2.producer`, so it can only ask "is this spellable at all", while the document-level check owns "is this the right KIND for this producer" -- and that check is exact rather than weaker, refusing a native operator on an ingested document and an ingested one on a native document alike. |
| A-363 | **`cwd_declared` is a plain optional ROOT property and is deliberately NOT a member of `LANE_RESOLVED_FIELDS`, joining neither the tuple nor the schema's 10x9 `dependentRequired` matrix -- though it still requires a resolved lane.** | Every member of that group is all-present-or-all-absent because each is known the moment a lane loads and none has an honest "unset". `cwd` does have one: B043's contract says the verdict records it "absent when not declared -- absent, never `"."`". Adding it to either half of the group would make every verdict from every lane that declares no `cwd` -- nearly all of them -- schema-invalid. The one edge it does keep is checked in the model instead (`Verdict._check_cwd_declared`): a declared working directory is a fact ABOUT a lane, so a verdict that never loaded one cannot witness it. |
| A-364 | **`judgment.r1.coverage_producer` is a bare non-empty string in the packaged schema, not an enum -- the closed PER-FORMAT vocabulary lives in `assay.vocabulary` and the loader.** | It follows `coverage_format` beside it exactly, and for `coverage_format`'s own reason: the legal producer set is keyed BY FORMAT, the format vocabulary is registry-driven and deliberately open in this document, and closing producers here would make adding a producer to an existing format (B047 will add `go-test`/`covdata` for `go-cover`) a schema VERSION BUMP while adding the format itself is not. The loader is the only layer that sees both halves of the (format, producer) pair, so it is the only layer that can close it correctly -- and a producer that is spellable but refused (`REFUSED_COVERAGE_PRODUCERS`) is refused at lane LOAD and can never reach this field at all. |
| A-365 | **`survived_uncovered` and `lines_without_candidates` are arrays of a new `source_position` object (`path` + `lineno`), not of `"path:line"` strings; and they are REQUIRED-and-possibly-EMPTY under `ingested` rather than omitted-when-empty.** | Two fields because a path may legally contain a colon, and because `mutant_outcome` one `$def` over already models the same pair the same way -- one grammar, not two. Required-and-possibly-empty because absent and empty say different things here: absent means "this producer does not compute that at all" (a native judgment), while `[]` is the positive statement "the ingested path looked and found none". Collapsing them would delete exactly the distinction the fields exist for, and would make `verify.py`'s re-derivation partial instead of total. Sortedness is array ORDER, which draft 2020-12 cannot express, so it is owned by the model and, independently worded, by the raw verifier -- the same split `targets` already lives under. |
| A-366 | **`snapshot_policy.link_paths` is INDEPENDENT of `selection`, with no conditional pairing between them, and its own grammar is transcribed from `unsafe_symlink_omissions` rather than factored into a shared helper.** | Linking content IN is orthogonal to the unsafe-symlink omission policy: a lane may declare `link_paths` under either selection, and inventing a pairing would refuse honest lanes to buy nothing. The transcription is deliberate for the reason B035/A-329 already gives one field over: the two lists mean OPPOSITE things -- one names what the snapshot left out, the other what was linked in -- and a shared implementation would let one edit silently weaken both. The field is recorded at all because it is the one thing about a snapshot that is not bound to the recorded commit: everything else came out of the object store at `commit`, while every path listed here came out of whatever the invoking checkout held at run time, and a verdict must state that plainly rather than let a reader assume full commit binding. |
| A-367 | **The single re-rooting site for B043's `cwd` is `CommandPlan.cwd_declared` + `runner.resolve_run_cwd`, called from `execute_plan` alone -- NOT four joins at the four execution sites.** A plan built by hand rather than by `resolve_command_plan` therefore leaves it `None` and is not re-rooted. | B043's contract says the lane command, every R2 candidate re-execution and every R3 canary run must use the SAME resolved directory. Four joins is four places to get it right and four places for a later edit to get one wrong -- exactly the drift `MUTATION_BUCKETS` was named once to prevent (A-228's own root cause). Carrying the declaration on the PLAN instead means every executor that runs a lane's command already funnels through the one join, and the property becomes structural rather than tested-four-times. The `None` default is not a convenience: it is how `environment_command`'s probe gets the RIGHT behaviour by construction rather than by remembering to opt out -- that probe builds its own `CommandPlan` literal, and B010/DESIGN-GUIDE §4 says it runs in the INVOKING environment because it is not the lane command. |
| A-368 | **`cwd`'s "must resolve to a tracked DIRECTORY at the resolved commit" is checked TWICE, in two layers, with two different messages: the loader proves it is a directory in the INVOKING CHECKOUT, and `runner._execute_snapshot_unit` proves it is a directory in the MATERIALISED SNAPSHOT, naming the commit.** Neither check is redundant and neither can stand in for the other. | The contract asks for a commit-bound check "at load", and at load there is no resolved commit: base resolution happens per-run, well after `assay.toml` is read, so a loader that claimed to check tracked-ness at a commit would be checking it at whatever `HEAD` happened to be -- a different fact, wrongly named. Splitting it puts each half at the earliest moment its own fact EXISTS: a typo is caught while a human is editing the file, and a present-but-untracked directory (an ignored `build/`, the realistic case) is caught by the only layer that can name the commit in the refusal. The snapshot-side check runs once per lane rather than per unit, and is sound at that granularity for the same reason the tracked-artifact checks beside it already state: every later snapshot a lane materialises (each R2 mutant's, each R3 canary half's) is built from the SAME commit, so tracked-ness is invariant across them. |
| A-369 | **`cwd` re-roots the COMMAND and nothing else** -- `judge.coverage.artifact`, `judge.mutation.*` artifact paths, `source_roots`, `targets`, `canary.target` and every `infrastructure` fact stay project-root-relative, and the docs say so in the key's own section as a table. | A-271's one-path-grammar rule, applied to the one key that could plausibly break it. The artifact paths in particular CANNOT re-root even if that felt symmetric: `safeio.reserve_output` arms the reservation from the project root BEFORE the command runs, so a re-rooted artifact path would need the reservation discipline rebuilt around a directory the lane might not even have. More fundamentally, a path in a verdict must mean the same thing wherever it appears -- a consumer reading `judgment.r1.coverage_artifact` cannot be expected to first read `cwd_declared` and mentally join. The asymmetry is real (the command writes `.assay/cov.json`, the lane declares `app/.assay/cov.json`) and is stated in the docs rather than hidden. |
| A-370 | **`link_paths` are planted AFTER `SnapshotRepository._verify`, not before it, and inside `_build` -- the one function every snapshot this module ever yields goes through.** | `_verify` proves the materialised tree is EXACTLY the commit's content: clean status, index tree equal to HEAD's, the declared omission set and nothing else. A link planted before it would make that proof FALSE about a tree it is supposed to be true about -- and weakening `_verify` to tolerate the link would weaken it for every other lane too. Planting after keeps the committed-objects-only guarantee stated over exactly the committed objects, and the links are still in place before any consumer command runs, which is what B041(b) rule 3 actually requires. Placing it in `_build` rather than at each call site is what makes rule 3's "for every snapshot the lane creates (R3 canary snapshots included)" structural: `materialize` AND `materialize_replacement` -- an R2 mutant's own entry point, and the one a per-call-site implementation would most easily have missed -- both reach it. |
| A-371 | **A `link_paths` entry must be ignored by a COMMITTED `.gitignore`, and assay refuses at materialisation, by name, when it is not. This is a FIFTH refusal B041(b)'s own rule list does not have.** | Without it the feature self-refuses later with a MISNAMED terminal. `git.dirty_paths` deliberately consults committed ignore policy and nothing else (A-177/A-290 -- it unions `status` with `ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore` precisely so that `.git/info/exclude` cannot hide anything), so an un-ignored link surfaces as `DIRTY_TREE` after the lane's command: assay would be blaming the lane's command for something the lane's own isolation table declared. Writing the path into the snapshot's `.git/info/exclude` was considered and REJECTED -- it is defeated by the `ls-files` half by design, and hiding a declared link from the dirt check is the wrong shape of fix for a fact the verdict is supposed to state out loud. |
| A-372 | **A directory-only `.gitignore` pattern (`node_modules/`, with the trailing slash) does NOT cover a planted link, and the refusal message says so in as many words.** MEASURED, not reasoned about. | The realistic consumer rule carries the trailing slash -- it is what every JS project's `.gitignore` has. Git treats a trailing-slash pattern as directory-only and does not count a SYMLINK as a directory, so the consumer's existing, correct-looking rule leaves the link untracked. Measured directly with a real git: under `app/node_modules/` both `git status --porcelain` and `git ls-files --others --exclude-per-directory=.gitignore` report `app/node_modules`; under `app/node_modules` both report nothing. Not anticipated by B041(b)'s own contract text, which says only "the link is not a tracked path so the diff never sees it". Pinned as its own test (`test_a_trailing_slash_ignore_rule_does_not_cover_the_link_and_is_refused`) rather than left as a note, and given its own paragraph in CONSUMERS.md, because a consumer who hits it with a bare "snapshot is unclean" message has no way to guess the cause. |
| A-373 | **Rule 6's proof is an empirical canary on BOTH teardown paths plus the teardown helper in isolation -- never the sentence "stdlib `rmtree` unlinks symlinks rather than recursing".** | That sentence is true and it is not evidence. It is a claim about code assay does not own; it would silently stop being true if `_remove_owned_tree` were ever rewritten as a manual walker; and it says nothing at all about the SECOND teardown beside it (`_materialize`'s best-effort `rmtree(root, ignore_errors=True)` on the exception path) -- which is the one a consumer reaches when something has already gone wrong, i.e. exactly when destroying their `node_modules` would be least explicable. The tests plant a real symlink to a real directory outside the snapshot, put a canary file inside it, run the real teardown on the success path, on the exception path, and through `_remove_owned_tree` directly on a hand-built tree carrying a nested link, and assert the canary's BYTES afterwards. |
| A-374 | **`IsolationConfig.link_paths` is a DEFAULTED field, unlike its two siblings, and the loader reads `link_paths` BEFORE the `snapshot_selection` fork.** | The no-default rule on `unsafe_symlink_omissions` exists because "empty" has two meanings there -- `repository` mode's derived empty and an omission mode's refused empty -- and a default would collapse them. `link_paths` has exactly one meaning for empty (nothing was linked), so a default collapses nothing, and requiring every direct constructor across the suite to pass `link_paths=()` would be churn buying no distinction. | A-375 | **`projectRoot` is REQUIRED in an ingested mutation report, though the upstream `mutation-testing-report-schema` makes it optional** (only `schemaVersion`/`thresholds`/`files` are required there). `framework` is required for the same reason. | `projectRoot` is the only member that says where the report's own relative `files` keys are ANCHORED. B046's non-repudiation item (iii) asks assay to check that the report describes THIS snapshot rather than some other checkout the same tool ran in -- and without `projectRoot` that check would have to begin by assuming its own answer. Making it optional-with-a-fallback would mean inventing the anchor, which is A-018's "no invented values" at the artifact boundary. `framework` is required because `judgment.r2.producer_tool` is the whole answer to "whose word is this verdict resting on", and a verdict that records `ingested` without naming the producer states a dependency it cannot identify. The package imports only `assay.errors` and `assay.vocabulary`, both leaves, so the graph stays a strict DAG. The `parse` asymmetry is real rather than an oversight: a COVERAGE parser needs the declared producer because one format has several producers that measurably DISAGREE about what a field means (A-344/A-346); here the producer's identity is IN the document (`framework`) and is RECORDED rather than dispatched on -- assay reads the format identically whichever Stryker wrote it, and says on the wire which one that was. |
| A-377 | **An in-scope `Ignored` mutant REFUSES the lane** (`ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`), rather than being dropped or counted in `discarded`. | The v9 wire has no field that can state "the tool was told to skip this", and both alternatives are false statements rather than merely lossy ones. Dropping it silently is exactly how a gate is made green from inside the mutation tool's own config -- `mutator.excludedMutations` or a `// Stryker disable` comment would become a way to delete evidence, which is the silent-green class this project exists to remove. Folding it into `discarded` would report a deliberately suppressed mutant as one that failed to COMPILE, which that field's own shipped schema description forbids in as many words. Refusal is the third option and the only honest one: it names the mutant, says why, and gives two fixes (remove the suppression, or move the line out of scope). B046's own status map says "`Ignored` -> `excluded` (counted, never laundered as a kill)"; a wire that cannot count it leaves refusal as the only spelling of "never laundered". Out-of-scope `Ignored` mutants are irrelevant and are filtered by scope first, exactly like every other status. |

The approximation errs toward OVER-reporting, which is the safe direction: an entry asserts "the tool declined to mutate this line", and that is a TRUE statement about the tool for an import line or a closing brace just as much as for an untested expression. Under-reporting is the dangerous direction -- it would hide the case the field exists for, a changed line the mutation tool never touched at all. Lines are read from the report's OWN `source` text rather than from the snapshot, so a line number here means exactly what the tool's own line numbers mean. |
| A-379 | **`judge_mutation` takes NO producer fork: an ingested R2 claim is judged by the same function, the same precedence and the same terminals a native one is.** A `fail_under` parameter was written and then REMOVED. | It would have broken the one property `JudgmentR2`'s own docstring promises -- that "an independent consumer can already re-derive the R2 claim's *status* from `Mutation`'s own bucket fields alone", with no external policy input. A floor below 100 makes the status depend on a number that is nowhere in the document, so a PASS beside recorded survivors becomes unexplainable from the artifact, and `verify._check_r2_rederivation` (which reuses `judge_mutation`) would correctly call such a document inconsistent. | A-380 | **`judge.mutation.fail_under` is REQUIRED on an ingested lane and must be exactly `100.0` in this build; any other value is refused with a message naming the missing wire field and B050.** | Three options and the other two are worse. DROPPING the key leaves B046's own acceptance box unmet and makes the documented worked lane unloadable. ACCEPTING it and ignoring it is inert config that cannot fail loudly when it is wrong -- AGENTS.md 4.2a's own test, failed. So: declarable, honoured at exactly the one value v9 can express, and refused loudly with the reason everywhere else. The refusal is not a placeholder for laziness; `100.0` is a real and common policy (any surviving mutant fails the lane) and is what the worked lane declares. The gap is filed as B050 rather than left as a comment, because the fix is a schema field and therefore a later cut's work. |
| A-381 | **`survived_uncovered` is deduplicated to distinct POSITIONS, not one entry per `NoCoverage` mutant.** | Forced by the model, which refuses duplicate positions -- and the model is right. The field is documented as "listed BY POSITION so a consumer sees the untested line": one line carrying nine NoCoverage mutants is still ONE untested line, and repeating it nine times would turn a list of places into a disguised count of mutants, which is precisely what the field exists instead of. The real committed report has this shape (69 NoCoverage mutants over 31 distinct positions; ten of them share `src/format.ts` line 34), so this was not a hypothetical -- the first run over the real artifact is what surfaced it. |
| A-382 | **The ingested report is read through `safeio.reserve_output`, armed before the command and consumed after, exactly like the coverage artifact -- and a report TRACKED by git refuses the lane.** | Without the reservation, a report committed into the repository or left behind by an earlier run would satisfy the declared path and be judged as evidence of a run that never happened. The tracked-by-git refusal is the same check the coverage artifact and `equivalence_artifact` already get, for the same reason one field over. | A-384 | **`cwd`'s commit-bound check is a MANIFEST lookup (`isolation.Snapshot.tracked_directories`), never a filesystem test; a `cwd` that is or lies beneath a declared `link_paths` entry is additionally refused AT LOAD; and a symlink `cwd` is refused explicitly.** Fixes a real snapshot escape found in fix-round review. | The shipped check was `run_cwd.is_dir()`. `_plant_link_paths` plants real symlinks into the snapshot after `_verify` (A-370), so for a lane declaring both `cwd = "deps"` and `link_paths = ["deps"]` over an untracked, gitignored `deps/`, that test FOLLOWED the planted link into the invoking checkout and answered `True` -- and the lane's command then executed in the consumer's real working tree, writing to it for real (reproduced twice: a `pwd` witness showing the checkout path, and a file that persisted after teardown). A filesystem test cannot answer a question about a COMMIT: it reports what stands at a path, and a snapshot deliberately contains one thing that is not committed content. The manifest can, and it is not a new mechanism -- it is the SAME oracle `_plant_link_paths`' own tracked-ness rule (rule 2) already consults, now shared with the one question that was asking it a second, weaker way. The load-time pair refusal is exact rather than merely defensive: a linked path must be UNTRACKED (rule 2 refuses linking a tracked path) and a `cwd` must be TRACKED, and a tracked directory's ancestors are tracked -- so no commit can satisfy both, and TRACKED+LINKED is not a legitimate combination being given up. | A-386 | **`SUPPORTED_REPORT_SCHEMA_MAJORS` is `{"1"}`: every admitted `mutation-testing-report-schema` major must have a committed real artifact behind it.** Major 2 is refused as UNPROVEN, not as proven-defective. | It shipped as `{"1", "2"}` under a docstring saying it is "pinned to the major the committed real fixture carries" -- and the fixture carries `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, so major 2 was admitted on the strength of nothing at all. Draft 2020-12 cannot express array order at all, so for these three fields the raw verifier is not a redundant second opinion -- it is one of only two witnesses in the entire system, and the schema layer is not among them. Deleting it would have left three v9 fields resting on the model alone, which is exactly the single-witness state the three-place-registration discipline exists to prevent, and would have done it by editing the sentence that was telling the truth about what the design intended. Each check is worded DIFFERENTLY from the model's ("strictly ascending ... is not before" vs "must be ascending ..., got"), the convention this module has kept since P16, so a test can tell which layer fired; each test asserts that wording rather than merely that the document was refused, because the model refuses it too and "it went red" would prove nothing. `link_paths`' check sits BEFORE `_check_snapshot_policy`'s `selection` fork for A-366's own reason: after the fork it would have been dead code under `selection = "repository"`, the selection most lanes declare. The schema layer closes the enum end to end, so this was never exploitable; it was a layer-independence violation, and independence is the entire product of having a raw layer at all. Enforced the way `_SNAPSHOT_SELECTIONS` already is one check over (name the value, name the legal set). Only `go_cover` populates it. | A-084 assigns the extension to the package proving the need, and this is the shape that need has. The columns are the load-bearing part and were the field's whole reason for existing: `cmd/cover` ends a block at the START position of its own last statement, so `shapes.go` really contains `28.22,29.2` immediately followed by `29.2,31.3`, and a line-only key fuses two different records into one. Keeping `count` per block rather than pre-classifying is the same argument one level down — A-239 rejected correcting after the merge as information-theoretically insufficient precisely because `go_cover`'s executed-wins overlap merge discards, DURING parsing, the per-block data any correction would need. A sibling type rather than six loose fields because `FileCoverage` already validates its own invariants in one place (P15/A-067 finding 4) and a bare tuple-of-ints would put position arithmetic back into every consumer. The alternative considered and rejected: a `CoverageProfile`-level block table keyed by path, which would have duplicated the path keying `files` already owns and left `FileCoverage` unable to validate its own blocks. |
| A-391 | **The intersection joins WHOLE EXTENTS, never positional containment: `attribute_statements` matches a parsed `CoverageBlock` to an oracle `StatementBlock` on the exact `(start_line, start_col, end_line, end_col)` tuple, checks `num_stmts` agreement, and takes that block's own `stmt_lines`.** A disagreement in either direction — an extent only in the profile, an extent only in the source, or a matched pair whose statement counts differ — is refused `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` naming the extents, never attributed anyway. | The obvious design ("a statement at position P belongs to the block whose extent contains P") is not merely messier, it is WRONG, and a frozen witness proves it: because a block ends at the start position of its last statement, `shapes.go`'s `28.22,29.2` and `29.2,31.3` share position `29.2`, and the statement beginning there belongs to the first block only. A closed interval matches both; a half-open interval matches neither. The structure is not recoverable from positions, so the oracle reports each block's own statement list and the join is on the block. The refusal is the second half of the same argument and is what makes the whole mechanism checkable rather than merely plausible: A-217 records that the instrumenter is DETERMINISTIC, so re-running it over the same source must reproduce the same extents — which means every disagreement is real evidence that the profile and the source are different revisions (a stale artifact, an edit between run and judgment, a different toolchain). There is no safe direction to guess in, because attributing anyway publishes a verdict about lines that are not the lines that ran. AGENTS.md's DERIVE / READ / **FAIL** ordering, at its third branch. It also turned the go1.25.12-vs-go1.25.14 toolchain delta between the frozen witnesses and this wave's probe from an assumption into a MEASUREMENT: all eight profiles joined exactly, which they could not have done had the instrumenter changed. |
| A-392 | **`CoverageProfile.statement_attributed: bool = False`, whose ONLY producer is `attribute_statements` — no parser sets it — paired with a new `LanguageAdapter.requires_statement_attribution` and a refusal in `evaluate_coverage`/`evaluate_targets` when an adapter requires attribution and the profile it was handed reports `False`.** | Without it the correction is a step somebody has to remember, and "we will remember" is exactly the check that cannot fail. The failure it guards is AGENTS.md anti-pattern 3, the **masked default**, in its purest form: an un-corrected block-based profile is a wrong answer that looks exactly like a right one, is rendered harmless by every context that happens to run the correction, and is therefore INVISIBLE to testing — it surfaces only on the one path that skips the step. The two rejected alternatives were both worse in the same way. Having `go_cover` emit EMPTY line sets until corrected fails loudly but with the wrong sentence, reporting `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE` over a complete artifact — AGENTS.md's "absence for emptiness", a false certification rather than a missing one. Filed as B055. | `BLOCKED-grammar.md` §3 already says this is "line granularity's own limit — `coverage.py` shares it", and it is right: line 4 of `lit.go` genuinely carries two counted statements (the assignment, count 1; the func-literal body it contains, count 0), both of which genuinely begin on line 4. What is genuinely worth proving is the part that is not a theorem: that the shipped CLI produces a real statement-granular Go R1 verdict on a real tree, and that every difference from covergate is accounted for by a NAMED mechanism rather than an unexplained delta. The classification is load-bearing and is why the criterion says it: extent-expansion (assay right, the theorem) and file-absence (covergate's `Evaluate` `fc == nil` branch splitting a changed file into `NoCode` vs `Unmeasured` by `HasExecutableCode` — the mechanism behind the P14 "silently skipped a package" caveat) are different questions with different correct answers, and a single parity number averages them into a conclusion about neither. Ruled by the controller at `vbpub@3a95459e`; the name, signature and rejected alternatives are the implementer's under A-084, as A-239 → A-397. |
| A-405 | **A Go coverage record's COLUMN may be `0`, and a file that carries one is `//line`-remapped: the parser ACCEPTS it, `attribute_statements` skips the file, and `evaluate` IGNORES it outside the judged set / REFUSES `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` inside it.** Lines stay `>= 1`; columns become `>= 0` at all three sites that asserted otherwise (`go_cover._parse_pos`, `CoverageBlock.__post_init__`, `StatementBlock.__post_init__`), each now citing `go/token`'s `//line`-without-column behaviour and `cmd/cover`'s own comment. The flag is `FileCoverage.line_directive_remapped`, DERIVED from `blocks` (any record with a zero column flags the whole file), internal representation only — no wire field, no schema change, no new reason code. Controller ruling DA-R2, `vbpub@5b6f77cc`. | The invariant was a **guessed fact about `cmd/cover` that `cmd/cover` disproves** — the exact class of defect this whole re-carve exists to remove (A-334: measure the external system; A-217: adapt, do not invent). `go/token` gives a position remapped by a `//line file:line` directive with no column `Column == 0` by design, and that is precisely the case `cover.go:1055-1060`'s own comment names ("positions can repeat when there is a line directive that does not specify column information", issues #27530/#30746) and the reason `dedup` exists at all. The round-1 reviewer built Go's OWN canonical corpus for it (`cover_test.go`'s `lineDupContents`, the `TestLineDup` fixture) and the branch refused the entire artifact with "column number 0 in '100.0' is not positive" — one generated file poisoning a whole lane, for bytes that are exactly what `go test -coverprofile` wrote. **Why the flag is per FILE and conservative:** within a file carrying a `//line` directive there is no way to tell a physical position from a virtual one — the directive applies from where it appears onward and the profile records only the result — so `linedup.out` legitimately mixes `6.21,7.25` with `100.0,102.1` and trusting the physical-looking half would be a guess about where the directive's scope began. **Why DERIVED and not stored:** a stored flag is a second fact about the same four numbers that can disagree with them, and every layer that rebuilds a `FileCoverage` would have to remember to carry it — the "check that cannot fail" shape `statement_attributed`'s own docstring argues against; `blocks` is already carried through every rebuild, so the property is carried by construction. **Why the three-way disposition and not the two simpler ones.** Shape (i), simply relaxing the bound and judging the file, was REJECTED: `git diff` names physical lines, the records name the directive's virtual ones, so a 24-line file measuring lines 100-105 intersects nothing and reports 0 executable of 0 changed — a clean percentage over nothing measured, which is DESIGN-GUIDE §5's laundering gate and the north star's "0/0 is never 100%". Shape (iii), refusing the whole lane with a correct message, was REJECTED because it would leave every Go project carrying one goyacc/peg/ragel-generated file unable to use R1 at all, on the eve of the first Go consumer, when the per-file seam is a bounded change (a derived flag, a join skip, one evaluate check). What is left is the north star's own rule applied literally: pre-existing code outside the diff is invisible to the verdict BY CONSTRUCTION, because it is not what is under review — so a remapped file the lane does not judge costs nothing, and one it does judge is refused rather than silently measured. **Why `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` for the refusal**, from the closed vocabulary with no new code: nothing is wrong with the artifact, and `UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` would repeat the very misattribution BLOCKER 1 (b) named — telling a consumer their profile is malformed when it is byte-for-byte what the toolchain emitted. The fault is that the LANE asked assay to judge a generated file it structurally cannot judge, and the remedy is in the lane file (generated sources belong outside `judge.source_roots`/`judge.targets`), which is exactly the artifact-versus-lane distinction `go_modfile._refuse`'s own docstring exists to keep. `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`TARGET_NOT_MEASURED` was the near-miss alternative in whole-target mode and was rejected for naming the wrong cause and implying the wrong remedy ("write tests"). **The witness is committed**, with its provenance and its re-runnable in-image recipe (`carve-assets/P27-recarve/{probe-linedup.sh,linedup.out,linedup-oracle.json}`), and it discharges a second open item on the way: REPORT §5 item 4's `dedup` replication, unproven for want of a fixture, is proven by it — nine extents and nine `num_stmts` reproduced exactly. Main's B054 (an istanbul record for a never-executed file outside the judged set) is the SAME principle and is explicitly NOT fixed here; the controller's ruling assigns it to the post-Wave-C patch wave. |
| A-406 | **DA-R1 implemented: there is no vacuous statement attribution. (1) A lane whose language is judged from block extents and whose `judge.coverage.format` carries none is refused at CONFIG LOAD, `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`, naming the language, the format and `go-cover` with its producers. (2) `runner._attribute_statements_for_lane`'s "no block-bearing files, so mark it attributed" branch is DELETED and replaced by a refusal.** The seam is one new fact in `assay.vocabulary`, `STATEMENT_ATTRIBUTABLE_FORMATS_BY_LANGUAGE = {"go": {"go-cover"}}`. Controller ruling DA-R1, `vbpub@5b6f77cc`. | The hole was REACHABLE, which is what makes this a defect rather than a tidiness question. `judge.language` and `judge.coverage.format` are independent by design, so a Go lane may declare `format = "lcov"`; lcov converted from a Go coverprofile (the `gcov2lcov` family) carries the naive block expansion — signatures, closing braces and `case` labels as executable lines — which is exactly the over-approximation A-392 exists to refuse. Such a profile arrived with `blocks = None` everywhere, the runner marked it `statement_attributed=True` with **no oracle and no `helpers` entry**, and A-392's guard — whose entire purpose is to make skipping the oracle impossible — passed it. The old branch's stated justification, "such a profile is already statement truth, vacuously", is RETRACTED at the site: it is true of a line-based format judged by a line-based adapter, and that case never enters this function at all, because `evaluate_r1` gates the call on `adapter.requires_statement_attribution`. So the branch was never protecting the case it named, and was swallowing the case it did see. **Why the fact lives in `vocabulary` and not on the adapter**, which is the half worth arguing: the check has to run at config load to be worth having (the fault is a property of the FILE, knowable before any artifact exists), and `assay.config` must not import `assay.adapters` — "there is no `adapters/python.py` import anywhere in this module" is `registry.py`'s own mechanical guarantee behind O2, and dragging the registry into the lane-file loader to answer one question would trade a structural guarantee for a convenience. The REJECTED alternative was an adapter attribute (`statement_attributable_formats`) consulted at load: it forces exactly that import, or else moves the check out of load time to somewhere an adapter is already resolved, which is where DA-R1 explicitly does not want it. `vocabulary` is where language-scoped facts already live (`MUTATION_OPERATORS_BY_LANGUAGE` is the same shape one field over) and `config` already imports it. The cost is honestly stated: this mapping and `LanguageAdapter.requires_statement_attribution` are two statements of one fact and can drift, so `test_config_statement_attribution_format.py` derives the requiring languages from `cli._built_in_registry` and asserts set equality, with a vacuity guard that fails if no registered adapter requires attribution — the derivation shape A-400 had to repair when it was written the other way round. **Two independent guards, deliberately.** Load-time refusal makes the run-time one unreachable through the CLI; it is kept anyway because a library caller can hand-build a `Lane`, and because the two name different faults (a file a human can fix vs a contradiction no honest producer emits). **The empty profile is not this case and needed proving, not asserting:** it terminates one step earlier at `coverage.check_empty_coverage`, `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE`, payload-free — now pinned by a test that also asserts no `helpers` entry and not a PASS, because "the branch was protecting the empty profile" was the plausible-sounding defence and it is false. |
| A-407 | **A helper whose payload the judge took away is dropped where the payload went, so the wiring guard never becomes the consumer's message.** In `runner._run_prepared_lane`, immediately after `claims += (r1_claim,)`, a payload-free R1 claim (`r1_claim.coverage is None`) drops every recorded helper whose role is not in `supported_helper_roles((r1_claim,))`. `assemble_verdict`'s B047-item-5 guard is left exactly as it is and stays a wiring assertion; no reason code, no wire field, no `diagnostics` route. Controller ruling on round-2 BLOCKER R2-1, `vbpub@12e028c7`. | **The defect was consumer-visible and swallowed two different refusals, including a ruled one.** `_attribute_statements_for_lane` records the `statement-positions` helper the instant `adapter.statement_blocks` returns (`runner.py:997`), which is BEFORE `attribute_statements` joins anything and before `evaluate_*` judges anything. Every judge refusal downstream of the oracle — a stale profile refused by the join (`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT`), A-405's own refusal for a `//line`-remapped file with judged lines (`BAD_LANE_CONFIG`) — is caught by `evaluate_r1` and rendered as a payload-free R1 claim, exactly as designed. The helper entry then had no claim to support it, `assemble_verdict` refused, and `run_lane` raised: the operator was told `lane 'unit' recorded helper role(s) ['statement-positions'] …` — a sentence about assay's own `helpers[]` array — and **no verdict document was written at all**, because the CLI's `write_verdict` call is downstream of the return. The round-2 reviewer measured all five shapes in-image against the shipped zipapp: the DA-R2 ignore path (A) works, D is correct only because its oracle never runs, and B, C and E were masked with no artifact. It is NOT a regression from the A-405 round — the reviewer disproved that itself against `875382d2`, where scenario E reproduces identically — but A-405 gave it a second, ruled path, which is what made a pre-existing hole a blocker. **Why the drop belongs at the claim site and not in the guard.** `assemble_verdict`'s comment already named the correct shape before this fix existed: the one legitimate way to hold a helper whose claim has been voided is to drop the entry *at the site that took the payload away*, which is what `_replace_highest_higher_rigor_claim_with_git_failed` does for the cleanup-only failure. There are now two such sites and they are the same move; filtering inside `assemble_verdict` instead would cover both AND swallow a genuine wiring defect — a producer that recorded a role no claim could ever support — which is the one thing that guard exists to catch, so the guard would be deleted in effect while appearing to remain. **Why `supported_helper_roles` and not a local test for `role == "statement-positions"`:** it is the SINGLE definition of the correspondence rule, read by `Verdict._check_helpers` as well, and a second copy of the table in the producer is exactly the disagreement A-385/A-367 argue against — its failure shape is a verdict this module built and the schema then refuses with a bare `ValueError` no caller catches. **Why the R1 claim and not the whole claim set:** at this point in `_run_prepared_lane` R2/R3 have not been assembled, so `claims` is not yet the set `assemble_verdict` will validate; the question being answered is only "did the payload THIS helper produced survive", and that payload is R1's. **What is deliberately NOT done here:** the refusal's own TEXT still reaches no `assay run` consumer — the operator sees `unit: ERROR/UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT (exit 2)` and the verdict's `reason_code`. That is main's B053, ruled DA-R3 as docs-now (SF-R2-3) with the `diagnostics` stream (`runner.py:365`, the seam `environment_command` already uses) recorded as the candidate mechanism for the post-Wave-C consumer-diagnostics patch wave. A-407 makes the refusal REACH a verdict; it does not widen what a verdict can say. **Proof:** two toolchain-free regression tests in `test_runner_helpers_envelope.py` (so the registered gate runs them), both red at the pre-fix commit with the masked message verbatim, and the reviewer's scenarios B and E through the shipped zipapp in-image in `tests/qualification/test_go_r1_real.py`, likewise red without the fix. |
| A-408 | **B049 ruled: option (4). A reservation whose HELD parent directory has been unlinked is refused by name, at `consume()` time, on `os.fstat(parent_fd).st_nlink == 0`.** The refusal is `ERROR`/`UNREADABLE_ARTIFACT` (a value the frozen schema already reserves for "an object that exists but cannot be trusted … or a race the caller's own reservation already detected") with a message naming the replaced directory, the cause, and the remedy (turn the tool's clean/rm-first option off, e.g. Vitest's `coverage.clean = false`, or write into the existing directory). The check lives in `assay.safeio.OutputReservation._refuse_if_parent_was_replaced`, called from `consume()` — so it applies at ALL the sites B049 names, by construction rather than by repetition: the coverage read (`runner.py:2241`), the SQL R2 `equivalence_artifact` (`runner.py:2256`), the ingested mutation report (`runner.py:2286`), and `mutation.py`'s per-mutant equivalence (`:1642`) and kill-signal (`:1192`) reads, whose absent-read fold classifies a perfectly-executed mutant `crashed`. No new reason code, no schema change (A-138/A-170). CONSUMERS/README downgrade `clean: false` from REQUIRED to RECOMMENDED and state what a consumer who forgets it now sees. Ruling source: `WAVE-PROMPT-2026-09-02-wave-d-v10-integrity.md` DA-D1. | The shipped message asserted a checkable falsehood — `NO_MEASUREMENT`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE`, "the lane's command exited without writing the declared artifact at all", over a complete 678-byte artifact that was on disk at the declared path the whole time (B049's own A/B measurement, real Vitest, nothing mocked). That is AGENTS.md's "a check is only as strong as what it actually compares" one layer down: the absence is real, the conclusion drawn from it is not. `st_nlink == 0` on the ALREADY-HELD descriptor is the exact, name-free witness for a deleted-and-recreated directory — an unlinked directory inode keeps a zero link count while an open descriptor holds it, even though a NEW inode answers to the same path — and costs one `fstat` on a descriptor this object already owns. **Rejected, from B049's own list:** (1) re-open the parent chain by NAME at consume time — loses exactly the TOCTOU cover P20's held descriptor exists to provide, and would have to re-derive it some other way; (2) as originally worded, an inode/device comparison — strictly more work than (4) for the same signal; (3) document only — today's mitigation, silent about whether other external tools share Vitest's convention, which they do (any `rm -rf out && mkdir out` build step). The legitimate states are constructed and asserted before the refusal ships (AGENTS.md's own corollary): an artifact written INTO the reserved directory still reads, and a genuinely absent artifact in an intact directory is still the truthful `None`/`EMPTY_COVERAGE`, so this is not a superset refusal. |
| A-409 | **B053 (a)+(b) ruled: ONE emitter, `assay.runner.announce_refusal(exc, *, diagnostics)`, printing exactly `assay: {outcome}/{reason_code}: {message}`, called at every site where an `AssayError` becomes a refusal `Claim` or `Verdict` — not one `try` at the CLI boundary.** The stream is the `diagnostics: TextIO | None` `environment_command`'s probe refusal (A-322) and B033's whole-target note already use, threaded `cli.py` (`diagnostics=err`) → `run_lane` → `_run_higher_rigor_lane` → `_run_prepared_lane`, so half (a) (the CLI's stderr line) and half (b) (a library caller's own stream) are one mechanism, not two. `cli.py`'s two pre-existing prints of that same text are refactored onto the emitter. `evaluate_r1` gains `diagnostics`, defaulting to `None`, so `assay.canary`'s two internal calls stay silent (a variant's R1 refusal is a step inside the canary mechanism, not the lane's refusal). Announced at the point of CONVERSION, never at an intermediate catch: a coverage-read failure on a lane that declares no R1 never becomes a claim, and one error that refuses two levels (an R2 orchestration fault also refusing R3) is announced at the first only. No wire change — DA-D2 (c)'s per-claim `detail` is phase 2. Ruling source: `WAVE-PROMPT-2026-09-02-wave-d-v10-integrity.md` DA-D2 (a)+(b), as read by the controller in DA-R1. | The document is a closed vocabulary by design and stays one (A-138/A-170), so the sentence naming WHICH artifact, WHICH declaration or WHICH target refused has nowhere on the wire to live — and it was being discarded entirely, which is the defect B053 filed: `ERROR`/`BAD_LANE_CONFIG` and a guess. **Rejected: a single `except AssayError` at the `run` command's boundary** — the shape DA-D2 (a) names as "the narrower fix". Measured, it cannot see the defect: `grep -n 'except AssayError' src/assay/runner.py` finds 15 handlers that convert the error into a refusal `Claim` or `Verdict` and RETURN a document; nothing propagates. A boundary handler prints for the handful of structural errors that already reached `cli.py` (and already printed) and stays silent for exactly the internal conversions B053 is about. **Rejected: a second, per-site message format** — B033's whole-target note was already a second spelling of the same line, and is now the emitter's line plus an indented context line, because two spellings of one refusal is how the two drift apart. **Not done, and named as a limit rather than papered over:** refusals no `AssayError` carries (`DIRTY_TREE`, `HEAD_CHANGED`, `MISSING_EXTERNAL_TOOL`, the `env_required` and `--shard` refusals at `runner.py:3719/3721/3960/4103/4149/4221`) print no line — there is no message to copy, and minting one at the point of refusal rather than the point of knowledge is what §5's defaults doctrine forbids. Recorded as a decision ask in the Wave D REPORT. The one-line-per-refusal property is tested by COUNT, not by presence, over the whole `errors.REASON_CODES` enumeration plus four end-to-end refusals reached through the installed CLI. |
